“I could not wait, *ma donna*! I had planned it all﻿—to ride with you Rotterdam tonight﻿—and then tomorrow on the seas﻿—with you﻿—and England in sight, I could not wait!” he reiterated, almost pathetically, so great was his impatience.

“I am ready to start when you will, my lord,” she said again, with a smile.

“And you’ll not be afraid?” he insisted. “It will be dark﻿—and cold. We could not reach Rotterdam before midnight.”

“How should I be afraid of the darkness or of anything,” she retorted, “when I am with you. And how should I be cold, when I am nestling in your arms?”

===Chinese===
「那待会儿动身，你可会害怕？」他催问道，声音激动得发颤。「天黑漆漆的——路我熟得很。午夜前我们就能赶到鹿特丹——明天就按我预先安排好的，扬帆去英格兰——」

「全凭亲爱的夫君做主，」她爽快地应道。

「我等不及了，我的夫人（ma donna）！」他一遍遍地说，急不可耐得近乎可怜。「我什么都盘算好了——今夜带你同去鹿特丹——明天一早就到了海上——同你在一起——英格兰遥遥在望——我等不及了！」

「夫君几时动身，我几时就准备好，」她又微笑着说了一遍。

「你真的不怕？」他还是不放心。「天会黑——还会冷。午夜之前我们赶不到鹿特丹。」

「有你在身边，我还怕什么黑、怕什么别的呢？」她反驳道。「偎在你怀里，我又怎么会冷？」

===Original===
He had his arms round her in an instant. He would have kissed her if he dared. But with the kiss all restraint would of a surety have vanished, as doth the snow in the warm embrace of the sun. He would have seized her then and there once more and carried her away. And this time no consideration on earth would have stayed him. With a muttered exclamation, he jumped to his feet and passed his slender hand across his forehead.

“Good St. Bavon!” he murmured whimsically. “Why are you so unkind to me tonight?”

And she, a little disappointed because, in truth, she had been ready for the kiss, rejoined with a quaint little pout:

“You are always appealing to St. Bavon, my dear lord! Why is that?”

“Because,” he replied very seriously, “St. Bavon is the patron saint of all men that are weak.”

She fixed great, wondering eyes on him. The reply was ambiguous; she did not quite understand the drift of it.

===Chinese===
转眼间他已把她搂进怀里。他真想吻她，若是敢的话。可这一吻下去，所有的克制必将荡然无存，就像雪在太阳温暖的怀抱里消融一样。那样，他定会当场再一次把她夺过来、带走，这一次，天下再没有什么能拦得住他。他低低喊了一声，跳起身来，细长的手抹过额头。

「好心的圣巴沃（St. Bavon）啊！」他半是认真半是玩笑地咕哝道。「您今夜为什么待我这样狠心？」

她呢，其实早已等着那一吻，不免有几分失望，娇嗔地噘起小嘴回了一句：

「夫君老是求告圣巴沃！这是为什么呀？」

「因为，」他一本正经地答道，「圣巴沃是天下一切软弱男子的主保圣人。」

她睁大一双惊奇的眼睛望着他。这回答模棱两可，她不很明白其中的深意。

===Original===
“But you, my lord, are so strong,” she objected.

It was perhaps too dark for her to see the expression in his face; but even so she felt herself unaccountably blushing under that gaze which she could not clearly see. Whereupon he uttered an ejaculation which sounded almost as if he were angered, and abruptly, without any warning, he turned on his heel and went out of the room, leaving Gilda alone once more beside the virginal.

But she no longer felt the desire to sing. The happiness which filled her entire soul was too complete even for song.

===Chinese===
「可是夫君，您这样强壮有力，」她不同意道。

屋里或许太暗，她看不清他脸上的神情；可即便这样，在那道她看不分明的注视底下，她还是没来由地红了脸。他随即迸出一声近乎恼怒的短喝，毫无征兆地猛一转身，出了房间，把吉尔达又一次独自留在了维金纳琴旁。

可她再没有心思唱歌了。充盈她整个灵魂的那份幸福太圆满了，圆满得连歌都容纳不下。

### IV / 四

===Original===
One of the equerries had awhile ago found his way to the guest-chamber where the sick man was lying, and had informed Diogenes that the Stadtholder was now ready to start on his way, but desired his presence that he might take his leave. Then it was that Diogenes sent an urgent message to his Highness, entreating him to remain but a little while longer. The sick man was better, would soon wake out of a refreshing sleep. Diogenes would then question him. Poor old Pythagoras had something to say, something that the Stadtholder himself must hear. Of this Diogenes was absolutely convinced.

“I know it,” the young soldier asserted earnestly. “I seem to feel it in my bones.”

Whereupon the Stadtholder had decided to wait, and Diogenes, after his brief glimpse of Gilda, felt easier in his mind, less impatient. Already he chided himself for his gloomy forebodings. Since his beloved was ready to entrust herself to him, the journey to England would only be put off by a few hours. What need to repine? Joy would be none the less sweet for this brief delay.

A quarter of an hour later Pythagoras was awake, the physician out of the room, and Diogenes was sitting on the edge of the bed holding his faithful comrade’s hand, and trying to disentangle some measure of coherence out of the other’s tangled narrative, whilst Socrates stood by making an occasional comment or just giving an expressive grunt from time to time. It took both time and patience, neither of which commodities did Diogenes possess in superabundance; but after the first few moments of listening to the rambling of the sick man, he became very still and attentive. The busy house, the noisy guests, the waiting Stadtholder down below, all slipped out from his ken. Holding his comrade’s hand, he was with him on the snow-clad Veluwe, and had found his way with him into the lonely mill.

===Chinese===
早些时候，一名侍从武官寻到病人躺着的客房，告诉第欧根尼：执政已准备启程，但想见他一面，好当面道别。于是第欧根尼给殿下捎去一封急信，恳请他再多留片刻。病人好些了，很快就会从一场酣睡中醒来，届时第欧根尼要问他几句话。可怜的老毕达哥拉斯有话要说，有句执政本人非听不可的话。这一点，第欧根尼深信不疑。

「我知道他有话，」年轻的军人恳切地说，「我简直是从骨头缝里觉出来的。」

执政于是决定等一等。第欧根尼同吉尔达匆匆见了一面之后，心里松快了些，也不那么焦躁了。他已在暗暗责怪自己不该那样阴郁地预感不祥。既然心爱的人已愿意把终身托付给他，英格兰之行不过迟上几个钟头罢了，有什么可抱怨的？短短一耽搁，快乐丝毫也不会减色。

一刻钟后，毕达哥拉斯醒了，医生也出了房间。第欧根尼坐在床沿上，握着他这位忠实伙伴的手，努力想从他那颠三倒四的叙述里理出些头绪；苏格拉底立在一旁，时不时插一句嘴，或者意味深长地哼上一声。这既要工夫又要耐心，而这两样东西第欧根尼都不富余；可听病人絮叨了开头几句之后，他就静了下来，全神贯注。忙碌的宅子、喧闹的宾客、楼下等候的执政，全都从他心里退了出去。他握着伙伴的手，跟他一起置身在积雪的费吕韦荒原上，跟着他摸进了那座孤零零的磨坊。

===Original===
“It was the Lord of Stoutenburg,” Pythagoras averred, with as much strength as he could command. “I’d stake my life on’t! I knew him at once. How could I ever forget his ugly countenance, after all he made you suffer?”

“Well﻿—and?” queried Diogenes eagerly.

“I knew the other man too, but could not be sure of his name. He was one of those who was with Stoutenburg that day at Ryswick, when you so cleverly put a spoke in their abominable wheel. I knew them both, I tell you!” the sick man insisted feverishly; “but I had the good sense not to betray what I knew.”

“But Stoutenburg did not know you?” Diogenes insisted.

“Yes, he did,” the other replied, sagely nodding his head. “That is why he ordered his menial to put a bullet into my back. The two noble gentlemen questioned me first,” he went on more coherently; “then they plied me with wine. They wanted to make me drunk so as to murder me at their leisure.”

“They little know they, eh, thou bottomless barrel?” Diogenes broke in with a laugh. “The cask hath not been fashioned yet that would contain enough liquor even to quench thy thirst, what?”

===Chinese===
「是斯托滕贝格勋爵（the Lord of Stoutenburg），」毕达哥拉斯使出全身的力气断言道。「我拿性命打赌！我一眼就认出了他。他害你吃了那么多苦头，他那张丑脸我怎么能忘？」

「嗯——后来呢？」第欧根尼急切地追问。

「另外那个人我也认得，只是拿不准他的名字。那天在赖斯韦克（Ryswick）跟斯托滕贝格在一起的就有他——就是你妙计戳破他们那桩罪恶勾当的那一回。他们两个我都认得，我跟你说！」病人发着热坚持道。「不过我乖觉得很，没有露出认得他们的口风。」

「可斯托滕贝格没认出你来？」第欧根尼追问。

「认出来了，」对方答道，煞有介事地点着头。「所以他才吩咐他的下仆朝我背上开枪。两位高贵的老爷先盘问了我一番，」他往下说得渐渐连贯起来，「接着就灌我酒。他们想把我灌醉，好从容不迫地下手宰了我。」

「他们可真是打错了算盘，嘿，你这口无底洞的大酒桶？」第欧根尼朗声笑着插嘴道。「天底下还没造出一只桶，装的酒够你解一回渴呢，你说是不是？」

===Original===
“They plied me with wine,” Pythagoras reiterated gravely; “and then I pretended to get very drunk. For I soon remarked that the more drunk they thought I was, the more freely they talked.”

“Well, and what did they say?”

“They talked of De Berg crossing the Ijssel with ten thousand men between Doesburg and Bronchorst; and of Isembourg coming up from Kleve at the same time. I make no doubt that the design is to seize Arnheim and Nijmegen. They talked a deal about Arnheim, which they thought was scantily garrisoned and could easily be taken by surprise and made to surrender. Having got these two cities, the plan is to march across the Veluwe and offer battle to the Stadtholder with a force vastly superior to his, if in the meanwhile﻿—”

He paused. It seemed as if his voice, hoarse with fatigue, was refusing him service. Diogenes reached for the potion which stood on a small table beside the bed. The sick man made a wry face.

===Chinese===
「他们灌我酒，」毕达哥拉斯一本正经地重申道，「我就装作烂醉如泥。因为我很快就看出来了：他们当我醉得越凶，说起话来就越没遮拦。」

「嗯，那他们都说了些什么？」

「他们说德·贝格（De Berg）将带一万人马在多斯堡（Doesburg）和布龙克霍斯特（Bronchorst）之间渡过艾瑟尔河（the Ijssel）；还说伊森堡（Isembourg）同一时节从克莱沃（Kleve）开上来。我毫不怀疑，他们的图谋是夺取阿纳姆（Arnheim）和奈梅亨（Nijmegen）。他们大谈特谈阿纳姆，说那里守军单薄，可以轻而易举地偷袭得手，逼它投降。拿下这两座城之后，计划就是挥师横穿费吕韦，以一支远胜执政的兵力向他求战，倘若在此期间——」

他停住了。他那副累哑了的嗓子，仿佛再不肯听他使唤。第欧根尼伸手去拿床头小桌上放着的药饮。病人挤出一副苦脸。

===Original===
“Physic?” he ejaculated reproachfully. “From you, old compeer? Times were when﻿—”

“There will be a time now,” retorted the other gruffly, “when you’ll sink back into a raging fever, and will be babbling bibulous nonsense if you don’t do as you are told.”

“I’ll sink into a raging fever now,” the sick man retorted fretfully, “if I have not something potable to drink ere long.”

“You’ll drink this physic now, old compeer,” Diogenes insisted, and held the mug to his friend’s parched lips, forcing him to drink. “Then I’ll see what can be done for you later on.”

He schooled himself to patience and gentleness. At all costs Pythagoras must complete his narrative. There was just something more that he wished to say, apparently﻿—something fateful and of deadly import, but which for some obscure reason he found difficult to put into words.

“Now then, old friend, make an effort!” Diogenes urged insistently. “There is still something on your mind. What is it?”

===Chinese===
「药？」他一脸责备地嚷道。「你给的，老伙伴？想当年——」

「再不听话，回头就要轮到你烧得说胡话了，」对方没好气地顶回去，「烧得一塌糊涂，满嘴酒话连篇。」

「这会儿我就要烧起来说胡话了，」病人烦躁地回嘴道，「要是再不快点给我点能进口的东西喝的话。」

「先把这药喝了，老伙伴，」第欧根尼不容分说，把杯子凑到朋友干裂的唇边，逼着他喝了下去。「回头我再想法子给你弄点别的。」

他强压着性子，放轻放缓。无论如何，毕达哥拉斯得把话讲完。看样子他还有话要说——某件关系重大、性命攸关的事，可不知怎的，他就是难以启齿。

「好了，老朋友，加把劲！」第欧根尼再三催促道。「你心里还有事。到底是什么？」

===Original===
Pythagoras’ round, beady eyes were rolling in their sockets. He looked scared, like one who has gazed on what is preternatural and weird.

“Stoutenburg has a project,” he resumed after a while, and sank his spent voice to the merest whisper. “Listen, my compeer; for the very walls have ears. Bend yours to me. There! That’s better,” he added, as Diogenes bent his long back until his ear was almost on a level with the sick man’s lips. “Stoutenburg hath a project, I tell you. A damnable project, akin to the one which you caused to abort three months ago.”

“Assassination?” Diogenes queried curtly.

The sick man nodded.

“Do you know the details?”

“Alas, no! But it is aimed at the Stadtholder. What form it is to take I know not, and they had evidently talked it all over before. It seemed almost as if the other man﻿—Stoutenburg’s friend﻿—was horrified at the project. He tried to argue once or twice, and once I heard him say quite distinctly: ‘Not that, Stoutenburg! Let us fight him like men; even kill him, like men kill one another. But not like that.’ But my Lord Stoutenburg only laughed.”

===Chinese===
毕达哥拉斯那双圆滚滚的珠子眼在眼窝里直转。他一脸惊恐，像见过什么超乎自然的怪异之物的人。

「斯托滕贝格有一个计划，」过了一会儿他又开了口，衰竭的声音低成了耳语。「听着，我的伙伴；隔墙有耳。把耳朵凑过来。对！这就好了。」第欧根尼弯下他长长的脊背，耳朵几乎贴到病人唇边，他又添了一句。「我告诉你，斯托滕贝格有个计划。一个狠毒的计划，跟你三个月前搅黄的那个是一路货。」

「行刺？」第欧根尼一针见血地问。

病人点了点头。

「细节你可知道？」

「唉，不知道！可那计谋是对着执政来的。要闹成什么样子，我不得而知；他们显然早就合计停当了。倒像是另外那个人——斯托滕贝格那位朋友——一听这计谋就骇得魂不附体。他有一两回想争辩几句，有一回我听得清清楚楚，他说：『不能那样，斯托滕贝格！咱们像男子汉一样同他斗；哪怕杀了他，也照男子汉彼此相杀的规矩杀。可不能那样干。』可斯托滕贝格勋爵只是笑。」

===Original===
Diogenes was silent. He was deep in thought.

“You had no other indication?” he asked reflectively.

“No,” Pythagoras replied. “All I saw was that my lord kept the finger and thumb of his right hand in a hidden pocket of his doublet, and once he said: ‘The Prince of Poets taught me to manufacture them; and I supply them to him you know of, wherever he can find an opportunity to come out here to me. He uses them at his discretion. But we can judge by results!’ And then he laughed because his friend appeared to shudder. I was puzzled,” the sick man went on wearily, “because of it all; and I marvelled who the Prince of Poets might be, for I am no scholar and I thought that perhaps﻿—”

“You are quite sure Stoutenburg said ‘Prince of Poets’?” Diogenes insisted, frowning. “Your ears must have been buzzing by then.”

“I am quite sure,” Pythagoras asserted. “But I could not see what he had in his hand.”

===Chinese===
第欧根尼不作声了。他陷入了沉思。

「旁的迹象就没有了？」他若有所思地问。

「没有了，」毕达哥拉斯答道。「我只看见老爷大人右手的拇指和食指一直插在紧身衣一只暗袋里，有一回他还说：『是诗人亲王（the Prince of Poets）教我造这些东西的；我供给那位你知道的贵人，只等他寻着机会出城到我这里来。他只管看着情形用。咱们看结果就知道成效了！』说完他见他的朋友似乎打了个寒噤，便哈哈笑了。这一桩桩一件件，」病人倦怠地往下说，「把我闹糊涂了；我心里纳闷，这位诗人亲王究竟是谁——我本不是个读书人，我想说不定——」

「你敢肯定斯托滕贝格说的是『诗人亲王』？」第欧根尼皱着眉追问。「你那时耳朵八成已经嗡嗡作响了吧。」

「我敢肯定，」毕达哥拉斯一口咬定。「只是他手里捏的是什么，我可看不见。」

===Original===
Diogenes said nothing more, and silence fell upon the stately chamber, the sombre panelling and heavy tapestries of which effectually deadened every sound that came from the outside. Only the monumental clock up against the wall ticked in a loud monotone. The sick man, wearied with so much talking, fell back against the pillows. The shades of evening were quickly gathering in now; the corners of the room were indistinguishable in the gloom. Only the bedclothes still gleamed white in the uncertain light. From the distant tower of St. Maria Kerk a bell chimed the hour of seven. A few minutes went by. Anon there came a scratching at the door.

In response to Diogenes’ loud “Enter!” the physician came in, preceded by a serving-man carrying two lighted candles in massive silver sconces.

“His Highness cannot wait any longer,” the physician said, as soon as he had perceived Diogenes, still sitting pensive on the edge of the bed. “And as I have no anxiety about the patient now, I will, by your leave, place him in your hands.”

Diogenes appeared to wake as if out of a dream. He rose and looked about him somewhat vaguely. The physician thought he must have been asleep.

===Chinese===
第欧根尼没再说什么。寂静落在那间气派的房间里，深色的壁板和厚重的挂毯把外面的一切声响都严严实实地闷了下去。只有靠墙立着的那座大座钟单调而响亮地滴答着。病人说了这么多话，乏了，倒回枕头上。暮色此刻正迅速合拢；屋子的几个角落已在昏暗里分辨不清。只有被褥在明灭不定的光线里还泛着白。远处圣玛丽亚教堂（St. Maria Kerk）的钟楼上，一口钟敲响了七点。几分钟过去，随后门上响起一阵窸窣的敲击声。

第欧根尼高喝一声「进来！」，医生应声进来，前头引路的是个仆人，捧着两支巨大的银烛台，烛火通明。

「殿下不能再等了，」医生一看见第欧根尼还坐在床沿上出神，便开口道。「好在如今病人已无大碍，恕我擅专，就把他交到您手上了。」

第欧根尼像从梦中醒来似的。他站起身，神色恍惚地四下望了望。医生还道他是睡着了。

===Original===
“Will you pay your respects to his Highness?” the latter said. “I think he desires to see you.”

Just for a moment Diogenes remained quite still. The physician had approached the sick man, and was surveying him with critical but obviously reassured attention. Socrates was again snoring somewhere in a far corner of the room, and the serving-man, having placed the candles on the table, stood waiting at the door.

“Yes. I’ll to his Highness,” Diogenes said abruptly; and, beckoning to the serving-man to precede him, he strode out of the room.

Outside on the landing he paused. Then, with a characteristic, impulsive gesture, he suddenly beat his forehead with the palm of his hand.

“The Prince of Poets, of course!” he murmured under his breath. “Francis Borgia, the true descendant of his infamous ancestors! Poison! And a slow one at that! Oh, the miserable assassins! Please God, this knowledge hath not come too late!” he added with earnest fervor.

===Chinese===
「您去给殿下请个安吧？」后者说道。「我想殿下正要见您。」

第欧根尼有一会儿纹丝不动。医生已走到病人跟前，正带着挑剔却又分明安心的神色细细察看。苏格拉底又在屋子远处哪个角落里打起了鼾；那仆人把蜡烛摆上桌子，垂手立在门边候着。

「好。我这就去见殿下。」第欧根尼突兀地说。他招手示意仆人头前引路，大步走出了房间。

出了门，在楼梯口他停住了。接着，他带着那股天生的一时冲动，猛地用手掌拍了一下自己的额头。

「诗人亲王，当然是他！」他低声自语。「弗朗西斯·博尔吉亚（Francis Borgia），他那些臭名昭著的祖上地地道道的孝子贤孙！毒药！还是一味慢性的毒药！哦，这群卑鄙的刺客！求上帝保佑，这消息来得还不算太晚！」他又满心热切地补了一句。

### V / 五

===Original===
A quarter of an hour later the Stadtholder was in possession of all the facts as they had been revealed to Diogenes by his comrade in arms.

“I seem fated,” he said to Diogenes kindly, yet not without a measure of bitterness, “to owe my safety to you and your brother philosophers.”

He was discussing De Berg’s surprise plans on Arnheim and Nijmegen. Of that abominable crime, hatched with the chance aid of a poison-mongering Borgia, Diogenes had not as yet spoken one word. Accustomed to swift decisions and prompt action, he had already made up his mind that he would speak of it first to the English physician, whose business it would be to see to it that the insidious poison no longer reached the prince’s lips, at the same time enjoining the strictest secrecy in the matter; for it would only be by rigid circumspection and ceaseless watching that the assassin’s accomplice could be brought to justice.

Mynheer Beresteyn and some of his older friends were in the room with his Highness. They all put their grave heads together, for there was no doubt that the Archduchess’s advisers had planned an invasion of the United Provinces on a grand scale.

“Arnheim is insufficiently defended, of that there’s no doubt,” the Stadtholder said. “It was my intention to reinforce all the frontier cities, and to keep their garrisons up to the requisite numbers. If I only had the strength﻿—”

He paused. The feeling of physical weakness consequent on disease caused him endless and acute bitterness.

===Chinese===
一刻钟后，执政已经尽知他的战友向第欧根尼吐露的全部实情。

「我像是命里注定，」他对第欧根尼和颜悦色地说，却又带着几分苦涩，「全靠你和你那几位哲人兄弟，才保得住这条性命。」

他此刻谈的是德·贝格偷袭阿纳姆和奈梅亨的图谋。至于那桩穷凶极恶的罪愆——偶然得了个贩卖毒药的博尔吉亚相助才孵出壳来的——第欧根尼还只字未提。他素来当机立断、雷厉风行，早已拿定主意：先把这事告诉那位英格兰医生，让他设法从此叫那阴毒的毒药再沾不得亲王的唇，同时嘱咐他此事须守口如瓶；因为唯有严加提防、时刻监视，才能将刺客的同伙捉拿归案。

贝雷斯坦老爷和他的几位老友都在殿下房里。一个个忧心忡忡地凑在一处，因为毫无疑问，大公夫人（the Archduchess）的谋臣们正在筹划一场大规模入侵尼德兰联省共和国（the United Provinces）的战争。

「阿纳姆守备不足，这一点毫无疑问，」执政说。「我本打算加固所有边境城镇，把各地守军补足到应有的数目。只恨我心有余而力不足——」

他停住了。疾病留下的体虚乏力，给他带来无穷无尽的锥心苦楚。

===Original===
“It is not too late to send troops to Arnheim and to Nijmegen,” Diogenes broke in, in his usual abrupt manner. “Three thousand in one city, four thousand in the other would be sufficient, if your Highness can act quickly.”

“I cannot detach seven or eight thousand troops from my forces at the present moment,” the prince rejoined. “If Spinola were to attack from the south I am only just strong enough to defend myself as it is.”

“Marquet is in Overijssel, I believe,” urged the soldier. “He hath three or four thousand troops. Let him push on to Arnheim to reinforce the garrison.”

“And De Keysere is at Wageningen,” the prince broke in, fired, despite himself, by the other’s enthusiasm. “He hath three thousand mercenaries from Switzerland and Germany.”

“Excellent fighters and well-seasoned,” Diogenes asserted. “And trained under Maurice of Nassau, the first captain of this or any epoch!”

“Ay!” sighed Maurice wearily. “But time is against us. Marquet is at Vorden﻿—”

===Chinese===
「现在派兵去阿纳姆和奈梅亨还不算晚，」第欧根尼用他惯常那种直截了当的口气插话道。「一座城三千，另一座四千，也就够了——只要殿下动作快。」

「眼下一时半会儿，我可抽不出七八千兵马，」亲王答道。「万一斯皮诺拉（Spinola）再从南面打过来，我这点兵力，如今也只够勉力自保。」

「马凯（Marquet）我记得正驻在上艾瑟尔（Overijssel），」军人怂恿道。「他手底下有三四千人马。让他直趋阿纳姆，增援守军就是了。」

「德·凯泽雷（De Keysere）就在瓦赫宁恩（Wageningen），」亲王插话道，不由自主地被对方的热忱点燃了。「他手下有三千瑞士和德意志的雇佣兵。」

「个个都是百战精兵，」第欧根尼断言道。「还是在拿骚的莫里斯——古往今来第一等将才——麾下调教出来的！」

「唉！」莫里斯疲惫地叹道。「可是时间跟我们作对。马凯眼下在沃尔登（Vorden）——」

===Original===
“But Arnheim and Nijmegen can hold out for awhile,” Diogenes argued forcefully.

“And would hold out to the last man,” Mynheer Beresteyn added, “if they knew that succor would come in due course.”

“ ’Tis only uncertainty that paralyses the endurance of a garrison,” Diogenes went on with firm emphasis. “Send to Arnheim and to Nijmegen, your Highness! Bid them hold out against any attack until you come with ten thousand troops to their aid. In the meanwhile, send orders to Marquet and to De Keysere to advance forthwith with reinforcements for these two garrisons. Then raise your standard once more in Friesland, Drenthe, and Groningen. I’ll warrant you will have twenty thousand men there ready to fight once more for liberty and for you!”

His sonorous voice rang clear and metallic in the small, panelled room. His enthusiasm appeared almost like a living thing, a tangible force that touched the hearts and minds of all the solemn burghers here, causing their eyes to glow and their fists, not yet wholly unskilled in the use of the sword, to clench with inward excitement. The Stadtholder looked up at him with undisguised admiration.

“Is it the English blood in you, man,” he said with a smile, “that makes you valorous in war and wise in counsel?”

Diogenes shrugged his broad shoulders.

===Chinese===
「可阿纳姆和奈梅亨支撑一阵子总还撑得住，」第欧根尼振振有词地争辩道。

「而且会血战到最后一人，」贝雷斯坦老爷补充道，「只要他们知道救兵迟早会到。」

「叫守军撑不住的，从来就是吉凶未卜，」第欧根尼接着说，语气斩钉截铁。「派使者去阿纳姆和奈梅亨吧，殿下！传令他们顶住一切进攻，直到殿下亲率一万人马前去解围。与此同时，火速传令马凯和德·凯泽雷，即刻发兵增援这两处守军。然后再到弗里斯兰（Friesland）、德伦特（Drenthe）和格罗宁根（Groningen）把大旗重新竖起来！我敢担保，那里会有两万人枕戈待旦，重又为自由、为殿下而战！」

他那洪亮的嗓音在这间镶板小屋里铿然作响，清越如金铁。他的热忱几乎像个活物，一股摸得着的力量，触动着在座每一位庄重的市民的心和头脑，叫他们眼中冒火，叫他们那双还不曾全然荒疏剑术的拳头，因心头的激奋而攥紧。执政抬起眼望他，钦慕之情毫不掩饰。

「朋友，是你血管里的英格兰血，」他含笑说道，「叫你临阵勇猛、运筹明智的吗？」

第欧根尼耸了耸宽阔的肩膀。

===Original===
“I fought for your Highness before now,” he rejoined, with a quaint, self-deprecating laugh, “when I had nothing to lose save my skin, and still less to gain. The English blood in me dearly loves a fight, and all doth hate the Spaniard and all his tyrannies.”

“Then I can reckon on you?” the prince riposted quickly.

“On me, your Highness?” the other exclaimed.

“On you, of course. With your mother’s blood in your veins, the United Provinces have a double claim on you. You have fought for us before, as you say, unknown to us then, an obscure soldier of fortune with nothing to lose and but little to gain. Join us now, man, in the field and under the council tent. Get to horse tonight. You will find Marquet at Vorden, on his way south from Overijssel. Tell him to push on at once to Arnheim with all the troops he hath at his command. From thence I would bid you go straightway to De Keysere, who is at Wageningen, and order him to reinforce Nijmegen forthwith with three thousand men, if we have them. Tell both Marquet and De Keysere to fight and hold the towns. I’ll to their aid as soon as may be. Then, man, join my brother Frederick, and help him to raise my standard in Gelderland and in Overijssel, and rally ten thousand men to our cause. I feel that success will attend our arms if we keep you by our side.”

Maurice of Nassau had spoken with more vigor and verve than he had shown for the past three months. Indeed, his deeply anxious friends could not help but feel that the old fighting spirit of this peerless commander had not wholly been undermined by disease. Five pairs of eager eyes had scanned his features while he spoke; five hearts beat in response to his enthusiasm. Now, when he had finished speaking, Mynheer Beresteyn and the others turned their expectant gaze upon the stranger who had been so signally honoured; but he looked uncertain, gravely perturbed. In the flickering light of the wax candles his face appeared haggard and drawn, and a set line had crept around his ever-laughing lips.

“You seem to hesitate, my friend,” the Stadtholder remarked, with that tone of bitterness which had become habitual to him. “Methought you said that the English blood in you dearly loved a fight. But in truth, I had forgotten! You have other claims upon you now﻿—one, at least, which is paramount. An easy, untroubled life awaits you. No wonder you hesitate to embark on so perilous an adventure!” Then, as if loth to give up the thought that was foremost in his mind, he added, with persuasive insistence; “If you followed me, you’d have everything to gain﻿—nothing to lose save a sentimental pastime.”

===Chinese===
「我从前就为殿下打过仗，」他答道，发出一声古怪的自嘲的笑，「那时节我除了这条性命一无所有，更谈不上什么图获。我身上的英格兰血最爱厮杀，也最恨西班牙人和他那一整套暴政。」

「那么我可以指望你了？」亲王立刻反问。

「指望我，殿下？」对方失声道。

「当然是指望你。你血管里流着你母亲的血，联省对你便有双重的权利。你说你从前为我们打过仗，那时我们并不知情——一个无名无姓的冒险军人，一无所有，也无所图。现在，朋友，到阵地来、到军帐中来同我们并肩吧。今夜就上马。你到沃尔登会找到马凯，他正从上艾瑟尔南下。叫他即刻率麾下全部人马直趋阿纳姆。然后我要你从那里径去瓦赫宁恩找德·凯泽雷，命他立刻发三千人增援奈梅亨——如果拿得出的话。告诉马凯和德·凯泽雷两人：只管死战守城，我必尽早驰援。然后，朋友，去找我弟弟弗雷德里克（Frederick），助他在海尔德兰（Gelderland）和上艾瑟尔把我的大旗竖起来，再召一万人马投到我们麾下。我觉得，有你在我们身边，我们的臂膀所向必能成功。」

拿骚的莫里斯这一番话，说得比过去三个月里任何一次都有气魄、有神采。他那几位忧心如焚的朋友不禁感到：这位无双统帅的老迈斗志，并没有叫病魔消磨殆尽。他说话时，五双热切的眼睛紧盯着他的面容；五颗心随着他的热忱怦怦跳动。此刻他说完了，贝雷斯坦老爷和众人都把期待的目光投向这位备受殊荣的外乡人；可他神色踌躇，心事重重。烛光摇曳里，他的脸显得憔悴而紧绷，那素来挂着笑意的嘴角，也爬上了一道僵硬的纹路。

「你似乎在犹豫，我的朋友，」执政说，带着那股已成为他习惯的苦涩口吻。「我记得你刚说过，你身上的英格兰血最热爱厮杀。不过说真的，我倒是忘了！你如今另有牵挂——至少有一桩比什么都要紧。安逸清闲的日子正等着你。也难怪你不敢贸然踏上这场凶险的冒险！」随即，仿佛不甘心放弃心头的第一念，他又执着地劝道：「你要是追随我，有百利而无一害——除了消遣一段风流情事，你毫无损失。」

===Original===
Just then Diogenes caught Mynheer Beresteyn’s eyes fixed steadily upon him. The old man who knew well enough what was going on in that wayward, turbulent mind﻿—the doubts, the fears, the hideous, horrible disappointment.

Nothing to lose! Ye gods, at the hour when a whole life’s happiness not only beckoned insistently, but was actually there to hand, like a bunch of ripe and luscious fruit, ready to drop into a yearning hand! Here was the end of a vagabond life, here was love and home and peace, and all to be given up as soon as found to the equally insistent call of honour and of duty!

The others did not speak; perhaps they, too, understood. Men in those days were used to stern sacrifices. They and their forebears had given up their all so that their children’s children might live in freedom and security. They only marvelled if this stranger, with the combative English blood in him, would give up what was so infinitely dear to him﻿—the exquisite wife to whom he had plighted his troth but a few hours ago﻿—and if he would fight for them again as he had done in the past.

The Stadtholder remained moody and silent, and the close atmosphere of the heavily curtained room seemed to become suddenly still, hushed, as if expectant of the grave decision to come. The wax candles burned quite steadily, with just a tiny fillet of smoke rising up towards the low-raftered ceiling, almost like the incense of silent prayer rising unwaveringly to God.

To many the silence appeared absolute, but not to the man who stood in the midst of them all beside a table littered with papers and documents, his slender hand﻿—the hand of an idealist, rendered firm and hard by action﻿—resting lightly upon the board. A tense look in his eyes. Through the silence he could hear his beloved in the little room behind the heavy tapestry. He could hear the soft, insidious sound of the quaint-toned virginal, and her voice, tender and melancholy as the call of the bird to its mate, humming the sweet refrain gently under her breath, with every note she seemed to tear at his heart with an unendurable regret for what might have been.

Oh, it had been such a perfect dream! Gilda and that stately home over in England, and the ride through the night in pursuit of happiness which had proved as elusive as Fata Morgana, as unreal as the phantoms born in the mind of a rhapsodist.

===Chinese===
就在这时，第欧根尼撞上了贝雷斯坦老爷紧盯着他的目光。老人心里明镜似的，知道那颗倔强躁动的心里正翻腾着什么——犹疑、恐惧，还有那难堪而可怕的失望。

毫无损失！老天爷啊——恰恰在这毕生幸福不但频频招手、而且已然唾手可得的时辰，像一串熟透甘美的果子，只等着落进一只渴盼的手掌！流浪生涯的尽头就在这里，爱情、家园、安宁都在这里——可这一切才刚到手，就得奉送给那同样声声催逼的荣誉与责任的召唤！

其他人都没有作声；或许他们也心里有数。那个年头的人，早就惯了严酷的牺牲。他们和他们的先辈倾尽所有，为的就是让子孙后代能在自由与安宁中过活。他们只是暗自悬心：这个满身好斗英格兰血的外乡人，肯不肯舍下那对他无比珍贵的东西——几个钟头前才与他盟誓成婚的娇妻——肯不肯像往日那样，再为他们一战。

执政兀自郁郁沉默着。这间垂着厚重帘幔的屋子，空气本就窒闷，此刻似乎骤然凝滞、噤寂，仿佛在等候一项重大决断的降临。蜡烛烧得纹丝不动，只有细细一线烟笔直上升，飘向低梁的天花板，宛如无声祷告的馨香，坚定不移地升向上帝。

在许多人听来，这寂静已到了极处；可有一个人不这么觉得——他正立在众人当中，靠着一张堆满文书文件的桌子，那只细长的手——一只理想主义者的手，被行动磨砺得坚实——轻轻搭在桌面上。他眼中含着紧张的神色。透过寂静，他听得见心爱的人在那幅厚重挂毯后面的小屋里。他听得见那音色古雅的维金纳琴轻柔缠绵的乐音，听得见她的歌声——温柔而忧郁，宛如鸟儿呼偶的啼鸣——轻轻地、屏着气哼着那甜美的叠句；她每唱一个音，都仿佛在撕扯他的心，叫他为一桩本可成真的事，忍受着难堪的怅憾。

啊，那本是一场多么圆满的好梦！吉尔达，英格兰那座气派的宅邸，还有为追逐幸福而彻夜纵马的旅程——可那幸福到头来缥缈得如海市蜃楼（Fata Morgana），虚幻得如行吟诗人心中生出的魅影。

===Original===
Then the silence did, indeed, become absolute, even to him. Gilda had ceased her song. Only his straining ears caught the sound of her footsteps as she rose from the virginal, then moved swiftly about the room.

“Well,” the Stadtholder reiterated, after awhile, “which is it to me, my friend? I start for Utrecht within the hour and if we are to save Arnheim and Nijmegen, you should be on your way to Vorden with the necessary moneys and my written orders tonight. Of course, I cannot compel you,” he added simply. “The decision rests with you, and if you﻿—”

The words died on his lips, and in an instant all eyes were turned to that end of the room where a heavy portière divided it from the room beyond. A faint rustling sound had come from there, then the grating of metal rings upon the cornice-pole that held the tapestry. The next moment Gilda appeared in the doorway, shadowy, wraithlike in her sombre gown that melted into the gloom. Just her small, white face and delicate hands stood out against the murky background, and the gossamer lace at her throat and wrists.

For a moment she stood there, one hand still holding back the heavy portière, quite still, taking in the company at a glance. A sigh of longing and of renunciation came from an overburdened heart, and was wafted up to the foot of Him who knows all and understands all. Then Gilda allowed the tapestry to fall together behind her, and she came quickly forward. In the other hand she was holding, firmly clasped, her husband’s heavy sword.

She came close to him, and then said simply, with an ingenuous smile: “I thought you might wonder where you had left it. It was in the other room. You will be wanting it, my dear lord, if you start for Vorden within the hour.”

With deft fingers she buckled the sword to his belt. This, in truth, was her decision, and she had acted with scarce a moment’s hesitation, even whilst he marvelled how he could set to work to break her heart by leaving her this night.

===Chinese===
随后，寂静当真到了极处，连他也听不见了。吉尔达停了歌。只有他竖起的耳朵，还捕捉到她从维金纳琴前起身、随即在屋里急急走动的脚步声。

「那么，」过了一会儿，执政又开口道，「你怎么答复我，我的朋友？一个钟头之内我就动身去乌得勒支。要救阿纳姆和奈梅亨，今夜你就该带着必要的款项和我的手令，动身去沃尔登。当然，我不能强逼你，」他坦率地补了一句。「主意由你自己拿，要是你——」

话到他唇边便咽住了。刹那间，所有的目光都转向屋子那一头——那里一道厚重的门帘把里外两间屋隔开。那边先传来一阵轻微的窸窣声，接着是挂帘铜环在帘杆上滑动的涩响。转眼间，吉尔达出现在门口，身影幽幽的，像一缕轻魂，一身深色裙袍融进暗影里。只有她小小的白脸和一双纤手，衬着昏暗的背景凸显出来，还有她喉头和腕上的轻绡蕾丝。

她在那里站了片刻，一只手还挽着那道厚门帘，一动不动，一眼把满屋的人收在眼底。一声渴望与割舍交织的叹息，从一颗不堪重负的心里发出，袅袅地飘送到那全知全能者的座前。随后吉尔达松手让挂帘在身后合拢，快步走上前来。另一只手里，她紧紧攥着她丈夫那柄沉重的剑。

她走到他跟前，带着一副天真的笑容，坦然说道：「我想你也许要纳闷把它落在哪儿了。它在那边屋里。一个钟头之内你就要动身去沃尔登了，亲爱的夫君，你会用得上它的。」

她用灵巧的手指把剑系上他的腰带。这，实实在在，就是她的决断；她几乎不曾有片刻的迟疑，而他还在心里盘算，这一夜弃她而去、可怎么开得了口来伤她的心。

===Original===
Now, when their glances met, they understood one another. The power that lay within both their souls had met and, as it were, clasped hands. They accepted one another’s sacrifice. Hers, mayhap, was the more complete of the two, because for her his absence would mean weary waiting, the dull heartache so terrible to bear.

For the man, the wrench would be eased by action, danger and hard fighting; for her there would be nothing to do but wait. But she acquiesced. No one had seen the struggle which it had cost her, over there in the little room, all alone with only the dumb virginal and the dying light to see the tears of rebellion and of agony which for one brief moment﻿—for her an eternity﻿—had seared her eyes. By the time the full meaning of what she had overheard from the other side of the portière had entered into her brain, she had recovered full outward calm, and had brought him his sword in token of her resolve.

Gilda Beresteyn came of a race that had learned to fight even from its infancy. She had handled her father’s sword at an age when little maids are content with playthings. Now, when she made the buckles of her husband’s sword secure, she met his glance with perfect serenity, and said simply and calmly:

“You will find me, as before, in the other room. I will be waiting there to bid you farewell.”

Then she glided out of the room, wraithlike, ethereal, as she had come. And Diogenes woke as if out of a trance.

===Chinese===
此刻，两双目光相遇，他们彼此都懂了。深藏在两颗灵魂里的那股力量相遇了，并且，可以说，紧紧交握在了一起。他们承受了彼此的牺牲。她这一份，或许比他的更彻底，因为他的离去，对她来说将意味着无尽的等待，意味着那种最难煎熬的、闷闷的心痛。

于男人，生离之痛会有行动、危险与血战来减轻；于她，却什么也没有，只有等。可她默认了。没有人看见她在那间小屋里独自经受的挣扎——只有那架默默无言的维金纳琴和将熄的天光，看见过那叛逆与痛苦的热泪，在短短一瞬——于她却是永恒——灼烧过她的眼睛。等到门帘那边偷听来的话在她脑中显出全部意义时，她外表已恢复了十足的平静，并把他的剑捧到他面前，作为她决心的凭证。

吉尔达·贝雷斯坦生在一个自襁褓起便学会战斗的种族。在小小姑娘们还只知玩弄玩偶的年纪，她已把弄过父亲的剑。此刻，她系牢丈夫剑上的搭扣，神色安详地迎住他的目光，平静而坦然地说：

「我还在那边屋里等你，同方才一样。我会在那儿等你来道别。」

说罢她便飘然出了房间，幽幽的，轻得像个影子，一如她来时那样。第欧根尼如梦方醒。

===Original===
The Stadtholder jumped to his feet. “Then you’re with us?” he exclaimed.

“If your Highness hath need of me,” the soldier replied.

“Have I not said so?” the prince retorted. “Henceforth, Sir Percy Blakeney﻿—for that is your name, is it not?﻿—accompanies us as our Master of the Camp wherever we go!”

“Nay,” the other replied quite firmly and without even a sigh of regret this time, “my name is Diogenes, as it hath always been. It is the nameless and homeless adventurer, the son of the poor Dutch tramp, who once again places his sword at your Highness’s disposal. Sir Percy Blakeney was only a myth, a shade that hath already been exorcized by the magic of your Highness’s call, in the name of our faith and of liberty.”

“Frankly, man,” the Stadtholder retorted with a smile, “I could not picture you in the character of a placid and uxorious country gentleman, watching with unruffled complacence the life and death struggles of your friends.”

“I should have waxed obese, your Highness,” Diogenes assented whimsically; “and the horror of it would have sent me to my grave.”

===Chinese===
执政一跃而起。「那么你肯跟我们干了？」他失声喊道。

「只要殿下用得着我，」军人答道。

「我不是已经说了吗？」亲王反诘道。「从今往后，珀西·布莱克尼爵士（Sir Percy Blakeney）——这是你的名字，不是吗？——将以我军行营总管（Master of the Camp）之职，随我们转战四方！」

「不，」对方答得斩钉截铁，这一回连一声惋惜都没有。「我的名字是第欧根尼，一向都是。此刻把剑再度献到殿下阶前的，是那个无名无家、无根无底的冒险客，是那个贫苦荷兰流浪汉的儿子。珀西·布莱克尼爵士不过是个虚造的名号、一道幻影，早叫殿下以我们的信仰和自由之名的召唤降服祛除了。」

「老实说，朋友，」执政含笑回敬道，「我实在想象不出你做那种心平气和、贪恋家室的乡绅是什么模样——悠哉游哉地旁观着朋友们作生死之斗。」

「那我早就该脑满肠肥了，殿下，」第欧根尼乖觉地应道，「光是想想那副尊容的可怕，也足够送我进坟墓了。」

===Original===
“Then, you inveterate mocker, are you ready to start?”

“Booted and spurred, your Highness, and a sword on my hip,” replied the other lightly. “And my horse hath been waiting for me these two hours past.”

Already Maurice of Nassau was on his feet. He took the sacrifice, the self-denial, as a matter of course; was unaware of it, probably. Every other thought was completely merged in that of the coming struggle﻿—De Berg crossing the Ijssel, Spinola threatening from the south, and victory beckoning once more.

The burghers crowded round him, speaking words of loyalty and of encouragement. He responded with somewhat curt farewells. His thoughts were no longer here; they were across the Veluwe with Marquet and De Keysere; inside Arnheim and Nijmegen.

He kept Diogenes by his side, wrote out his orders in sign-manual, discussed plans, possibilities with the man in whose luck and resource he had unbounded belief.

It took time to get everything ready. There was the financial question, too, for some of the troops were mercenaries, who would be demanding their pay ere they engaged to start on a fresh expedition. For this the aid of the loyal burghers had again to be requisitioned. Arrangements had to be made for credits at Zutphen and Arnheim.

===Chinese===
「那么，你这积习难改的爱挖苦人的家伙，这就准备动身了？」

「长靴马刺都齐了，殿下，剑就挂在胯上，」对方轻快地答道。「再说我的马已经等了我两个钟头了。」

拿骚的莫里斯已经站起身来。他把这份牺牲、这份自我克制视作理所应当，多半根本无所察觉。别的念头已全然汇入那场即将到来的争斗之中——德·贝格渡过艾瑟尔河，斯皮诺拉在南面虎视眈眈，还有再一次招手的胜利。

市民们围拢上来，说着效忠与鼓劲的话。他的道别却有些简慢。他的心思已不在这里；早已飞过费吕韦，到了马凯和德·凯泽雷身边，进了阿纳姆和奈梅亨城里。

他把第欧根尼留在身边，亲笔写下手令，同这个他对其运气与才干怀有无穷信心的男人商讨计划与种种可能。

诸事齐备颇费了些工夫。还有钱的问题：一部分军队是雇佣兵，不先讨到饷银，是不肯应承开拔新征的。为此又不得不仰仗忠心市民的援手。在聚特芬（Zutphen）和阿纳姆两地支取银钱的安排，也得一一落定。

===Original===
This part of the great adventure the Stadtholder was willing to leave in the hands of Mynheer Beresteyn and his friends. Money to him was dross, save as a means of gaining his great ends. For the nonce he was in a hurry to get away, to get back to his camp at Utrecht, and to make ready for the coming fight.

Then at last there came a moment when everything appeared settled. The messenger had his sealed orders, and the credit notes and the read money upon his person. The Stadtholder was back in the hall with his equerries around him, ready for departure, giving brief, decisive orders such as soldiers love to hear.

But Diogenes did not follow him immediately, and Mynheer Beresteyn remained behind with him. He was the only one who really understood what the once careless and thoughtless adventurer felt at this moment, in face of the inevitable farewell. It was an understanding born in a staunch heart that had known both love and sorrow.

Beresteyn had idolized his young wife, who had died leaving her baby-girl in his arms. That deep affection the lonely widower had thereupon transferred to his motherless daughter, had cherished and guarded her as his most precious treasure, and had only consented to relinquish her into the guardianship of another because he knew that the other was worthy of the trust.

===Chinese===
这桩大业里这一部分，执政乐于交到贝雷斯坦老爷和他朋友们手里。于他，钱财不过是粪土，除非是用作成大事的手段。眼下他只急着脱身，赶回乌得勒支的大营，为即将到来的战斗做准备。

终于，万事齐备的时辰到了。信使身上带着火漆封缄的命令，还有支款凭证和现钱。执政已回到大厅里，侍从武官们环立左右，他整装待发，正下达着军人最爱听的那种简短干脆的命令。

可第欧根尼没有立刻跟出去，贝雷斯坦老爷也同他一道留了下来。这位曾经无忧无虑、万事不萦于心的冒险家，此刻面对无可回避的诀别究竟是什么滋味，唯有老人真正懂得。这份体谅，生在一颗既识得爱、也识得愁的坚贞的心里。

贝雷斯坦曾把他年轻的妻子奉若神明；她撒手人寰，把襁褓中的女儿留在他臂弯里。鳏居的孤独老人便把那一腔深爱移到失去母亲的女儿身上，把她当作最珍贵的宝贝疼惜守护；之所以肯应允把她交托到另一个人的护佑之下，只因他知道那人配得上这份信托。

===Original===
He knew also what hungering passion means; he knew the bitterness of parting and of a burning disappointment with the prospect of loneliness through the vista of years. But, with that infinite tact which is the attribute of a selfless heart, he offered no words of consolation or even of comment.

“I will leave you to bid farewell to Gilda alone,” was all that he said.

Diogenes nodded in assent. The most terrible moment of this terrible hour was yet to come, for Gilda, having precipitated his decision, was now waiting for the last kiss.

===Chinese===
他也懂得如饥似渴的痴情意味着什么；他尝过生离的苦味，尝过灼人的失望，以及往后经年累月孤独前景的滋味。可是，凭着一颗无私的心才有的那份无限体贴，他没说一句宽慰的话，甚至连一句评论也没有。

「我让你单独同吉尔达道别吧。」他只说了这一句。

第欧根尼点头应了。这难堪时辰里最难堪的一刻还没有来——吉尔达既已亲手促成他的决断，此刻正等着最后那一吻。

### VI / 六

===Original===
She was, in truth, waiting for him, submissive and composed. What she had done, when she with her own act had mutely bidden him to go, that she did not regret. She had done it not so much perhaps from a sense of duty or of patriotism, but rather because she knew that this course was the only one that he would never rue.

Hers was that perfect love that dwells on the other’s happiness, and not on its own. She knew that, though for the time being he would find bliss and oblivion in her arms, he would soon repine in inactivity whilst others fought for that which he held sublime.

So now, when he pushed aside the tapestry and once more stood before her, with the lovelight in his eyes obscured by the shadow of this coming parting, she met him without a tear. The next moment he had her in his arms, and his hand rested lightly across her eyes, lest they should perceived that his were full of tears.

For a long while he could not speak; then he drew her closer to him and pressed his lips against hers, drinking in all the joy and rapture which he might never taste again.

===Chinese===
她确实在等他，温顺而沉静。她亲手把剑捧给他、无言地遣他出征，这桩事她并不懊悔。她那样做，与其说是出于责任或爱国之心，不如说是因为她知道：唯有这条路，是他永生不会追悔的。

她的爱是那种至善至美的爱：念兹在兹的是对方的幸福，而不是自己的。她知道，他虽能一时在她的怀抱里觅得极乐与忘忧，可一旦别人为着他视若神圣的事业奋战、他却闲坐无为时，他很快就会郁郁难安。

于是此刻，当他拨开挂毯、重新站到她面前，眼中的爱意被将临的别离蒙上一层阴翳时，她迎着他，眼眶里没有一滴泪。转眼间他已把她搂进怀里，一只手轻轻覆住她的眼睛，免得她瞧见他自己眼里已噙满了泪水。

好长一阵子他说不出话来；随后他把她搂得更紧，将唇贴上她的唇，痛饮那他也许再也无从品尝的欢愉与至乐。

===Original===
“What is it that hath happened, my lord?” she murmured. “I could not hear everything, and did not wish to be caught prying. All that I heard was that the Stadtholder needed you, and that in your heart you knew that your place, whilst there was danger to our land, was by his side, and not by mine.”

“Your father will explain more fully, my beloved,” he replied. “You are right. The Stadtholder hath need of every willing sword. This unfortunate land is gravely threatened. The Archduchess is throwing the full force of her armies against the Netherlands. His Highness thinks that I might help to save the United Provinces from becoming once more the vassals of Spain. As you say, my place is on this soil where I and my mother were born. I should be a coward indeed were I to turn my back now on this land when danger is so grave. So I am going, my beloved,” he continued simply.

“Tonight I go to Vorden on his Highness’s business, thence on to Wageningen. I shall go, taking your dear image in my heart, and with your exquisite face before me always. For I love you with every fibre of my being, every bone in my body and with every beat of my heart. Try not to weep, my dear. I shall return one day soon to take you in my arms, as I shall clasp your spirit only until then. I shall return, doubt it not. Such love as ours was not created to remain unfulfilled. Whatever may happen, believe and trust in me, as I shall believe in you, and keep the remembrance of me in your heart without sadness and without regret.”

He spoke chiefly because he dared not trust to the insidiousness of silence. He knew that she wept for the first time because of him. Yet how could it be otherwise? And sorrow made her sacred. When, overcome with grief, she lay half-swooning in his arms, he picked her up quite tenderly and laid her back against the cushions of the chair. Then, as she sat there, pale and wan-looking in the uncertain light of the wax candles, with those exquisite hands of hers lying motionless in her lap, he knelt down before her.

For a second or two he rested his head against those soft white palms, fragrant as the petals of a lily. Then he rose, and, without looking at her again, he walked firmly out of the room.

===Chinese===
「到底出什么事了，夫君？」她喃喃地问。「那边的话我没有全听见，也不愿落个偷听的嫌疑。我只听见执政需要你，而你心里也知道，只要国土尚在危难之中，你的位置就在他身边，不在我身边。」

「你父亲会把一切详详细细讲给你听，我亲爱的，」他答道。「你说得对。执政需要每一柄肯出力的剑。这不幸的国土正受着严重的威胁。大公夫人正倾其全军之力压向联省。殿下认为，我或许能助一臂之力，使联省不至再度沦为西班牙的附庸。正如你说的，我的位置在这片土地上——我和我母亲都生于斯。危难如此深重，此刻我若背过身去不理这片国土，那我就真是个懦夫了。所以我要去了，亲爱的，」他坦然地接着说。

「今夜我奉殿下之命去沃尔登，再从那里去瓦赫宁恩。我会带着你可爱的影像在心里上路，让你那姣好的面容永远在我眼前。因为我爱你，用我全身的每一缕血肉、每一根骨头、每一次心跳来爱你。亲爱的，尽力别哭。不久的将来，总有一天，我会回来把你拥进怀中——在那之前，我怀抱的只是你的魂灵。我会回来的，不要疑心。像我们这样的爱，造物主造它出来，原不是叫它落空的。无论发生什么，都要信我、信赖我，如同我信你、信赖你一样；把我留在你心里，不要悲伤，也不要遗憾。」

他絮絮地说，多半是因为不敢任凭沉默作祟。他知道，为了他，她这是头一回落泪。可这又怎能避免呢？悲伤使她显得神圣。当哀恸将她压倒、她半晕地瘫在他怀里时，他无比温柔地抱起她，放回到椅子的软垫上。随后，当她坐在那里，在明灭不定的烛光里面色苍白、形容憔悴，那双纤美无伦的手一动不动地搁在膝上，他在她面前跪了下来。

有一两秒钟，他把头靠在那双柔软白皙的手掌上——那手心里透着百合花瓣般的芬芳。然后他站起身，没有再看她一眼，坚定地走出了房间。

## IV Adder’s Fork / IV 蝰蛇之叉

### I / 一

===Original===
Nicolaes Beresteyn accompanied his brother-in-law during the first part of the journey. He had insisted on this, despite Diogenes’ preference for solitude. There was not much comradeship lost between the two men. Though the events of that memorable New Years Day, distant less than three months, were ostensibly consigned to oblivion, nevertheless, the bitter humiliation which Nicolaes had suffered at the hands of the then nameless soldier of fortune still rankled in his heart. Since then so many things had come to light which, to an impartial observer, more than explained Gilda Beresteyn’s love for the stranger, and Mynheer her father’s acquiescence in an union based on respect for so brave a man.

But Nicolaes had held aloof from the intimacy, and soon his own courtship of the wealthy Kaatje gave him every reason for withdrawing more and more from his own family circle. But tonight, after the tempestuous close of what should have been a merely conventional day, he sought Diogenes’ company in a way he had never done before.

===Chinese===
旅程的头一段，尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦（Nicolaes Beresteyn）伴送他的妹夫同行。尽管第欧根尼（Diogenes）更愿意独自赶路，他却执意如此。两人之间，本就谈不上多少情谊。那个难忘的元旦，距今还不到三个月；那些事表面上已被置诸脑后，然而尼古拉斯当日在那位尚且无名无姓的雇佣冒险家手里所受的奇耻大辱，依旧在他心头隐隐作痛。自那以后，多少内情已水落石出——在一位持正的旁观者看来，这些已足以说明吉尔达·贝雷斯坦（Gilda Beresteyn）为何爱上这个异乡人，也足以说明她的父亲贝雷斯坦老爷（Mynheer）为何默许了这桩姻缘——它原是建立在敬重这般勇士之上的。

可是尼古拉斯始终与这份亲近保持着距离，不久，他自己对豪富的卡特耶（Kaatje）的求婚，又给了他十足的理由，使他离自己的家门越来越远。然而今夜，这个本该照章礼俗度过的日子，却以一场风波收了场；此后他竟一反常态，来寻第欧根尼作伴，这是从来没有过的事。

===Original===
“Like you,” he said, “I am wearied and sick with all this mummery. A couple of hours on the Veluwe will set me more in tune with life.”

Diogenes chaffed him not a little.

“The lovely Kaatje will pout,” he suggested, “and rightly, too. You have no excuse for absenting yourself from her side at this hour.”

“I’ll come with you as far as Barneveld,” Nicolaes insisted. “A matter of less than a couple of hours’ ride. It will do me good. And Kaatje is still closeted with her garrulous mother.”

“You think it will do her good to be kept waiting,” Diogenes retorted with good-natured sarcasm. “Well, come, if you have a mind. But I’ll not have your company further than Barneveld. I am used to the Veluwe, and intend taking a shortcut over the upland, through which I would not care to take a companion less well acquainted with the waste than I.”

===Chinese===
「同您一样，」他说，「这一通虚文缛节也叫我厌烦透了、恶心透了。到费吕韦（the Veluwe）去走上两个钟头，我才能重新觉得活着有滋味。」

第欧根尼没少拿他打趣。

「可爱的卡特耶可要撅嘴了，」他提点道，「而且撅得应该。这个时辰离开她身边，您可没有借口。」

「我送您到巴讷费尔德（Barneveld），」尼古拉斯执意道，「不过骑马不到两个钟头的路程，对我也有好处。再说，卡特耶这会儿还陪着她那位絮絮叨叨的娘关起门说话呢。」

「您倒觉得，让她干等着对她有好处，」第欧根尼带着善意的讥诮回敬道。「好吧，您有兴致就来。可是过了巴讷费尔德，我就不再要您做伴了。费吕韦我走惯了，打算抄高地上的一条近路；那条路上，谁要是对那片荒原不及我熟，我可不愿意带他走。」

===Original===
Thus it was decided. Already the Stadtholder had gone with his numerous retinue, with his bodyguard and his pike-men and with his equerries, and those of the wedding-party who had come in his train from Utrecht, friends of Mynheer Beresteyn, who had ridden over for the most part with wife or daughter pillioned behind them, and all glad to avail themselves of the protection of his Highness’s escort against highway marauders, none too scarce in these parts. Torchbearers and linkmen completed the imposing cavalcade, for the night would be moonless, and the tracks across the moorland none too clearly defined.

Diogenes had waited with what patience he could muster until the last of the numerous train had defiled under the Koppel-poort. Then he, too, got to horse. Despite Socrates’ many protestations, he was not allowed to accompany him.

===Chinese===
于是就这么定了。执政（the Stadtholder）早已带着他浩大的随行队伍动身了：卫队、长矛兵、侍从马官，还有随他从乌得勒支（Utrecht）一路同来的那些婚宴宾客——都是贝雷斯坦老爷的亲友，大半骑着马，鞍后载着自家的太太或女儿——人人都乐于借殿下扈从的庇护，防备这一带并不罕见的拦路响马。火炬手和执灯人给这支赫赫扬扬的马队收了尾，因为今夜无月，荒原上的路径又模糊难辨。

第欧根尼强耐着性子，直等到那长长一列人马尽数从科佩尔门（the Koppel-poort）门洞下走过，这才也上了马。任凭苏格拉底（Socrates）再三请求同行，也没获他准许。

===Original===
“You must look after Pythagoras,” was Diogenes’ final word on the subject.

“ ’Tis the first time,” the other answered moodily, “that you go on such an adventure without us. Take care, comrade! The Veluwe is wide and lonely. That swag-bellied oaf up there hath cause to rue his solitary wanderings on that *verfloekte* waste.”

“I’ll be careful, old compeer,” Diogenes retorted with a smile. “But mine errand is not one on which I desire to draw unnecessary attention, and I can remain best unperceived if I am alone. ’Tis no adventure I am embarking on this night. Only a simple errand as far as Vorden, a matter of ten leagues at most.

“And the whole of the *verdommte* Veluwe to traverse at dead of night!” the other muttered sullenly.

“I know every corner of it,” Diogenes rejoined impatiently. “And it will not be the first time that I travel on it alone.”

===Chinese===
「你得照看毕达哥拉斯（Pythagoras），」这是第欧根尼在这件事上的最后一句话。

「这可是头一回，」对方郁郁地答道，「你不带着我们，就独自去冒这样的险。当心啊，伙计！费吕韦又宽又荒。那边那个大肚皮的蠢汉，就够他懊悔独自在那片该死的（*verfloekte*）荒原上乱闯的了。」

「我会当心的，老伙计，」第欧根尼含笑回敬道。「只是我这趟差事，不想招来不必要的注目；一个人走，才最不惹眼。今夜我踏上的可不是什么冒险，不过是去沃尔登（Vorden）办件寻常差事，满打满算十里格的路。」

「可要在大半夜里穿过整个该死的（*verdommte*）费吕韦！」对方悻悻地咕哝道。

「它的每一个角落我都认得，」第欧根尼不耐地应道。「况且这也不是我头一回独自动身上路了。」

===Original===
Thus Socrates was left grumbling, and anon Diogenes, accompanied by Nicolaes Beresteyn, started on his way.

===Chinese===
就这样，苏格拉底被撇在一边咕咕哝哝；不多时，第欧根尼便由尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦作伴，踏上了行程。


### II / 二

===Original===
At first the two men spoke little. The air was still cold and very humid, and the thaw was persisting. The horses stepped out briskly on the soft, sandy earth.

The distance between Amersfoort and Barneveld is but a couple of leagues. Within the hour the lights of the little city could be seen gleaming ahead. After a while Nicolaes Beresteyn became more loquacious, talked quite freely of the past.

===Chinese===
起初两人都很少说话。空气依旧寒冷而潮湿，融雪的天气还在持续。马儿踏着松软的沙土地，步子轻快。

阿默斯福特（Amersfoort）到巴讷费尔德不过两里格的路。不出一个钟头，小城的灯火已在前方闪烁。过了一会儿，尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦的话多起来了，把过去的事也敞开来谈了。

===Original===
“My father no longer trusts me,” he said, with ill-concealed bitterness. “Did you see how he shut me out of the council-chamber?”

“Yet the Stadtholder himself told you everything that occurred subsequently,” Diogenes retorted kindly, “including his own plans and mine errand at this hour. I think that your conscience troubles you unnecessarily, and you see a deliberate intention in every simple act.”

“If I thought that my father still suspected me—” Nicolaes mumbled under his breath.

“And if he did, you could scarce blame him. ’Tis only in the future you can prove your true worth. And methinks,” he added, more seriously than he was usually wont to speak, “that you will have occasion to do this very soon.”

===Chinese===
「我父亲已经不再信任我了，」他说，那股怨苦几乎掩不住。「您瞧见没有，他怎么把我关在议事厅的门外的？」

「可执政他老人家，事后的一切都对你说得明明白白，」第欧根尼好言回敬道，「连他自己的方略、连我此刻这趟差事，也都告诉了你。我看哪，是你的良心在白白折磨你，叫你把每件平常事都看出了成心的算计。」

「倘若我认定父亲还在猜疑我——」尼古拉斯压着嗓子咕哝。

「就算他猜疑，你也怪不得他。唯有将来，才能证明你真正的价值。而且我想，」他补了一句，说得比平日郑重，「这样的机会，很快就会落到你头上。」

===Original===
“In the meanwhile, here’s Barneveld ahead of us,” Nicolaes rejoined, with a quick, indefinable sigh, and giving a sudden turn to the conversation. “I’ll see you across the city, then return to the bosom of my family, there to live in uxorious idleness, whilst you, a stranger, are entrusted with the destinies of our land. A poor outlook for a man who is young and a patriot, you’ll own.”

To this Diogenes thought it best to make no reply. He knew well enough that the mistrust of which Nicolaes accused his father was a very real thing, and that it was indeed only time that would soften the proud burgher’s heart toward his only son. It was not likely that one who but a brief while ago had conspired against the Stadtholder’s life with that abominable Stoutenburg could be admitted readily into the councils of the very man whom he had plotted to assassinate. With every desire to forgive, it was but natural that Mynheer Beresteyn should fail entirely to forget.

===Chinese===
「眼下嘛，巴讷费尔德就在咱们前头了，」尼古拉斯应道，一声短促而说不清所以的叹息，猛地把话头一转。「我送您过城，然后就回到我那个家的怀抱里去，守着老婆过安逸日子；可你呢，一个外乡人，倒被托付了我们家国的命运。对一个年纪轻轻又爱国的人来说，这前景可实在够惨淡的，你自己也得承认。」

这话，第欧根尼觉得还是不答为妙。他心里清楚得很，尼古拉斯埋怨父亲的那种不信任，确有其事；也确实唯有时间，才能软化那位高傲的市民对独子的心肠。一个不久前还同那个可憎的斯托滕贝格（Stoutenburg）合谋刺杀执政性命的人，要想顺顺当当被接纳进他曾经图谋行刺的那本人的议事机要里，谈何容易。贝雷斯坦老爷纵有一万个愿意宽恕，也断然忘不掉前尘，这是人之常情。

===Original===
No more, however, was said upon the subject now, and Nicolaes soon relapsed into that sullen mood which had of late become habitual to him. Thus Diogenes was glad enough to be rid of his company. At Barneveld he obtained a fresh horse, left his own in charge of a man known to him, with orders to ride it quietly on the morrow as far as Wageningen, where he himself would pick it up a couple of days later. His journey would now lie due east to Zutphen. There he meant to make a halt of a few hours, and thence proceed to Vorden, where Marquet was in camp, with four thousand seasoned troops, trained under Mansfeld, and rested now since the campaign in Groningen.

The Stadtholder’s orders were that the general proceed, at once to Arnheim, ere the forces of the Archduchess had time to cross the Ijssel, and to cut off all access to so important a city.

From Vorden to Wageningen, which lies due south form Barneveld, the journey would be a long one, and, with De Berg’s army so near, might even prove perilous. But De Keysere was at Wageningen, with three thousand troops and some artillery. His help would be of immense service to Nijmegen if the latter city, too, were to be attacked.

===Chinese===
这个话题，此刻便不再提起了，尼古拉斯很快又堕回那副近来已成习惯的阴郁神气里。于是，第欧根尼巴不得就此摆脱他的陪伴。到了巴讷费尔德，他换了一匹新马，把自己那匹托付给一个相熟的人，吩咐第二天让它缓缓地骑去瓦赫宁恩（Wageningen），过两天他亲自去取。他这一程将径直东行，赶赴聚特芬（Zutphen），打算在那里歇上几个钟头，再往沃尔登去——马凯（Marquet）正在那里扎营，麾下四千身经百战的精兵，都是曼斯费尔德（Mansfeld）操练出来的，自格罗宁根（Groningen）一役之后休整至今。

执政的军令是：命这位将军即刻挥师阿纳姆（Arnheim），赶在大公夫人（the Archduchess）的兵马来得及渡过艾瑟尔河（the Ijssel）之前，切断通往这座重镇的一切通路。

从沃尔登到瓦赫宁恩——那城正在巴讷费尔德的正南方——路程漫长，而德·贝格（De Berg）的大军又近在咫尺，说不定还会有凶险。好在德·凯泽雷（De Keysere）驻在瓦赫宁恩，手中有三千人马和一些炮队。万一奈梅亨（Nijmegen）那座城也遭攻击，他的援助将对它有莫大的裨益。

===Original===
“How will you journey from Vorden to Wageningen?” Nicolaes asked Diogenes in the end. “You will have to avoid the Ijssel.”

“I’ll cut across to Lang Soeren,” the other replied; “and thence go to Ede.”

“There’s scarce a track on the Veluwe just there,” the other urged.

“Such as there is, I know,” Diogenes retorted curtly. “And I must trust to luck.”

===Chinese===
「从沃尔登到瓦赫宁恩，你打算怎么走？」尼古拉斯末了问第欧根尼。「你总得避开艾瑟尔河。」

「我斜插到朗瑟伦（Lang Soeren），」对方答道；「从那儿去埃德（Ede）。」

「费吕韦那一带，几乎连条路都没有，」对方提醒道。

「有路没路，我心里都有数，」第欧根尼简短地回敬。「剩下的，就只好看运气了。」

===Original===
They had brought their horses to a halt about a quarter of a league outside Barneveld, where the two men decided to part. The stretch of the great waste, with its undulating, barren hills, and narrow, scarce visible tracks, lay straight out before them. Diogenes was sniffing the frosty air out toward the east, where lay Vorden, and whence there came to his nostrils the sharp tang of the breeze, that cut like a knife. The thaw which had held sway in the cities and on the low-lying lands had been vanquished ere it reached the arid upland. The snow upon the Veluwe lay as even and as pure as before. Above, a canopy of stars seemed but a diamond-studded veil of mysterious indigo, stretched over a world of light, which it failed altogether to dim. The silence and desolation were absolute; but not so the darkness. To the keen eye of the adventurer, accustomed to loneliness, the vast stretches of open country and limitless horizons, there was no such thing as absolute darkness. He could perceive the slightest accidental upon the smooth carpet of snow, noted every tiny mound that marked a clump of rough shrub or grass, and every footmark of beast or bird, mere flecks of blue upon the virgin pall.

“Such track as there is, I know,” he had carelessly asserted awhile ago, in response to a warning from Nicolaes. And now, without an instant’s hesitation, and tossing to the other a last curt word of farewell, he gave his horse a slight taste of the spur, and soon became a mere speck upon the illimitable waste.

===Chinese===
在巴讷费尔德城外约四分之一里格的地方，两人勒住了马，就此分手。大片荒原在眼前笔直地铺展开去：波状起伏的童山，窄细得几乎看不见的路径。第欧根尼朝着东方嗅着凛冽的空气——沃尔登就在那个方向，一阵刀割似的疾风，正把那锐利的气息灌进他的鼻孔。在城里和低地上称雄一时的融雪天气，还没爬上干旱的高地，就已经败下阵去了。费吕韦的积雪，依旧平整如初、洁白如初。头顶上，一天星斗恰似一袭缀满钻石的神秘靛蓝面纱，罩在一个光明的世界上，却丝毫无从遮暗它。寂静与荒凉是绝对的；黑暗却不然。在这位惯于孤独的冒险家那双锐眼——那看惯了辽阔旷野和无边地平线的眼睛——看来，世上并没有绝对的黑暗。平滑的雪毯上，哪怕一点最细微的偶然异物，他都察觉得出：每一座标出荆棘灌丛或草丛的小小土丘，每一枚走兽飞禽的足印，他一一收入眼底——那些不过是素白原野上的点点幽蓝。

「有路没路，我心里都有数」——方才面对尼古拉斯的告诫，他曾这样漫不经心地夸下口。此刻，他半刻也不迟疑，朝对方抛下最后一句简短的道别，两脚轻轻一磕马刺，不多时，便化作茫茫无际的荒原上的一个小黑点了。


### III / 三

===Original===
It was close on midnight when, weary, saddle-sore, his boots covered in half-melted snow, Mynheer Nicolaes Beresteyn demanded admittance into his native city.

At first the guard at the Koppel-poort, roused from his slumbers, refused to recognize in the belated traveler the bridegroom of a few hours ago. Had anyone ever heard, I ask you, of a bridegroom absenting himself on the very night of his nuptial until so late an hour? And then returning in a mood that was so irascible and inconsequent that the sergeant in command of the gate was on the point of ordering his detention in the guardroom, pending investigation and the orders of the burgomaster, whose decision on such points was final? But since the burgomaster happened to be Mynheer Beresteyn, and as the weary and pugnacious traveler did, in truth, appear to be his only son—why, it was perhaps best on the whole to take the matter as a joke, and not to say too much about it. The sergeant did, indeed, as Nicolaes was finally allowed to ride over the bridge, essay one or two of the most time-honoured witticisms at the expense of the belated bridegroom; but Mynheer Nicolaes was clearly in no mood for chaff, and when he had passed by, the sergeant and one or two of the men, who had witnessed his strangely sullen mood, shook their heads in ominous prognostication of sundry matrimonial difficulties to come.

===Chinese===
将近午夜时分，尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦老爷才叫开他故乡的城门，此时他一身疲惫，鞍马劳顿、臀股磨得生疼，靴子上糊满了半融的雪。

起初，科佩尔门上被从睡梦中叫醒的守卒，怎么也不肯在这位深夜迟归的旅人身上，认出几个钟头前的那位新郎。请问诸位，谁曾听说过有新郎在洞房花烛之夜抛下新娘，一直逛到这么晚才回来的？回来时还是那副火气冲天、语无伦次的样子，害得守门的军士差一点下令把他扣押进卫兵室，听候查究和市长的裁断——这类事情，市长的话就是铁案。可巧市长正是贝雷斯坦老爷本人，而这疲惫而好斗的旅人，看样子又千真万确是他的独子——得，通盘算下来，这件事还是当作一桩玩笑揭过去为妙，少说为是。等到终于放尼古拉斯过桥时，那军士倒也真的试着拣了一两句拿迟归新郎打趣的老笑话来说；可尼古拉斯老爷分明没有打趣的兴致；等他走远，军士和一两个见过他那副古怪阴郁神气的士兵直摇头，预感到小两口日后少不得有种种风波，凶多吉少。

===Original===
The house on the quay, plainly visible from the Koppel-poort, was dark enough to suggest that every one of its inmates was already abed. Nicolaes, however, did not ride up to the front door; but, after he had crossed the bridge, he went straight on through one or two narrow streets which lay at the back of his home until he reached the corner of the Korte Gracht, which, again, abuts on the quay. Thus he had gone round in a semicircle, in obvious avoidance of the paternal house, and now he brought his horse to a halt outside a tall and narrow door which was surmounted by a lantern let into the wall. A painted sign which hung from an iron bracket above the door indicated to the passing wayfarer that the place was one where rider and horse could find food and shelter.

Nicolaes dismounted, and going up to the door, he knocked against it with the point of his foot. This he had to do several times before the welcome sound of someone moving inside the house came to his ear. A moment or two later the door was opened cautiously. A man appeared on the threshold, wrapped in a night-robe and still wearing a night-bonnet on his head.

===Chinese===
从科佩尔门望过去，码头上那座宅子看得清清楚楚：一片漆黑，足见阖宅上下都已上床就寝。尼古拉斯却没有骑向正门；过了桥，他径直穿过自家宅后那一两条窄巷，一直走到科尔特运河街（the Korte Gracht）的街口——那条街的一头也抵着码头。他就这样兜了个半圆，分明存心绕开父亲的宅子；此刻，他在一扇又高又窄的门前勒住了马，门楣上方的墙里嵌着一盏灯笼。门上铁支架垂挂下来的一块漆画招牌，向过路的行人标明：这里是个骑者与马匹都能觅得食宿的去处。

尼古拉斯下了马，走到门前，抬起脚尖踢门。他接连踢了好几下，才听见屋里有人走动的可喜声响。片刻之后，门被小心翼翼地打开了。一个男人出现在门槛上，裹着睡袍，头上还戴着睡帽。

===Original===
“Is that you, mynheer?” he queried drowsily.

“Who else should it be, you loon?” Nicolaes replied irritably. “Here’s your horse,” he added, and without waiting for further commend or protest from the unfortunate landlord thus roused from his slumbers, he proceeded to tether the animal by the reins to one of the iron rings in the wall.

“It is so late, mynheer,” the man protested dolefully; “and so cold. Will you not take the horse round to the stable yourself? It is but a step to the right, and there’s the gate—”

“It is late, as you say, and cold,” Nicolaes retorted curtly. “And when I paid you so liberally for the horse, I did not bargain to take service with you as ostler in the middle of the night.”

“But, mynheer—” urged the landlord, still protesting.

===Chinese===
「是老爷您吗？」他睡眼惺忪地问。

「不是我还是谁，你这蠢货？」尼古拉斯没好气地答道。「你的马在这儿，」他补了一句，不等这位被从睡梦中叫醒的倒霉店主再吩咐一声或抱怨一句，就自顾自把缰绳拴到了墙上的铁环上。

「这么晚了，老爷，」那人苦巴巴地抱怨道；「又这么冷。您就不能自己把马牵去马棚么？往右不过几步路，门就在那儿——」

「晚是晚了，也冷，」尼古拉斯三言两语地抢白道。「我出那么大的价钱买你这匹马的时候，可没讲好要半夜里给你当马夫。」

「可是，老爷——」店主还在央告。

===Original===
But Nicolaes did not listen. In faith, he had ceased to hear, for already he was striding rapidly down the Korte Gracht, and the next moment was back on the quay. A few steps brought him to the door of his father’s house. Here he paused for a moment ere he mounted the stone steps that led up to the massive front door, stamped his feet so as to shake the melted snow from his boots, and with a few quick touches tried to reestablish some semblance of order in his clothes. Indeed, when presently he rapped vigorously with the iron knocker against the door, he looked no longer like a wearied and querulous traveler, but rather like a man just returned from a short and pleasant ride.

To his astonishment it was Maria, his sister Gilda’s faithful tire-woman, who opened the door for him. She anticipated his very first query by a curt:

“Everyone is abed. The jongejuffrouw alone chose to wait for you, and I could not let her wait alone.”

===Chinese===
可尼古拉斯根本不听。说真的，他已经听不见了，人早已大步流星地顺着科尔特运河街走去，转眼又回到了码头上。几步路就到了他父亲宅邸的门前。登上那几级通向厚重正门的石阶之前，他站定了片刻，跺跺脚，抖掉靴子上的湿雪，又飞快地整了几把衣裳，想找回几分体统。果然，等他抡起铁门环砰砰敲门的当口，他那样子已不像个疲惫乖戾的旅人，倒像个骑马闲逛了一小趟、尽兴而归的人了。

给他开门的竟是玛丽亚（Maria）——他妹妹吉尔达那位忠心耿耿的梳妆侍女，这叫他吃了一惊。她抢在他开口之前，干巴巴地撂下一句：

「都睡下了。只有小姐（jongejuffrouw）一个人偏要等您，我总不能让她独自等着。」

===Original===
Nicolaes uttered an angry exclamation.

“Tell my sister to go to bed, too,” he commanded briefly. “I’ll go to my rooms at once, as it is so late.”

Maria made no audible reply. She mumbled something about “Shameful conduct!” and “Wedding-night!” But Nicolaes paid no heed, strode quickly across the hall, and ran swiftly up the stairs.

But on the landing he came abruptly to a halt. He had almost fallen against his sister Gilda, who stood there waiting for him.

Behind her, a little way down the passage, a door stood ajar, and through it there came a narrow fillet of light. At sight of him, and before he could utter a sound, she put a finger to her lip, then let the way along the passage. The door which stood ajar was the one which gave on her own room. She went in, and he followed her, his heart beating with something like shame or fear.

===Chinese===
尼古拉斯忿忿地骂了一声。

「叫妹妹也去睡，」他简短地吩咐道。「天这么晚了，我这就回自己房里去。」

玛丽亚没有出声回答。她嘴里咕哝了几句什么「不知羞」啦、「新婚之夜」啦。可尼古拉斯全不理会，大步穿过厅堂，蹬蹬蹬跑上了楼梯。

可到了楼梯口，他猛地收住脚。他险些撞在他妹妹吉尔达身上——她正站在那儿等他。

她身后不远处的走廊里，一扇门虚掩着，门缝里漏出一窄条灯光。一看见他，不等他出声，她就把一根手指抵在唇上，随即领路沿走廊走去。那扇虚掩着的门，通的是她自己的房间。她走进去，他也跟了进去，心口跳得发慌，不知是羞愧还是恐惧。

===Original===
“Hush!” she whispered, and gently closed the door behind him. “Make no noise! Kaatje has at last sobbed herself to sleep. She hath been put to bed in her mother’s room. ’Twere a shame to disturb her.” Then, as Nicolaes muttered something that sounded very like a curse, the girl added reproachfully: “Poor Kaatje! You have shown very little ardour toward her, Klaas.”

“I lost my way in the dark,” he answered. “I had no thought it could be so late.”

Just then the tower clock of St. Maria Kerk chimed the midnight hour.

Gilda hazarded timidly: “You should not have thought of accompanying my lord. He was ready to start out alone; and your place, Klaas, was beside your wife!”

===Chinese===
「嘘！」她低声说，轻轻带上了他身后的门。「别弄出声响！卡特耶总算哭着哭着睡着了。她被安置在她母亲房里睡下了。吵醒她，就作孽了。」尼古拉斯咕哝了一句听上去极像咒骂的话，姑娘便嗔怪地补上一句：「可怜的卡特耶！你待她可实在没什么热情，克拉斯（Klaas）。」

「我在黑地里迷了路，」他答道。「没料到会这么晚。」

正在这时，圣玛丽亚教堂（St. Maria Kerk）的钟楼当当敲响了午夜十二点。

吉尔达怯生生地试着开口：「你本就不该想着去陪我大人。他自己本来就要独自动身的；而你的位置，克拉斯，是在你妻子身边！」

===Original===
“Are you going to lecture me about my duty, Gilda?” he said irritably. “You must not think that because—”

“I think nothing,” she broke in simply, “save that Kaatje wept when the evening wore on and you did not return; and that the more she wept the greater was our father’s anger against you.”

“He knew that I meant to accompany your husband a part of the way,” Nicolaes retorted. “In truth, had he done me the justice to read my thoughts, he himself would have bade me go.”

“It was kind of you,” she rejoined somewhat coolly, “to be concerned as to my lord’s safety. But I can assure you—”

===Chinese===
「你是要教训我尽本分么，吉尔达？」他恼怒地说。「你千万别以为，因为——」

「我什么也不以为，」她干脆地打断道，「只知道天色越熬越晚、你迟迟不归，卡特耶哭了一场；而且她越哭，父亲对你的火气就越大。」

「他知道我本来就打算送你丈夫一程，」尼古拉斯回嘴道。「说真的，他若肯公平地体察我的心意，他自己就会打发我去。」

「多谢你的好意，」她答得有些冷淡，「这样惦记我大人的安危。不过我可以向你担保——」

===Original===
“ ’Twas not concern for *his* safety,” he broke in gruffly, “that caused me to accompany him tonight.”

“What then?”

But he gave no reply, but his lip and turned away from her, with the air of one who fears that he hath said too much and cares not to be questioned again.

“I’d best go now,” he said abruptly.

He looked around for his gloves, which he had thrown down upon the table. His manner seemed so strange that Gilda was suddenly conscious of a nameless kind of fear; the sort of premonition that comes to highly sensitive natures, at times when hitherto unsuspected danger suddenly looms upon the cloudless sky of life. She forced him to return her searching glance.

===Chinese===
「我今晚陪他同行，」他粗声大气地打断道，「可不是为了*他*的安危。」

「那是为了什么？」

他却不答话，只咬着嘴唇，从她面前扭过头去，那副神情，像是生怕自己说漏了嘴，再不愿被人追问。

「我最好这就走了，」他没头没脑地说。

他四下里找他的手套，那是他方才扔在桌上的。他这副样子实在古怪，吉尔达心里霎时升起一种无名的恐惧；那种预感，只有最敏于感应的心灵才会有——每当全然未曾疑心的危险，突然在万里无云的人生晴空上隐隐压来的时候。她直直地逼视着他，逼得他不能不回望她那搜问的目光。

===Original===
“You are hiding something from me, Klaas,” she said determinedly. “What is it?”

“I?” he riposted, feigning surprise. “Hiding something? Why should I have something to hide?”

“That I know not,” she replied. “But there was some hidden meaning in your words just now when you said that ’twas not concern for my lord’s safety that caused you to accompany him this night. What, then, was it?” she insisted, seeing that he remained silent, even though he met her gaze with a look that appeared both fearful and pitying.

She had her back to the door now, looked like some timid creature brought to bay by a cruel and hitherto unsuspected enemy.

“You must not ask me for my meaning, Gilda,” Nicolaes said at last. “There are things which concern men only, and with which women should have no part.”

===Chinese===
「你有事瞒着我，克拉斯，」她斩钉截铁地说。「是什么？」

「我？」他反问，装出一脸诧异。「瞒事？我有什么可瞒的？」

「那我不知，」她答道。「可你方才的话里藏着别的意思——你说你今晚陪他同行，不是为了我大人的安危。那是为了什么？」见他仍不作声——尽管他望着她的目光里既有惧意又有怜惜——她还是追问不放。

此刻她背抵着门，活像一只被残忍的、全然未曾防备的敌人逼到绝境的怯懦小兽。

「你别问我的意思，吉尔达，」尼古拉斯终于开了口。「有些事只与男人相干，女人家不该沾边。」

===Original===
His tone of ill-concealed compassion stung her like a cut from a whip across the face.

“There is nothing that concerns my lord,” she retorted proudly, “in which he would not desire me to bear my part.”

“Then let him tell you himself.”

“What?”

She threw the question at him like a challenge, stepped up to him and seized him by the wrist—no longer a timid creature at bay. But a strong, determined woman, who feels in some mysterious way that the man whom she loves is being attacked, and who is prepared, with every known and unknown weapon almighty love can suggest, to defend him, his life or his honour, or both.

===Chinese===
他那掩饰不住的怜惜口吻，刺得她像脸上挨了一鞭。

「凡是关涉我大人的事，」她傲然回敬道，「就没有一桩，是他不愿意让我分担的。」

「那就让他自己告诉你吧。」

「什么？」

她把这一问像下战书似的抛给他，抢上一步，一把攥住他的手腕——不再是困兽一般怯懦的小兽，而是一个刚强决绝的女人：她凭着某种神秘的直觉，感到她所爱的男人正在受人攻击，于是准备用万能的爱所能提示的一切明枪暗器，去保卫他——保卫他的性命、他的名誉，或者两者兼而有之。

===Original===
“You are not going out of this room, Klaas, until you have explained!” she said with unquestionable determination. “What is it that my lord should tell me himself?”

“Why he, newly wed and a stranger, was so determined on this, his wedding night, to carry the Stadtholder’s message across the Veluwe.”

Nicolaes spoke abruptly, almost fiercely now, as if wearied of this wrangling, and burdened with a secret he could no longer hold. But she did not at first understand his meaning.

“I do not understand what you mean,” she murmured vaguely, a perplexed frown between her eyes.

===Chinese===
「不说个明白，你就休想出这个房间，克拉斯！」她说得斩钉截铁、不容分说。「我大人该亲自告诉我的是什么？」

「为什么他——一个新婚的外乡人——偏要在今夜、他的洞房花烛之夜，执意抢着把执政的信送过费吕韦去。」

尼古拉斯说得突兀，此刻几乎是凶巴巴的了，仿佛已吵得不耐烦，又被一桩再也兜不住的秘密压得受不住了。可她起初并没有听懂他的意思。

「我不明白你的意思，」她茫然地低语，眉心蹙起一道困惑。

===Original===
“There were plenty there eager and willing to go,” Nicolaes went on roughly. “Nay, the errand was not in itself perilous. Speed was required, yes; and a sound knowledge of the country. But a dozen men at least who were in this house today know the Veluwe as well as this stranger, and any good horse would cover the ground fast enough. But he wanted to go—he, this man whom none of us know, who was married this day, and whose bride had the first call on his attention. He insisted with the Stadtholder, and he went—And I went with him; would have gone all the way if he had not forced me to go back. Why did he wish to go, Gilda? Why did he leave you deliberately this night? Think! Think! And why did he insist on going alone, with not even one of those besotted boon companions of his to share in his adventure? A message to Marquet—my God!” he added with a sneer. “A message to the Archduchess, more like, to cross the Ijssel ere it be too late!”

“You devil!”

===Chinese===
「今天在这宅子里的，多少人巴不得去、抢着去，」尼古拉斯粗声粗气地说下去。「不，这趟差事本身并不凶险。要的是快，不错；还要熟悉那一带的地理。可今天在这座宅子里的，少说也有一打人，跟这个外乡人一样认得费吕韦，随便一匹好马，都尽够把这段路赶下来。可他偏要去——他，这个咱们谁都不识底细的人，今天刚成亲，新娘本是他头一个该照应的人。他跟执政执意讨下了这差事，他就去了——我也跟了去；要不是他硬逼我回头，我本来会送他到底。他为什么偏要去，吉尔达？他为什么存心在今夜撇下你？想想！好好想想！还有，他为什么执意独个儿动身，连他那两个痴痴迷迷的酒友，也不带一个去搭伙？给马凯送信——我的老天！」他冷笑一声，加了一句。「倒更像是给大公夫人报信，叫她趁还来得及，赶紧渡过艾瑟尔河！」

「你这恶魔！」

===Original===
She hissed out the words through set lips and teeth clenched in an access of fierce and overwhelming passion. And before he could recover himself, before he could guess her purpose, she had seized his heavy, leathern gloves, which were lying on the table, and struck him with them full in the face. He staggered, and put his hand up to his eyes.

“Go!” she commanded briefly.

He tried to laugh the situation off, said almost flippantly:

“I’ll punish you for this, you young vixen!”

But she did not move, and her glance seemed to freeze the words upon his lips.

“Go!” she commanded once more.

He shrugged his shoulders.

===Chinese===
她从咬紧的唇齿间嘶声挤出这几个字，一股凶猛得无法遏制的激情贯满了全身。不等他回过神来，不等他猜出她的用意，她已经抓起桌上他那副沉甸甸的皮手套，兜脸抽了他一记。他踉跄了一下，抬手捂住了眼睛。

「走！」她简短地喝令。

他想把这一幕嘻嘻哈哈地揭过去，近乎轻佻地说：

「这笔账我定要跟你算，你这小泼妇！」

可她一动不动，她的目光仿佛把那几个字冻在了他唇上。

「走！」她又喝令一声。

他耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“I understand your indignation, Gilda. Nay, I honour it. But remember my warning! Your stranger lord,” he went on with slow and deliberate emphasis, “will be returning anon to the Stadtholder’s camp, a courted and honoured man; but ’tis the armies of the Archduchess who will have crossed the Ijssel by then, whilst the orders to Marquet will have reached that commander too late.”

Then he turned on his heel and went out of the room, and anon Gilda heard his footstep resounding along the passage. She listened until she heard the opening and closing of a distant door, after which she sighed and murmured, “Poor Kaatje!” That was all; but there was a world of meaning in the sorrowful compassion wherewith she said those words.

Then she raised her left hand, round the third finger of which glittered a plain gold ring. The ring she pressed long and lingeringly against her lips, and in her heart she prayed, “God guard you, my dear lord!”

===Chinese===
「你的义愤，我明白，吉尔达。不，我敬重它。可记住我的警告！你那位外乡人大人，」他一字一顿、慢慢地说下去，「不日就将荣归执政的营帐，成了人人趋奉、人人敬重的红人；可到那时，渡过艾瑟尔河的，将是大公夫人的大军，而送给马凯的军令到了那位将军手里，已经太迟了。」

说完，他一转身出了房间；不多时，吉尔达就听见他的脚步声在走廊里咚咚作响。她一直听到远处一扇门开而又关，才叹了口气，喃喃道：「可怜的卡特耶！」就这一句；可她吐出这几个字时那一腔哀怜，藏着千言万语。

然后她抬起左手，无名指上一枚素金戒指微微闪光。她把那戒指久久地、迟迟地贴在唇上，心里默默祈祷：「上帝保佑你，我亲爱的大人！」

## V A Race for Life / V 生死竞逐

### I / 一

===Original===
As for Diogenes, he reached Zutphen in the small hours of the morning, and after a few hours’ rest pushed on to Vorden at dawn. He himself would have deprecated any suggestion of making of this journey across the Veluwe a romantic adventure. The upland, under its covering of snow, held neither terrors nor secrets for him. The wind, the stars, an unerring instinct and sound knowledge of the scarce visible tracks, guided him across the arid waste. A real child of the open, he had less difficulty in finding his way across such a Godforsaken wild than he would through the intricate streets of a city.

Messire Marquet, encamped outside Vorden, welcomed the Stadtholder’s messenger effusively. His troops, for the most part composed of mercenaries from Germany, were getting restive in idleness; once or twice they had used threats when demanding their pay. Diogenes, bringing both money and the prospect of a fight, was doubly welcome. His stay at the camp was brief. By late morning he was once more on his way, with the intention of re-crossing the Ijssel at Dieren and of reaching Wageningen before dark. He had but half a dozen leagues to cover, and eight hours of daylight wherein to do it. Weather, too, and circumstances favored him. The thaw, which had been so completely vanquished upon the upland, had remained sole monarch in the plain. The air was mild and intensely humid. A dense sea-fog lay over the river and the surrounding marshes. The numerous little tributaries of the Ijssel and the intervening canals and ditches were already free from ice, and as Diogenes put his horse to an easy gallop in the direction of the river, the animal sank fetlock deep in mud.

The road was solitary, and, as far as the eye could reach through the mist, seemed entirely deserted. The countryside here had the desolate appearance peculiar to districts that have been fought over. The few thatched cottages, which from time to time loomed out of the mist, still bore the marks of passing fire and sword; the trees were truncated and sparse, the marshland was riddled with the scars of ceaseless tramping of men, of wagons, and of beasts. The inevitable windmills, gaunt-looking and ghostlike through the humid atmosphere, appeared neglected and forlorn.

===Chinese===
至于第欧根尼（Diogenes），他凌晨时分抵达了聚特芬（Zutphen），歇了几个钟头，天一亮便继续赶路，直奔沃尔登（Vorden）。倘若有人要把这趟横穿费吕韦（the Veluwe）的旅程渲染成一场浪漫的冒险，他自己头一个不以为然。积雪覆盖的高地，对他既无恐怖，也无秘密。风、星辰、万无一失的本能，再加上对那些依稀难辨的小径烂熟于胸，指引着他越过这片干旱的荒原。他是真正在旷野里长大的孩子，穿过这种天弃地绝的荒野，反倒比穿行城市里纵横交错的街巷还要省力。

驻营在沃尔登城外的马凯老爷（Messire Marquet），热情洋溢地欢迎了执政（Stadtholder）的信使。他的部队大半由德意志来的雇佣兵组成，闲得发慌，渐生躁动；讨要军饷时，已有一两回出言恫吓了。第欧根尼既带来了饷银，又带来了打仗的前景，自然受到双倍的欢迎。他在营中盘桓不久，近午时分便再度上路，打算在迪伦（Dieren）重渡艾瑟尔河（the Ijssel），赶在天黑之前抵达瓦赫宁恩（Wageningen）。他只剩六里格的路程，却有八个钟头的白昼可资利用。天气与情势也都有利于他。在高地上早被彻底击溃的解冻暖潮，到了平原上却依旧是唯一的君主。空气温和，潮润无比。浓重的海雾笼罩着河流与四周的沼泽。艾瑟尔河众多细小的支流，连同其间纵横的沟渠水道，都已冰消雪化；第欧根尼催马朝河边轻驰而去，那牲口便陷进泥泞，深及球节。

道路上冷冷清清，透过雾气极目所及，仿佛全然无人。这一带的乡野，有着兵燹之地特有的荒凉景象。零星几座茅草小屋不时从雾中隐现，仍带着战火与刀兵过境的痕迹；树木被斫得残缺稀疏，沼泽地上满布疮痍，那是人、车辆与牲畜无休无止的踩踏留下的累累创痕。那几座少不了的风车，在湿漉漉的水汽里形容枯瘦、状若鬼影，显得破败而凄凉。

===Original===
But the solitary rider had no eyes for landscape just now. He could have wished for a clearer day, for it was impossible even for his keen eyes to see what was going on behind that impenetrable wall of fog. If Pythagoras’ ears had not played him false, De Berg was there, not very far away, waiting to cross the Ijssel when opportunity arose.

Thanks to that faithful hypertrophied loon, the ambitious designs of the Archduchess could still be frustrated. De Berg’s armies were still on the right bank of the Ijssel, and if Marquet got his men on the move by midday, as he had promised he would do, the crossing of the enemy troops would become difficult, mayhap impossible.

These were pleasing thoughts for the man on whose speed and resource these important plans depended. All that he chafed against was the imperative slowness of his progress, as the mist enveloped him more closely the nearer he got to the river. But withal it protected him, too, hid him mayhap from the prying eyes of vedettes on the watch. Already, judging by certain landmarks that met him on the way, Brummen was half a league behind him on his right, Hengles far away on the left, and Dieren not more than another league on ahead. For the last quarter of an hour he had heard from time to time the heavy booming sound, akin to the reverberation of distant cannonade, which came from the breaking and cracking of the ice as it drifted downstream. He put his horse to slow trot, as he pried through the mist for the first indication of a shortcut he knew of, which would take him to the river bank in less than half an hour.

===Chinese===
可是这位孤身赶路的人，此刻哪有心思看风景。他巴不得天能更晴朗些，因为即便是他锐利的眼睛，也看不透那堵密不透风的雾墙后面正发生着什么。倘若毕达哥拉斯（Pythagoras）的耳朵没有听错，德·贝格（De Berg）就在那里，离此不远，只等时机一到便渡艾瑟尔河。

多亏那个忠心耿耿、高大得过分的活宝，大公夫人（the Archduchess）的勃勃图谋如今仍有可能被挫败。德·贝格的大军还在艾瑟尔河右岸，只要马凯如他允诺的那样在正午开拔，敌军渡河便会困难重重，说不定根本无法得逞。

这些念头令他心怀喜悦——这几桩重大的计划，全系于他的脚程与急智。他唯一焦躁的，是不得已的迟缓：越近河边，雾气把他裹得越紧。但这雾也在护着他，兴许还把他掩了起来，躲过守望哨探窥伺的眼睛。从沿途遇见的几处地标判断，布吕门（Brummen）已在他右后方半里格，亨格尔斯（Hengles）远在左手边，迪伦则就在前方不出一里格处。这一刻钟来，他不时听见一种沉重的隆隆声，宛如远处炮轰的回响——那是浮冰顺流漂下时迸裂碎开的声响。他放缓马步，徐徐小跑，一面透过雾气细察，搜寻他熟知的那条近道的第一丝迹象——走那条道，不出半个钟头就能把他带到河岸。


### II / 二

===Original===
The next moment he had spied the narrow track and set his horse to follow it; when suddenly, out of the mist, there came a loud report, and Diogenes heard the whistle of a bullet close to his ear. It almost grazed his shoulder. Without an instant’s pause, without turning to look whence had come this unexpected greeting, he set spurs to his horse and galloped at breakneck speed toward the river. Over fields and ditches; no thought of prudence now, only of speed! Mud and water flew out in all directions under the horse’s frantic gallopade, the plucky beast sinking at times almost to his knees in the marshy ground. A few minutes later—five, perhaps—Diogenes heard the sound of many hoofs behind him, obviously in pursuit. He turned to look this time, and through the mist vaguely discerned some three or four cavaliers, who were distant from him then less than two hundred yards. So far, so good! The Ijssel was close by now, and if, when he reached the banks, he turned off in the direction of the stream, he could easily reach the ford on this side of Brummen and get across—on foot, if need be, if his horse proved an obstacle to rapid progress.

A few more minutes now and the river was in sight, with, far away on the opposite bank, Brummen, nestling at the foot of the rising ground, the gate of the Veluwe. With renewed vigor the rider sped along, his blood whipped up by the chase, his whole body exhilarated by this sensation of danger and of one of those sportive races for life for which three months of idleness and luxury had given him a hitherto unsuspected longing.

Ah, there was the shore at last, the group of three windmills close to the bank, an unmistakable landmark. Here, too, within two hundred paces on ahead, was the ford, which no amount of drifting ice would cause the daring adventurer to miss. Already he was within a few yards of the low-lying bank, searching the approach to the ford with eyes now doubly keen, when, with staggering suddenness, another cavalier appeared, straight in front of him this time, and barring the way to the river-brink.

===Chinese===
下一刻，他已认出那条窄径，拨马要循它而去；不料雾中突然传来一声爆响，第欧根尼听见一颗子弹贴着耳边呼啸而过，险些擦着他的肩膀。他片刻不停，也不回头看这份突如其来的「见面礼」来自何方，只把马刺一夹，朝河边没命地飞驰。越过田野，越过沟渠；此刻顾不得谨慎，只顾得上速度！马儿疯狂驰骋，泥水四下飞溅，这匹有胆气的牲口时而陷进沼泽地里，几乎没到膝弯。几分钟之后——大约五分钟吧——第欧根尼听见身后蹄声杂沓，显然是追兵来了。这回他回头望了一眼，透过雾气依稀辨出三四名骑兵，离他已不到两百码。到目前为止，还算顺利！艾瑟尔河此刻已在近旁，只要到了岸边，他朝顺流的方向一拐，就能轻易赶到布吕门这一侧的渡口渡河——必要时不惜弃马徒步，倘若那畜生反倒拖累脚程。

又过了几分钟，河已望得见了；远远的对岸，布吕门偎依在高地的坡脚下——那是费吕韦的门户。骑手重又抖擞精神，一路疾驰，追逐撩得他热血沸腾；这危险的刺激，这带几分游戏兴味的生死竞逐，令他通体振奋——三个月的闲散与安逸，竟让他对此生出了自己都未曾察觉的渴望。

啊，河岸终于到了，紧挨河岸的那一组三座风车，是错不了的地标。前方不出两百步，便是渡口；无论漂来多少浮冰，也断不能叫这位大胆的冒险家错过它。他离那低平的河岸只剩几码，正以加倍敏锐的目光搜寻通往渡口的路径，冷不防，又一名骑兵冒了出来——这回是正对着他，拦住了通往河沿的去路。

===Original===
No time to note his face; just a second wherein to decide what had best be done, not only to save his own life, but also the message which he must carry to Wageningen, at whatever cost. Then the cavalier turned for one brief second in his saddle, to call to some companions as yet unseen. A brief second, did I say? ’Twas but a fraction. The next moment Diogenes had whipped out a pistol from his saddlebow, and with a steady hand fired at his foe. The cavalier reeled in his saddle and fell, just as half a dozen others issued with a shout from out the mist, and those in pursuit put fresh spurs to their mounts.

It had been madness to attempt the ford now. The young soldier, sore-pressed, might in truth have sold his life dearly, but with it, too, he would have sold Nijmegen and the possible success of the Stadtholder’s plans. Ofttimes before, in the course of his adventurous life, he had been in as tight a place, where life and death hung quite evenly in the scales of Fate; but never before had he been quite so anxious to flee. He could not trust the valor of his sword, his own well-nigh unexampled skill in a fight against odds that would have made the bravest pause. No! It meant running away, away as fast as his horse would take him, and faster if the poor brute gave out. A short gallop along the bank, the cavalier behind him warming to the pursuit; keeping closer and closer to the low-lying bank, till the horse began to flounder in what was sheer morass.

The ford now lay well behind him. The waters of the Ijssel, tossed for awhile upon the shallows, flowed with increased swiftness here. Huge ice-blocks floated seaward upon the heaving bosom of the stream. The foremost of the pursuing cavaliers was then less than fifty yards behind, and more than one bullet had whizzed past the fleeing rider, one of them piercing his hat, the other grazing his thigh, but none doing him serious injury. Already the rallying cry of the pursuers had turned to one of triumph as the distance lessened between them and their quarry, when, with a sudden jerk of the reins, Diogenes plunged headlong into the river.

===Chinese===
