For one fraction of a second the gleam of steel flashed in the humid air; then, without a word of warning, swift as a hawk descending on his prey, he struck at Diogenes with all his might.

It had all happened in a very few brief seconds. Diogenes, spent with fatigue, or actually struck, staggered and half fell against the bottom step. But Gilda, with a loud cry, was already by his side, and as Nicolaes raised his arm to strike once again, she was on him like some lithe pantheress.

===Chinese===
「滚开，你这蠢货！！」对方大声呵叱，正要推开他闯过去，尼古拉斯却猛然把裹在斗篷里的手臂抽了出来。

刹那之间，一道钢光在潮湿的空气里一闪；随即，没有半句警告，他饿鹰扑食一般，倾尽全力朝第欧根尼击了下去。

这一切不过发生在短短几秒钟之内。第欧根尼或是疲竭难支，或是实实在在挨了那一击，踉踉跄跄，半个身子跌在最低一级台阶上。可吉尔达一声高呼，已然扑到他身旁；尼古拉斯再度扬臂欲击时，她已像一头矫捷的母豹，扑到了尼古拉斯身上。

===Original===
She seized his wrist, and gave it such a violent twist that he uttered a cry of pain, and the dagger fell with a clatter to the ground. After which everything became a blur. She heard her brother’s loudly triumphant shout:

“His Highness’s life was threatened. Mine was but an act of justice!” even as he in his turn swung himself into the saddle.

===Chinese===
她攥住他的手腕，狠狠一拧，他痛得叫出声来，匕首当啷一声跌在地上。此后的一切便成了一片模糊。她听见哥哥那声得意扬扬的厉呼：

「殿下的性命受到了威胁。我那一击，不过是一桩正义之举！」话犹未落，他也翻身跨上了马鞍。


### V / 五

===Original===
The Stadtholder looked dazed. It had all happened so quickly that he had not the time to visualize it all. De Voocht, who was in the hall of the burgomaster’s house from the moment when the Stadtholder bade farewell to Gilda until that when he dug his spurs into his horse and scattered the crowd in every direction, tells us in his “Brieven”—the one which is dated March 21, 1626—that the incidents followed on one another with such astounding rapidity that it was impossible for anyone to interfere.

All that he remembers very clearly is seeing his Highness getting to horse, then the flash of steel in the air and Nicolaes Beresteyn’s arm upraised ready to strike. He could not see if anyone had fallen. The next moment he heard Gilda’s heart-piercing shriek, and saw her running down the stone steps—almost flying, like a bird.

Mynheer Beresteyn followed his daughter as rapidly as he could. He reached the foot of the steps just as his son put his horse to a walk in the wake of his Highness. He was wont to say afterwards that at the moment his mind was an absolute blank. He had heard his daughter’s cry and seen Nicolaes strike; but he had not actually seen Diogenes. Now he was just in time to see his son’s final dramatic gesture and to hear his parting words:

===Chinese===
执政显得茫然失神。这一切来得太快，他根本来不及把全部情形收进眼里。德·福赫特（De Voocht）——从执政向吉尔达道别那一刻起，直到他马刺一夹、把人群驱散四方那一刻止，此人一直在市长宅邸的大厅里——在他的《书简》（Brieven）里告诉我们——就是注了 1626 年 3 月 21 日日期的那一件——诸事接踵而至，迅疾得骇人，任谁也来不及插手。

他记得真真切切的只有这些：看见殿下上马，随后半空里钢光一闪，尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦扬臂欲击。他没有看见可有人倒下。下一刻，他听见吉尔达那声穿心裂肺的尖叫，看见她奔下石阶——简直是在飞，像一只鸟。

贝雷斯坦老爷尽快随着女儿追了下来。他赶到台阶脚下时，他的儿子正催马徐行，跟在殿下身后。他后来惯常说，那一刻他脑中是一片全然的空白。他听见了女儿的尖叫，也看见了尼古拉斯出手击人；可他并没有真正看见第欧根尼。此刻他来得恰是时候，正赶上儿子那最后一记戏剧性的手势，听见了他临去的话：

===Original===
“There, father,” Nicolaes shouted to him, and pointed to the ground, “is the pistol which the miscreant pointed at the Stadtholder when I struck him down like a dog!”

The people down on the quay had hardly perceived anything. They were too deeply engrossed in their own troubles and deadly peril.

When the horses reared under the spur they scattered like so many hens out of the way of immediate danger; but a second or two later they were once more surging everywhere, intent only on the business of getting away.

===Chinese===
「喏，父亲，」尼古拉斯朝他喊道，一面指着地上，「这就是那恶徒瞄准执政的手枪，我就像打一条狗似的把他打倒了！」

下面码头上的人们几乎什么也没有瞧见。他们正深深陷在自家的苦难与致命的危局里，无暇旁顾。

马匹在马刺下人立而起时，他们便如一群躲祸的母鸡，四散避开眼前的凶险；可一两秒之后，人潮又四处翻涌起来，一心一意只在逃命这一桩事上。

===Original===
Gilda, at the foot of the steps, saw and heard nothing more. The sudden access of almost manlike strength wherewith she had fallen on her brother and wrenched the murderous dagger from his grasp had as suddenly fallen from her again. Her knees were shaking; she was almost ready to swoon.

She put out her arms and encountered those of her father, which gave her support. Her brother’s voice, exultant and cruel, reached her ears as through a veil.

“My lord!” she murmured, in a pitiful appeal.

===Chinese===
吉尔达站在台阶脚下，其余的什么也看不见、听不见了。她方才扑向哥哥、从他手里生生夺下那把凶器时迸发出的那股近乎男子汉的膂力，又同样突兀地弃她而去。她双膝颤抖，几乎便要晕厥。

她伸出双臂，正遇上父亲的手臂，将她扶住了。哥哥的声音——得意而残忍——恍如隔着一层纱幕传进她耳中。

「大人！」她喃喃地唤道，一声凄楚的哀恳。

===Original===
She did not know how severely he had been struck; indeed, she had not seen him fall. Her instinct had been to rush on Nicolaes first and to disarm him. In this she succeeded. Then only did she turn to her beloved.

But the crowd, cruelly indifferent, was all around like a surging sea. They pushed and they jostled; they shouted and filled the air with a medley of sounds. Some actually laughed. She saw some comely faces and ugly ones; some that wept and others that grinned. It seemed to her even for a moment that she caught sight of a round red face and of lean and lanky Socrates. She tried to call to him, to beg him to explain. She turned to her father, asking him if in truth she was going mad.

For she called in vain to her beloved. He was no longer here.

===Chinese===
她不知道他被打得有多重；其实，她并没有看见他倒下。她的本能，是先扑向尼古拉斯，先缴他的械。这一点她做到了。然后她才转过身来，向着她的爱人。

可人群残酷地漠然着，四面向她围拢，如一片翻涌的怒海。他们推着，挤着；嚷着，把空气塞满嘈杂的声浪。有些人竟还在笑。她看见一些俊秀的脸和一些丑陋的脸；有的在哭，有的在咧着嘴。有一瞬间，她甚至觉得自己瞥见了一张红扑扑的圆脸，还有那瘦长条的苏格拉底（Socrates）。她想唤他，求他把这一切说个明白。她转向父亲，问他自己是不是当真要疯了。

因为她向爱人的呼唤，终归徒然。他已经不在这里了。

## VIII Devil’s-Writ / VIII 魔鬼手书

### I / 一

===Original===
When Diogenes, taken wholly unawares by Nicolaes’ treacherous blow, had momentarily lost his balance, he would have been in a precarious position indeed had not his faithful friends been close at hand at the moment.

It is difficult to surmise how terribly anxious the two philosophers had been these past few days. Indeed, their anxiety had proved more than a counterpart to that felt by Gilda, and had, with its simple-hearted sympathy, expressed in language more whimsical than choice, been intensely comforting to her.

Both these worthies had been inured to blows and hurts from the time when as mere lads, they first learned to handle a sword, and Pythagoras’ wound, which would have laid an ordinary man low for a fortnight, was, after four days, already on the mend. To keep a man of that type in bed, or even within four walls, when he began to feel better was more than anyone could do. And when he understood that Diogenes had been absent four days on an errand for the Stadtholder, that the jongejuffrouw was devoured with anxiety on his behalf, and that that spindle-legged gossoon Socrates was spending most of the day and one half of the night on horseback, patrolling the ramparts watching for the comrade’s return; when he understood all that, I say, it was not likely that he—Pythagoras—an able-bodied man and a doughty horseman at that, would be content to lie abed and be physicked by any grovelling leech.

Thus the pair of them were providentially on the watch on that memorable March 21, and they both saw their comrade-in-arms enter the city by the Joris Poort. They followed him as best they could through the crowd, cursing and pushing their way, knowing well that Diogenes’ objective could be none other than a certain house they wot of on the quay, where a lovely jongejuffrouw was waiting in tears for her beloved.

===Chinese===
第欧根尼（Diogenes）被尼古拉斯（Nicolaes）那记阴险的暗算打得猝不及防，一时失了平衡——倘若不是他那些忠实的朋友恰在此时近在咫尺，他的处境可当真要凶险万分了。

这两位哲人过去几天里究竟焦灼到何等地步，实在难以悬想。说真的，他们的焦灼比起吉尔达（Gilda）的有过之而无不及；而那一份憨直的同情——言辞或许俚俗多过雅驯——却给了她莫大的慰藉。

这两位好汉自少年初学用剑之时起，便挨惯了刀伤拳脚；毕达哥拉斯（Pythagoras）的那处伤，换了寻常人非得躺倒半个月不可，才过了四天便已见好。这等人一旦觉得松快了，还要把他按在床上——或者哪怕只是关在四面墙里——谁也办不到。待他听说第欧根尼为执政（Stadtholder）当差已经外出四天，那位小姐（jongejuffrouw）为他忧心如焚，而苏格拉底（Socrates）——那个细脚伶仃的小子——白天大半光阴外加半个夜晚都骑着马在城墙上巡行，守望伙伴归来；待他把这一切都弄明白了，我说，他毕达哥拉斯——一条精壮汉子，又是一名剽悍的骑手——岂肯安分躺在床上，由着哪个卑躬屈膝的郎中摆布。

就这样，天可怜见，在那个难忘的 3 月 21 日，他们两人正好守望着，双双望见他们那位战友从圣约里斯门（the Joris Poort）进了城。他们在人丛里连骂带挤，竭力跟在他身后——他们心里雪亮，第欧根尼此行的去处，除了码头边那座他们熟知的宅子，断不会是别处：一位美丽的小姐正在那里含泪等候她的心上人。

===Original===
Remember that to these two caitiffs the fact that the Spaniards were said to be at the very gates of Amersfoort was but a mere incident. With their comrade within the city, they feared nothing, were prepared for anything. They had been in far worse plights than this many a time in their career, the three of them, and had been none the worse for it in the end.

Of course, now matters had become more complicated through the jongejuffrouw. She had become the first consideration, and though it was impossible not to swear at Diogenes for thus having laid this burden on them all, it was equally impossible to shirk its responsibilities.

The jongejuffrouw above all. That had become the moral code of these two philosophers, and with those confounded Spaniards likely to descend on this town—why, the jongejuffrouw must be got out of it as soon as may be! No wonder that Diogenes had turned up just in the nick of time! Something evidently was in the wind, and it behooved for comrades-in-arms to be there, ready to help as occasion arose.

===Chinese===
要知道，对这两个泼皮来说，据说西班牙人已打到阿默斯福特（Amersfoort）城门口这件事，不过是一桩小小的枝节。伙伴既在城里，他们便什么都不怕，什么都有备。他们仨闯荡江湖，比这凶险得多的大风大浪不知经过多少回，到头来都毫发无伤。

当然，如今添了这位小姐，事情便复杂多了。她成了头一件要掂量的事；尽管免不得要咒骂第欧根尼，怪他把这副担子一古脑儿压在大家肩上，可要推卸这份责任，却同样是万万不能。

小姐的事高于一切——这已成了两位哲人的道德律。眼见那些该死的西班牙人说不定就要扑到这座城来——哎呀，得赶紧把小姐送出城去才好！怪不得第欧根尼来得正是时候！分明有什么事情正在酝酿，做战友的理当守在近旁，一有风吹草动便随时搭手。

===Original===
A simple code enough, you’ll admit; worthy of simple, unsophisticated hearts. Socrates, being the more able-bodied of the two, then took command, dismounted, and left his lubberly compeer in charge of the horses at a comparatively secluded corner of the marketplace.

“If you can get hold of one more horse,” he said airily, “one that is well-saddled and looks sprightly and fresh, do not let your super-sensitive honesty stand in your way. Diogenes’ mount looked absolutely spent, and I’m sure he’ll need another.”

With which parting admonition he turned on his heel and made his way toward the quay.

===Chinese===
你得承认，这条守则简单得很，配得上两颗质朴无华的心。苏格拉底是两人中更精壮的一个，当下便接管了指挥：他翻身下马，把那个笨手笨脚的同伴安顿在集市广场一处相对僻静的角落，叫他看管马匹。

「要是你还能再弄来一匹马，」他轻描淡写地说，「要鞍辔齐全、瞧着精神饱满的，可别让你那过分娇嫩的诚实碍了手脚。第欧根尼那匹坐骑看模样已经累散了架，我敢说他准得再要一匹。」

丢下这句临别告诫，他一转身，径自朝码头去了。

### II / 二

===Original===
Thus it was that Socrates happened to be on the spot, or very near it, when Diogenes was struck by the hand of a traitor, and, wearied, sick, and faint, lost his footing and fell for a moment helpless against the steps, whilst Nicolaes Beresteyn dug his spurs into his horse’s sides and urged the Stadtholder to immediate haste.

A second or two later these two were lost to sight in the crowd. It was Socrates who received his half-swooning friend in his arms, and who dragged him incontinently into the recess formed by the side of the stone steps and the wall of the burgomaster’s house.

By great good fortune, the dagger-thrust aimed by the abominable miscreant had lost most of its virulence in the thick folds of Diogenes’ cloak. The result was just a flesh wound in the neck, nothing that would cause so hardened a soldier more than slight discomfort. His scarf, tied tightly around his shoulders by Socrates’ rough, but experienced hands, was all that was needed for the moment. It had only been fatigue, and perhaps the unexpectedness of the onslaught, that had brought him to his knees for that brief second, and rendered him momentarily helpless. Time enough, by mischance for Nicolaes to drag the Stadtholder finally out of sight.

But by the time Diogenes’ faithful comrade had found shelter for him in the angle of the wall the feeling of sickness had passed away.

===Chinese===
事有凑巧，当第欧根尼挨了叛徒那一下毒手、又疲又病又眩晕、立足不稳有一霎瘫软在台阶上时，苏格拉底恰好在场，或者说近在场边；而尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦（Nicolaes Beresteyn）正把马刺死命往马腹里磕，催着执政火速起行。

一两秒之后，那两骑便没入了人丛。是苏格拉底张开双臂，接住了他那半昏迷的朋友，随即手忙脚乱地把他拖进石阶侧沿与市长宅邸墙面之间形成的凹角里。

算是老天保佑，那个十恶不赦的恶徒刺出的这一匕首，力道大半消解在第欧根尼斗篷的厚褶里。结果只是颈上一道皮肉之伤，对这般身经百战的军人来说，不过是些微的不适而已。苏格拉底那双粗鲁却老练的手把他的围巾在肩头紧紧一扎，眼下也就尽够了。方才教他跪倒那一瞬、教他片刻动弹不得的，不过是疲惫，兴许还有那番偷袭来得猝不及防。然而不幸，这一霎的工夫已尽够尼古拉斯把执政彻底拖出众人的视线。

可是，等第欧根尼这位忠实的伙伴把他安顿到墙角里时，那阵眩晕已经过去了。

===Original===
“The Stadtholder,” he queried abruptly, “where is he?”

“Gone!” Socrates grunted through clenched teeth. “Gone, together with that spawn of the devil who—”

“After him!” Diogenes commanded, speaking once more with that perfect quietude which is the attribute of men of action at moments of acute peril. “Get me a horse, man! Mine is spent.”

“In the marketplace,” Socrates responded laconically. “Pythagoras is in charge. You can have the beast, and we’ll follow.” Then he added, under his breath: “And the jongejuffrouw? She was so anxious—”

Diogenes made no reply, gave one look up at the house which contained all that for him was dearest on God’s earth. But he did not sigh. I think the longing and the disappointment were too keen even for that. The next moment he had already started to push his way through the throng along the quay, and thence into Vriese Straat in the direction of the marketplace, closely followed by his long-legged familiar.

===Chinese===
「执政，」他劈头便问，「人呢？」

「走了！」苏格拉底咬着牙咕哝道。「走了，跟那个魔鬼的崽子一道，他——」

「追上去！」第欧根尼喝令道，说话间又恢复了那种全然的镇静——那是实干家在危殆关头独有的气度。「给我弄匹马来，伙计！我那匹不中用了。」

「在集市广场上，」苏格拉底言简意赅地答道。「毕达哥拉斯看着呢。那牲口你只管骑去，我们随后就来。」接着他又压低嗓音添了一句：「那小姐呢？她方才急成那样——」

第欧根尼没有作声，只抬头朝那座宅子望了一眼——那里面装着他在人世间最珍爱的一切。可他没有叹息。我想，那份渴望与失望太过尖锐，连叹息都容不下了。下一刻，他已经动身挤过码头边的人丛，再折入弗里塞街（Vriese Straat）朝集市广场而去，身后紧紧跟着他那位长腿的亲随。

===Original===
As soon as the Groote Market lay open before him, his sharp eyes searched the crowd for a sight of the Stadtholder’s plumed bonnet. Soon he spied his Highness right across the place, with Nicolaes riding close to his stirrup.

The two horsemen were then tending toward Joris Laan, which leads straight to the *poort*.

At that end of the *markt* the crowd was much less dense, and Joris Laan beyond appeared practically deserted. It was, you must remember, from that side that the enemy would descend upon the city when he came, and the moving throng, if viewed from a height, would now have looked like a column of smoke when it is all blown one way by the wind. Already the Stadtholder and Nicolaes had been free to put their horses to a trot. Another moment and they would be galloping down Joris Laan, which is but three hundred yards from the *poort*.

===Chinese===
大市场（Groote Market）一在眼前铺展开来，他锐利的目光便在人群中搜寻执政那顶插羽的帽子。不多时，他便望见殿下就在广场那一头，尼古拉斯紧贴着他的马镫并辔而行。

两骑这时正奔着约里斯大道（Joris Laan）去——那条道笔直通向城门（poort）。

在市场（markt）的那一端，人群稀疏得多了，再往外的约里斯大道上更是几乎空无一人。你须记得，敌人进犯时正是要从那一头扑向城来；此刻那移动着的人潮，若从高处望去，活像一柱被风整个儿吹向一边的炊烟。执政和尼古拉斯早已能够放开马小跑起来。再过片刻，他们就要顺着约里斯大道纵马飞驰了——那条道离城门不过三百码。

===Original===
“Oh, God, grant me wings!” Diogenes muttered, between his teeth.

“What are you going to do?” Socrates asked.

“Prevent the Stadtholder from falling into an abominable trap, if I can,” the other replied briefly.

Socrates pointed to the distant corner of the *markt*, where Pythagoras could be dimly perceived waiting patiently beside three horses.

“I see the ruffian has stolen a horse,” he said. “So long as it is a fresh one—”

“I shall need it.” Diogenes remarked simply.

“I told him only to get the best, but you can’t trust that loon since good fortune hath made him honest.”

===Chinese===
「哦，上帝，赐我一双翅膀吧！」第欧根尼咬着牙喃喃道。

「你打算怎么办？」苏格拉底问。

「若是办得到，别让执政掉进一个可憎的陷阱。」对方简短地答道。

苏格拉底指了指市场远处的那个角落，隐约可见毕达哥拉斯守在三匹马旁，正耐心候着。

「我看这恶棍倒偷来了一匹马，」他说。「只要是匹脚力新鲜的就好——」

「我正用得着它。」第欧根尼淡淡说了一句。

「我只嘱咐他只管挑顶好的弄来，可自打好运道叫他改邪归正，这活宝反倒靠不住了。」

===Original===
The next few seconds brought them to the spot. Pythagoras hailed them with delight. He was getting tired of waiting. Three horses, obviously fresh and furnished with excellent saddlery, were here ready. Even Socrates had a word of praise for his fat compeer’s choice.

“Where did you get him from?” he queried, indicating the mount which Diogenes had without demur selected for himself.

Pythagoras shrugged his shoulder. What did it matter who had been made the poorer by a good horse? Enough that it was here now, ready to do service to the finest horseman in the Netherlands.

“Where do we go?” the others cried.

“After me!” he shouted in reply.

===Chinese===
不消几秒钟，他们便赶到了那地方。毕达哥拉斯喜滋滋地招呼他们。他早等得不耐烦了。三匹马就在这里侍候着，一望便知脚力新鲜，鞍辔也极讲究。连苏格拉底也夸了他那位胖伙伴一句，说他眼光不赖。

「这匹你从哪儿弄来的？」他问，指的是第欧根尼二话不说便挑定的那匹坐骑。

毕达哥拉斯耸了耸肩。一匹好马，总得有人为之破财，那又有什么要紧？要紧的是它如今就在这里，随时听候尼德兰最出色的骑手驱策。

「我们去哪儿？」其余两人喊道。

「跟我来！」他高声应道。

### III / 三

===Original===
Nose to heels, the three riders thundered through the city. It was deserted at this end of it, remember. Thank God for that! And now for a host of guardian angels to the rescue! Down the Oude Straat they galloped, their horses’ hoofs raising myriads of sparks from the uneven cobblestones. “God grant me wings!” the leader had cried ere he set spurs to his horse, and the others followed without an instant’s thought as to the whither or the wherefore. “After me!” he had called; and they who had fought beside him so often, who had bled with him, suffered with him, triumphed at times, been merry always, were well content to follow him now and forget everything in the exhilaration of this chase.

A chase it was! They could not doubt it, even though they seemed at this moment to be speeding in the opposite direction to that pursued by the Stadtholder and Nicolaes Beresteyn. But they well knew their friend’s way, when he let his mount have free rein and threw up his head with that air of intense vitality which in him was at its height when life and death were having a tussle somewhere at the end of a ride.

Down the Oude Straat, which presently abuts on the ramparts. Then another two hundred yards to Nieuwpoort. No one in sight now. This part of the city looks like one of the dead. Doors open wide, litter of every sort encumbering the road. The din from afar, even the ceaseless tolling of St. Maria bell, seem like dream-sounds, ghostlike and unreal. Now the *poort*. Still no one in sight. No guard. No sentry. The gate left open. Here two or three halberds hastily thrown down in some hurried flight. There a culverin, forlorn looking, gaping wide-mouthed, like some huge toad yawning, as if astonished or wearied to find itself deserted. Then again, a pile of muskets. It must have been a sudden panic that drove the guard from their post. But, thank God, the gate!

Diogenes is already through; after him his two compeers. A quarter of a league further on they suddenly draw rein. The horses rear, snorting and tossing, panting with the excitement and fretted with the curb. The riders blink for a second or two in the glare. The white mist is positively blinding here, where its sovereignty is unfettered. Just a clump of trees, way out on the right, here and there a hut with thatched roof and a piece of low fencing, or the gaunt arms of a windmill stretched with eerie stillness to the silvery sky. And above it all the mist—a pale shroud that envelopes everything.

To the east and the south the arid upland plunges headlong through it into infinity, cloaks within its stern bosom the secrets of the lurking enemy—the armies of De Berg, the Spanish outposts, the ambuscades. To the west Utrecht, unseen—and just now two tiny specks speeding along its road—the Stadtholder and Nicolaes Beresteyn. They came out into the open through Joris Poort, and are now some four hundred yards or less from the spot where three panting but exhilarated philosophers were now filling their lungs with the crisp, humid air.

They looked neither to right nor left. The Stadtholder, easily recognizable by his plumed bonnet, rides a length or less in advance of his companion. The fog has not yet swallowed them up. Diogenes takes all this in. A simple enough picture—the sea-fog and two riders speeding towards Utrecht. But a swift intaking of his breath, a tight closing of his firm lips, indicate to the others that all is not as simple as it seems.

===Chinese===
三骑首尾相衔，雷鸣般轰然穿过城池。别忘了，城的这一头早已空无人迹。谢天谢地！此刻唯有指望一群守护天使赶来搭救了！他们顺着旧街（Oude Straat）一路疾驰，马蹄在坑洼不平的鹅卵石路上迸起万千火星。「上帝赐我翅膀！」首领在夹马刺之前这样喊过；其余两人二话不说便跟了上去，全不计较去向与缘由。「跟我来！」他喊过这一声；而这些曾与他并肩血战、同他流血、共过患难、间或同奏凯歌、而始终不失欢乐的人，此刻也心甘情愿追随于他，在这一场驰逐的酣畅里把一切都抛在了脑后。

这确是一场追逐！他们对此毫无怀疑，尽管此刻他们飞驰的方向，似乎恰与执政和尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦所去的方向背道而驰。可他们太熟悉这位朋友的做派了：一旦他松开缰绳任马疾驰、昂起头来透出那股蓬勃的生气——这股生气在他身上，总在一程驰骋的尽头、生死正在某处相搏之时达到极致。

顺旧街而下，街道的尽头便接着城墙。再走两百码便是新门（Nieuwpoort）。此刻四下无人。城的这一片死气沉沉，恍如死城。门户大敞，各色杂物壅塞道路。远处的喧嚣，连圣玛丽亚教堂那不绝的钟声，都恍如梦中之音，鬼影般虚幻而不真。城门到了。仍旧不见人影。没有卫兵。没有哨兵。大门敞着。这里两三杆长戟仓皇弃置于地，显是仓皇奔逃所遗；那边一门长管炮孤零零张着大口，像只打呵欠的巨蟾，仿佛既惊讶又厌倦于自己竟遭丢弃。再往前，又是一堆火枪。想必是一场突发的惊惶把卫兵赶离了岗位。可是，感谢上帝，城门还在！

第欧根尼已经一马冲出；两个伙伴紧随其后。再往前约四分之一里格，他们蓦地勒住了缰。人马竖立起来，喷着响鼻，刨着蹄子，激喘未定，又被衔勒勒得躁怒难安。骑手们在白晃晃的光里眨了一两秒眼睛。白雾在这里实在晃眼——它的君权在此不受一丝束缚。只有右方远处一丛树木，零零落落几座茅顶小屋围着一截矮栅，再不就是风车那枯瘦的长臂，带着可怖的静默伸向银灰色的天空。而在这一切之上是雾——一袭惨白的裹尸布，把万物统统罩住。

东面和南面，干旱的高地一头扎进雾里，直没入无穷远处，把潜伏之敌的秘密严严裹在它冷峻的胸怀里——德·贝格（De Berg）的大军、西班牙人的前哨、一处处的埋伏。西面是乌得勒支（Utrecht），隐没不见——此刻它的大道上正有两个小黑点疾掠而过——执政和尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦。他们是从圣约里斯门出到旷野上来的，眼下离那三位气喘吁吁却兴致勃发的哲人立身之处，不过四百码上下。

他们目不斜视。执政凭那顶插羽的帽子一望便知，领先同伴一个马身光景。雾还没有把他们吞没。第欧根尼把这一切尽收眼底。这幅图景再简单不过——海雾，外加两骑朝乌得勒支疾驰。然而他倏地吸进一口气，两片坚毅的嘴唇紧紧抿住，向其余两人示意：事情并不像看上去那样简单。

### IV / 四

===Original===
Then a very curious thing happened. At first it seemed nothing. But to the watchers outside Nieuwpoort it had the same effect as a flash of lightning would have in an apparently cloudless sky. It began with Nicolaes Beresteyn drawing his horse close up to the Stadtholder, on his Highness’s right. Then for another few seconds the two riders went along side by side, like one black speck now, still quite distinguishable through the fog. Socrates and Pythagoras had their eyes on Diogenes. But Diogenes did not move. He was frowning, and his face had a set and tense expression. He had his horse tightly on the curb, and appeared almost wilfully to fret the animal, who was pawing the wet, sandy ground and covering itself with lather—a picture of tearing impatience.

“What do we do now?” Pythagoras exclaimed at last, unable, just like the horse, to contain his excitement.

“Wait,” Diogenes replied curtly. “All may be well after all.”

“In which case?” queried Socrates.

“Nothing!”

===Chinese===
接着，一件十分古怪的事情发生了。起初似乎不算什么。可对新门外观望的三人来说，这不啻看似无云的天空中骤然劈下的一道闪电。事情始于尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦催马贴近执政，到了殿下的右手边。随后几秒钟，两骑并行而进，此刻浑如一个小黑点，隔着雾仍清晰可辨。苏格拉底和毕达哥拉斯的眼睛都盯在第欧根尼身上。第欧根尼却一动不动。他皱着眉，脸上是一副凝重而紧张的神色。他勒得坐骑紧衔勒口，简直像是存心煎熬那牲口——它刨着湿漉漉的沙地，浑身汗沫交加——活画出一副急不可耐的模样。

「现在我们干什么？」毕达哥拉斯终于嚷了出来，他和那匹马一样，按捺不住心头的激动。

「等。」第欧根尼简截地答道。「说不定到头来一切都会平安。」

「若是那样呢？」苏格拉底问。

「那就什么都不用做！」

===Original===
A groan of disappointment rose to a couple of parched throats; but it was never uttered. What went on in the mist on the road to Utrecht, four hundred yards away, had stifled it at birth.

The Stadtholder’s horse had become restive. A simple matter enough; but in this case unexplainable, because Maurice of Nassau was a splendid horseman. He could easily have quietened the animal if there had not been something abnormal in its sudden antics. It reared and tossed for no apparent reason, would have thrown a less experienced rider.

“The brute is being teased with a goad,” Pythagoras remarked sententiously.

That was clear enough. Even in the distance, and experienced eye could have perceived that the horse became more and more unmanageable every moment, and the Stadtholder’s seat more and more precarious. Then suddenly there came the sharp report of a pistol. The horse, goaded to madness, took the bit between its teeth, and with a final plunge bolted across country, away from that strident noise, which, twice repeated at intervals, had turned its fretfulness into blind panic.

===Chinese===
两副干渴的喉咙里涌起一声失望的呻吟；却终究没有出口。四百码外通往乌得勒支的大路上、雾里正发生的事，把它生生扼杀在了襁褓之中。

执政的马躁动起来。这本是再寻常不过的事；可这一回却无从解释，因为拿骚的莫里斯（Maurice of Nassau）是位极出色的骑手。倘若这牲口突如其来的撒野没有什么反常之处，他本可以轻而易举叫它安静下来。它无缘无故地人立、颠跳，换了骑术稍差的人，早被掀下马来了。

「这畜生是叫尖棒戳弄着了。」毕达哥拉斯一语中的地说道。

这一点再明白不过。哪怕隔着老远，老练的眼睛也看得出那马一刻比一刻难以驾驭，执政的坐姿一刻比一刻岌岌可危。忽听得一声手枪的锐响。那马被戳弄得发了狂，咬紧嚼子，猛地一个前冲，脱缰狂奔，直向旷野里逃开那刺耳的声响——那声响隔一阵又是一响，接连两响，已把它的一腔烦躁变成了全然的惊惶。

===Original===
It was at the first report that Socrates and Pythagoras again glanced at their leader. A gurgle of delight escaped them when they caught his eye. They had received their orders. The next moment all three had dug their spurs in their horses’ flanks and were galloping over sand and ditch.

Diogenes’ horse, given free rein at last, after the maddening curb of awhile ago, was soon half a dozen lengths ahead of the others, tearing along with all its might at right angles to the direction in which the Stadtholder’s panic-stricken animal was rushing like one possessed. That direction was Ede.

===Chinese===
头一声枪响时，苏格拉底和毕达哥拉斯便又去看他们首领的眼色。目光一接上，两人喉咙里都溢出一声欢喜的低哼。号令已经领受。下一刻，三人已把马刺深深磕进马腹，越过沙地与沟渠，飞驰而去。

第欧根尼那匹马，熬过方才那番逼疯它的勒缰，如今总算缰绳尽松，不多时便抢先其余两骑六七个马身，拼尽全力狂驰，跑的路线恰与执政那匹失魂落魄、如中邪魔的坐骑直冲的方向成直角。那个方向，是埃德（Ede）。

### V / 五

===Original===
In truth, the low-lying land veiled in sea-fog must at that moment have presented a very curious spectacle. Maurice of Nassau, Prince of Orange, the hope and pride of the Netherlands, helpless upon a horse that was running away with him straight in the direction of the nearest Spanish outposts.

Three soldiers of fortune, strangers, in the land hastening to intercept him, and a couple of hundred yards or so behind the Stadtholder, Nicolaes Beresteyn, puzzled and terror-filled at this unexpected check to his manoeuvre, pushing along for dear life.

It had been such a splendid scheme, evolved over there in the lonely mill on the Veluwe, between a reprobate and a traitor. The Spaniards on the watch. The Stadtholder helpless, whilst his mount carried him headlong into their hands. What a triumph for Stoutenburg, who had planned it all, and for Nicolaes Beresteyn, the worker of the infamous plot! The Stadtholder prisoner in the hands of the Archduchess! His life the price of the subjection of the Netherlands!

And all to be frustrated by a foolish mischance! Three riders intent upon intercepting that runaway horse! Who in thunder were they? The mist, remember, would have blurred Nicolaes’ vision. His thoughts were not just then on the man whom he hated. They were fixed upon the possibility—remote, alas!—of convincing the Stadtholder after this that the goaded horse had been the victim of a series of accidents.

Even at this moment the foremost of the three riders had overtaken the runaway. He galloped for a length or two beside it, then, with a dexterous and unerring grip, he seized the panic-stricken animal by the bridle. A few seconds of desperate struggle ’twixt man and beast. Then man remained the conqueror. The horse, panting, quivering in every limb, covered in sweat and foam, was finally brought to a standstill, and the Stadtholder in an instant had his feet clear of the stirrups and swung himself out of the saddle.

===Chinese===
说实在的，那一刻笼罩在海雾里的低地，想必呈现着一幅奇极的图景。拿骚的莫里斯，奥兰治亲王（Prince of Orange），尼德兰的希望与骄傲，在一匹脱缰的马上束手无策，径直朝着最近的西班牙前哨狂奔而去。

三个异乡的雇佣冒险家在这片土地上疾驰，赶去拦截他；而在执政身后二百码上下，尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦眼见机关陡然受挫，又是惶惑又是惊怖，没命地催马赶来。

这原是何等妙的一条计策，在费吕韦（the Veluwe）荒原上那座孤零零的磨坊里，由一个浪荡子和一个叛徒共同谋成。西班牙人张网守望。执政束手无策，任坐骑载着他一头扎进他们掌心。对谋划这一切的斯托滕贝格（Stoutenburg），对操办这桩毒计的尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦，这是何等的凯旋！执政落入大公夫人（the Archduchess）之手成为阶下囚！他的性命，就是尼德兰俯首归降的身价！

而这一切，竟要毁于一场愚蠢的意外！三个骑手，一心要拦下那匹狂奔的马！他们究竟是何方神圣？要知道，雾气想必迷蒙了尼古拉斯的视线。他那时心思并不在他所痛恨的那个人身上，而全在那桩——唉，渺茫得很！——可能性上：事后说服执政，叫他不疑心有诈，只当那匹受戳弄的马是连番意外的不幸受害者。

便在此刻，三骑中最前面的一人已经追上了那匹狂马。他并辔驰出一两个马身，随即使出一记又巧又准的手法，一把攥住了那匹惊惶牲口的笼头。人马之间殊死相搏了几秒钟。最终，人还是征服者。那马喘息着，四肢战栗，通体汗沫，终于被勒定下来；执政转眼间已把双脚脱出马镫，翻身下了鞍。

### VI / 六

===Original===
Then, and then only, did Nicolaes Beresteyn recognize the man who for the second time had frustrated his nefarious plans—the man whom, because of his easy triumphs, the humiliation which he had inflicted upon him, because of his careless gaiety and his very joy of life, he hated with a curious, sinister intensity.

A ferocious imprecation rose to his lips. For awhile everything became a blank. The present, the future, even the past. Everything became chaos in his mind, he could no longer think. All that he had planned became a blur, as if the sea-fog had enveloped his senses as well as the entire landscape.

But this confused mental state only lasted a very little while—a few seconds perhaps. Slowly, while he gazed on that distant group of men and horses, his perceptions became clearer once more. And even before the imprecation had died on his lips it gave place to a smile of triumph. Nicolaes Beresteyn had remembered that his Majesty the devil might well be trusted to care for his own. Had he not served the hell-born liege lord well?

For the nonce he brought his horse to a halt. It would be worse than folly to go on. With recognition of those three horsemen over there had also come the certainty that he was now irretrievably unmasked. The Stadtholder, his father, his sister, even his young wife, would turn from him in horror, as from a traitor and an outcast—a pariah, marked with the brand of Cain.

===Chinese===
直到这时，直到这一刻，尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦才认出那个第二回挫败他毒计的人——那个人，他恨得诡异而阴毒：恨他不费吹灰之力的连连得手，恨他加诸自己的羞辱，恨他那副满不在乎的快活劲儿，恨他对生命本身的那份欢愉。

一声凶残的咒骂涌到他的唇边。有一阵子，一切都成了一片空白。现在、将来，连过去也是。他心中万事化作混沌，再也不能思考。他苦心筹划的一切模糊成了一团，仿佛海雾不只裹住了整个天地，也裹住了他的神志。

可这阵神志昏乱只持续了很短一会儿——也许几秒钟。他凝望着远处那一人一马的一小群，知觉渐渐又清晰起来。那声咒骂还没在他唇边消散，就让位给了一抹得胜的狞笑。尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦想起来了：魔鬼陛下尽可以放心，他自会照拂自家人。他不是把那位地狱所生的主公伺候得很周到么？

他暂且把马勒住。再往前去，就不止是愚蠢了。认出那边三名骑手的同时，他也确知自己如今已无可挽回地败露。执政、他的父亲、他的妹妹，连他年轻的新婚妻子，都会惊怖地躲开他，像躲开一个叛徒、一个逐徒——一个贱民，身上打着该隐（Cain）的烙印。

===Original===
No! Henceforth, for good or for evil, his fortunes must be linked openly with Stoutenburg—with the man who wielded such a strange cabalistic power over him that he (Nicolaes)—rich, newly wed, in a highly enviable worldly position—had been ready to sacrifice his all in his cause, and to throw in the last shred of his honour into the bargain. In Stoutenburg’s cause—ay, and in order to be revenged on the man who had never wronged him save in his conceit—that most vulnerable spot in the moral armour of such contemptible rogues as was Nicolaes Beresteyn.

The spot where he had brought his horse to a halt was immediately behind a low, deserted hut, which concealed him from view. Here he dismounted and, throwing the reins over his arm, advanced cautiously to a point of vantage at the angle of the little building, whence he could see what those four men were doing over there but himself remain unseen.

They, too, had dismounted, and were obviously intent on examining the Stadtholder’s horse. A sinister scowl spread over Nicolaes Beresteyn’s face. There was still a chance, then, of putting a bullet in one or other of those two men—the hated enemy or the Stadtholder. Nicolaes pondered; the scowl on his face became almost satanic in its expression of cruelty. Awhile ago, he had replaced his pistol in the holster, after it had served its nefarious purpose. Now he took it out again and examined it thoroughly.

It had one more charge in it, the devilish charge invented by the Borgias, the secret of which one of that infamous race had confided to Stoutenburg. The fumes from the powder when it struck the eyes must cause irretrievable blindness. Indeed, it had proved its worth already.

===Chinese===
不！从今往后，无论为善为恶，他的命运只能公然与斯托滕贝格拴在一起——同那个对他握着一种离奇、几乎如邪咒一般的支配力的人拴在一起；他尼古拉斯——家道殷实，新婚燕尔，在人间的处境令人艳羡——却甘愿为他牺牲一切，还搭上自己最后一点体面。为斯托滕贝格的大业——是啊，也为了向那个人报仇雪恨；那个人从没真正亏待过他，亏待他的只是他的自负——那正是尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦这等可鄙宵小道德甲胄上最脆弱的一处。

他勒马的所在，正在一座低矮的废屋背后，那屋子恰好把他遮得严严实实。他在这里下了马，缰绳往臂上一搭，蹑手蹑脚摸到小屋墙角一处瞰制要地，从那里望得见那四个人在做什么，自己却不致暴露。

那边四人也已下马，显然正忙着查看执政那匹马。一层狰狞的阴云漫上尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦的脸。这么说，还有机会给那两个人中的一个吃一颗子弹——不是那可恨的仇敌，便是执政。尼古拉斯盘算着；他脸上的阴云几乎显出撒旦般的残酷。方才那支手枪办完它的罪恶差使后，他已把它插回了枪套。此刻他又拔出来，细细查验。

枪膛里还剩一发弹药——那邪恶的一发，波吉亚（Borgias）家族的发明，那门秘诀，正是这万恶种族中的一人悄悄传授给斯托滕贝格的。火药的烟气一入眼睛，必致无可挽回的失明。事实上，它已经显过灵验了。

===Original===
Nicolaes, from his hiding-place, could see those four men quite clearly. The Stadtholder, Diogenes, the two caitiffs, all standing round the one horse. Then Diogenes took something out of his belt. He raised his arm, and the next moment a sharp report rang through the mistladen air. The poor animal rolled over instantly into the mud.

The scowl on Nicolaes’ face now gave place once more to a smile of triumph—more sinister than the frown. With the gesture of a conqueror, he clutched the pistol more firmly. The potent fumes had, in truth, wrought their fiendish work on the innocent beast. Diogenes had just put it out of its misery, and his two familiars were preparing to mount one of the horses, whilst he and the Stadtholder had the other two by the reins.

Why not?

The miscreant was sure enough of his aim, and the others would be unprepared. He was sure, too, of the swiftness of his horse, and the Spanish outposts were less than a quarter of a league away, whilst within half that distance Stoutenburg was on the watch with a vedette, waiting to capture the Stadtholder on his runaway horse as it had been prearranged.

Once there he—Nicolaes—would be amongst friends.

===Chinese===
尼古拉斯从藏身处能把那四个人看得清清楚楚。执政、第欧根尼、那两个泼皮，都围着那匹马站着。只见第欧根尼从腰带上拔出一件什么东西。他抬起手臂，下一刻，一声锐响划破浓雾弥漫的天空。那可怜的牲口登时翻倒在泥泞里。

尼古拉斯脸上的阴云此刻重又让位给得胜的狞笑——比那阴云更加不祥。他以征服者的姿态，把手枪攥得更紧。那烈性的毒烟，果真已在无辜的牲口身上奏了它恶魔的功。第欧根尼方才那一枪，正是结束它的苦难；他的两个亲随正准备同骑一匹马，他和执政则各自牵住了另外两匹的缰绳。

何乐而不为？

这恶徒对自己的准头深信不疑，而对方全无防备。他也深信自己坐骑的脚力：西班牙人的前哨不出四分之一里格，而不及一半路程的地方，斯托滕贝格正带着一名哨探守望，只等按事先的约定，捕获那匹狂马上载着的执政。

一到那里，他——尼古拉斯——便是置身于朋友中间了。

===Original===
Then, why not?

Already the riders had put their horses to a trot. Diogenes and the Stadtholder on ahead, the two loons some few lengths in their rear. In less than three minutes they would be within range of Nicolaes’ pistol and its blinding fumes. And Diogenes was riding on the side nearest to his enemy.

Nicolaes Beresteyn grasped his weapon more firmly. He realized with infinite satisfaction that his arm was perfectly steady. Indeed, he had never felt so absolutely calm. The measured tramp of the horses keyed him up to a point of unswerving determination. He raised his arm. The horses were galloping now. They would pass like a flash within twenty paces of him.

The next moment the sharp report of the pistol rang stridently through the mist. There was a burst, a flash, a column of smoke. Nicolaes jumped into the saddle and set spurs to his horse. The other riders went galloping on for a few seconds—not more. Then one of them swayed in his saddle. Nicolaes then was a couple of hundred yards away.

“You are hit, man!” the Stadtholder exclaimed. “That abominable assassin—”

But the words died in his throat. The reins had slipped out of Diogenes’ grasp, and he rolled down into the mud.

===Chinese===
那么，何乐而不为？

骑手们已经催马小跑起来。第欧根尼和执政在前，两个活宝落后几个马身。不出三分钟，他们就要进入尼古拉斯那支手枪及其致盲毒烟的射程。而第欧根尼骑的，恰是离他仇敌最近的一侧。

尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦把手中的武器攥得更紧。他带着无限的快意察觉到，自己的手臂稳如磐石。说实话，他从没这样心如止水过。马蹄那有节奏的踏响，把他的心弦拧上了一个绝不动摇的决断。他抬起手臂。马群此刻已经飞驰起来。它们将电掣一般，从他面前二十步之内掠过。

下一刻，手枪的锐响尖利地穿透雾气。一声爆裂，一道火光，一柱硝烟。尼古拉斯纵身上鞍，催马便走。其余几骑继续飞驰了几秒钟——不过几秒。随后，其中一人在鞍上晃了晃。那时尼古拉斯已在两百码开外了。

「你中弹了，伙计！」执政失声道。「那个可憎的刺客——」

可是话音在他喉咙里咽了下去。缰绳从第欧根尼手中滑落，他一头滚进了泥泞里。

### VII / 七

===Original===
A sudden jerk brought the Stadtholder’s horse to a halt. He swung himself out of the saddle, ran quickly to his companion.

“You are hit, man!” he reiterated; this time with an unexplainable feeling of dread.

The other seemed so still, and yet his clothes and the soft earth around him showed no stains of blood.

Pythagoras now was also on the spot. He had slid off the horse as soon as the infamous assassin had started to ride away. Socrates was trying to give chase. Even now two pistol-shots rang out in quick succession right across the moorland. But the hellhound was well mounted, and the avenging bullets failed to reach their mark. All this the Stadtholder took in with a rapid glance, even whilst Pythagoras, round-eyed and scared, was striving with gentle means to raise the strangely inert figure.

===Chinese===
猛地一拽，执政的马停了下来。他翻身下鞍，快步奔到同伴身边。

「你中弹了，伙计！」他又说了一遍；这一回，心里带着一种说不清的恐惧。

那人躺在那里一动不动，可他的衣衫和四周的软泥上，却不见一丝血污。

毕达哥拉斯此时也赶到了。那无耻的刺客一拨马逃走，他便溜下鞍来。苏格拉底还在追赶。甚至此刻，荒原上还接连响起两声枪响。可那头地狱的恶犬骑术精湛、坐骑精良，复仇的子弹没能追上他。执政一眼之间把这一切收在眼里，而那边毕达哥拉斯睁圆了眼睛，满脸惊惶，正轻手轻脚地想扶起那具莫名瘫软的身躯。

===Original===
“He hath swooned,” the Stadtholder suggested.

The stricken man had one arm across his face. His hat had fallen from his head, leaving the fine, square brow free and the crisp hair weighted by the sweat of some secret agony. The mouth, too, was visible, and the chin, with its four days’ growth of beard, the mouth that was always ready with a smile. It was set now in an awesome contraction of pain, and, withal, that terrible immobility.

Now Socrates was arriving. A moment or two later he, too, had dismounted, cursing lustily that he had failed to hit the hellhound. A mute query, an equally mute reply, was all that passed between him and Pythagoras.

Then the stricken man stirred as if suddenly roused to consciousness.

===Chinese===
「他晕过去了。」执政猜度道。

遭难的人一只手臂横遮着脸。帽子从头上滚落，露出清朗方正的额头，那头浓密的鬈发被某种隐秘痛楚沁出的冷汗压得沉沉坠坠。嘴也看得见，还有下巴——蓄了四天的胡茬——那张素来随时含笑的嘴。此刻它僵在一阵骇人的痛楚抽搐里，而且，还有那份可怕的僵滞。

这时苏格拉底也赶到了。一两分钟后，他也下了马，扯着嗓门大骂，恨自己没能打中那头地狱恶犬。他与毕达哥拉斯之间，只交换了一个无言的询问，和一个同样无言的回答。

随后，那遭难的人动了动，仿佛蓦地醒转过来。

===Original===
“Are you hit, man?” the Stadtholder queried again.

“No—no!” he replied quickly. “Only a little dazed. That is all.”

He raised himself to a sitting posture, helping himself up with his hands, which sank squelching into the mud; whereat he gave a short laugh, which somehow went a cold shiver down the listeners’ spines.

“Where is my hat?” he asked. “Pythagoras, you lazy loon, get me my hat.”

He must indeed have been still dazed, for when his friend picked up the hat and gave it to him, his hand shot out for it quite wide of the mark. He gave another laugh, short and toneless as before, and set the hat on his head, pulling down well over his eyes.

===Chinese===
「你中弹了吗，伙计？」执政又问了一遍。

「不——不！」他急忙答道。「只是有点发懵。就这些。」

他双手撑地，坐起身来，双手噗嗤噗嗤地陷进泥里；他随之短促地笑了一声，那笑声不知怎的，叫听的人脊背上一阵寒栗。

「我的帽子呢？」他问。「毕达哥拉斯，你这懒骨头活宝，把帽子给我拿来。」

他想必还懵着，因为朋友捡起帽子递给他时，他的手伸出去接，却偏出了老远。他又笑了一声，跟方才一样短促而木然，然后把帽子扣在头上，拉得低低的，严严实实罩住眼睛。

===Original===
“I had a mugful of hot ale at Amersfoort before starting,” he said. “It must have got into my head.”

He made no attempt to get to his feet, but just sat there, with his two slender hands all covered with mud, tightly clasped between his knees.

“Can you get to horse?” his Highness queried at last.

“No,” Diogenes replied, “not just yet, an’ it please you, I verily think that I would roll out of my saddle again, which would, in truth, be a disgusting spectacle.”

“But we cannot leave you here, man,” the Stadtholder rejoined, with a slight tone of impatience.

===Chinese===
「动身前我在阿默斯福特灌了一大杯热麦酒，」他说。「酒劲儿想必冲上头了。」

他不作起身的意思，只管坐在那里，两只修长的手满是泥污，紧紧夹在两膝之间。

「你能上马吗？」殿下终于问道。

「不行，」第欧根尼答道，「眼下还不成，请您包涵。我当真觉得，这会儿上了鞍还得再滚下来，那光景可就着实有伤大雅了。」

「可我们总不能把你撂在这儿呀，伙计。」执政应道，语气里带上一丝不耐。

===Original===
“And why not, I pray you?” he retorted. “Your Highness must get to Utrecht as quickly as may be. A half-drunken lout like me would only be a hindrance.”

His voice was thick now and halting, in very truth like that of a man who had been drinking heavily. He rested his elbows on his knees and held his chin between his mud-stained hands.

“Socrates, you lumpish vagabond,” he exclaimed all of a sudden, “don’t stand gaping at me like that! Bring forth his Highness’s horse at once, and see that you accompany him to Utrecht without further mishap, or ’tis with us you’ll have to deal on your return!”

“But you, man!” the Stadtholder exclaimed once more.

He felt helpless and strangely disturbed in his mind, not understanding what all this meant; why this man, usually so alert, so keen, so full of vigour, appeared for the moment akin to a babbling imbecile.

===Chinese===
「那又为何不可呢，我倒要请问？」他反诘道。「殿下必须尽快赶到乌得勒支去。我这样一个半醉的粗汉，跟着去只会碍手碍脚。」

他此刻嗓音含混，字句磕绊，实实在在像极了豪饮过的人。他把两肘支在膝上，下巴托在两只泥污的手中间。

「苏格拉底，你这呆头呆脑的流氓，」他猛地嚷道，「别站在那儿对我瞪眼！马上把殿下的马牵过来，你给我护送他到乌得勒支，路上再出半点差错，回来可少不得跟我们交代！」

「可是你呢，伙计！」执政又嚷了一声。

他只觉无能为力，心里又莫名地纷乱，不明白这一切究竟是什么缘故；这个人素来那般机警、那般敏锐、那般元气淋漓，此刻怎么竟像个口齿不清的痴子。

===Original===
“I’ll have a good sleep inside that hut, so please you,” the other replied more glibly. “These two ruffians will find me here after they have seen your gracious Highness safely inside your camp.”

Then, as the Stadtholder still appeared to hesitate, and neither of the others seemed to move, Diogenes added, with an almost desperate note of entreaty:

“To horse, your Highness, I beg! Every second is precious. Heaven knows what further devilry lies in wait for you, if you linger here.”

“Or for you, man,” the Stadtholder murmured involuntarily.

“Nay, not for me!” the other retorted quickly. “The Archduchess and her gang of vultures fly after higher game than a drunken wayfarer lost on the flats. To horse, I entreat!”

===Chinese===
「我要在那座小屋里好好睡上一觉，如您恩准。」对方答得愈发流利。「等这两位恶棍护送您殿下平安进了大营，自会回来寻我。」

见执政仍有迟疑之色，另外两人也似动未动，第欧根尼又添了一句，口气里带着近乎哀求的急切：

「上马吧，殿下，求您了！每一秒都贵似千金。天知道您若在此耽搁，还有什么更毒的奸计在前头候着您。」

「或者候着你呢，伙计。」执政不由自主地低声道。

「不，轮不到我！」对方急急驳道。「大公夫人和她那群兀鹰，追猎的可是更大的目标，犯不上理会一个醉倒在荒滩上的过路人。上马吧，我恳求您！」

===Original===
And once more he pressed his hands together, and so tightly that the knuckles shone like polished ivory, even through their covering of mud.

The Stadtholder then gave a sign to the two men. It was obviously futile to continue arguing here with a man who refused to move. He himself had very rightly said that every second was precious. And every second, too, was fraught with danger. Already his Highness had well-nigh been the victim of a diabolical ambuscade, might even at this hour have been a prisoner in the hands of the enemy, a hostage of incalculable value, even if his life had been spared, but for the audacious and timely interference of this man, who now appeared almost like one partially bereft of reason.

“We’ll see you safely inside the hut, at any rate,” was his Highness’s last word.

“And I’ll not move,” Diogenes retorted with a kind of savage obstinacy, “until the mist has swallowed up your gracious Highness on the road to Utrecht.”

===Chinese===
他又一次把双手紧紧合拢，攥得那样紧，指节竟像磨亮的象牙一般泛出光来，尽管蒙着一层泥污。

于是执政向那两人示意。同这等不肯挪窝的人在此地再争下去，显然徒劳。他自己方才说得很对，每一秒都贵似千金。而每一秒也都危机四伏。殿下方才已险些葬身于一场恶魔般的埋伏；若非此人这般胆大包天、又恰逢其时的横加干预，他此时此刻说不定已是敌人手中的囚徒、一名价值无可估量的人质——纵使留得性命。而这个人，眼下瞧上去竟几乎像个失了半个神智的痴人。

「无论如何，我们也得亲眼看着你安稳进了那座小屋。」这是殿下的最后一句话。

「那我便坐着不动，」第欧根尼带着一种近乎狠戾的固执回嘴道，「直到大雾把您殿下吞进乌得勒支的大路上为止。」

===Original===
After that there was nothing more to be said. And we may take it that the Stadtholder got to horse with unaccountable reluctance. Something in that solitary figure sitting there, with the plumed hat tilted over his eyes and the slender, mud-stained hands tightly locked together, gave him a strange feeling of nameless dismay, like a premonition of some obscure catastrophic tragedy.

But his time and his safety did not belong to himself alone. They were the inalienable property of a threatened country, that would be grasping in her death-throes if she were deprived of him at this hour of renewed and deadly danger.

The two gossoons immediately followed in his wake. This they did because the friend they had always been wont to obey had thus commanded, and his seeming helplessness rendered his orders doubly imperative at this hour. They rode a length or two behind the Stadtholder, who presently put his horse to a gallop. Utrecht now was only a couple of leagues away.

The three horsemen galloped on for a quarter of a league or less at the same even, rapid pace. Then Pythagoras slackened speed. The others did not even turn to look at him, he seemed to have done it by tacit unspoken consent. The Stadtholder and Socrates sped on in the direction of Utrecht and Pythagoras turned his horse’s head round toward the direction whence he had come.

===Chinese===
此后便再没有什么可说的了。我们可以想见，执政上马时那份难以言喻的不情愿。那个独坐地上的身影，插羽的帽子斜掩着眼，修长而泥污的双手紧紧交锁——那身影里有一种东西，给了他一种无名惶恐的怪异感觉，像是对某场隐晦大祸的不祥预感。

可他的光阴与安危，并不单属于他自己。它们是一个正遭威胁的邦国无可剥夺的财产；值此凶险重临、生死存亡之际，这邦国若失去他，势必要在垂死的抽搐中苦苦挣扎。

两个小子随即跟了上去。他们之所以如此，是因为那位他们一向习惯听命的朋友下了这道令，而他眼下这份无依无靠的模样，更让他的号令在此刻加倍不可违拗。他们落后执政一两个马身跟着，不多时执政便催马飞驰起来。乌得勒支此时不过两里格路了。

三骑以同样均匀迅疾的步子又驰了四分之一里格上下。随后毕达哥拉斯放缓了速度。其余两人竟连头也不回，他这样做好似得了彼此心照不宣的默许。执政和苏格拉底朝乌得勒支疾驰而去，毕达哥拉斯则拨转马头，朝着来路驰回。

### VIII / 八

===Original===
The afternoon lay heavy and silent upon the plain. There was as yet no sign of the approach of the enemy from the south, and the low-lying land appeared momentarily hushed under its veil of mist, as if conscious of the guilty secret enshrined within its bosom. The fog, indeed, had thickened perceptibly. It lay like a wall around that lonely figure, still sitting there on the soft earth, with its head buried in its hands.

Far away, the gaunt-looking carcase of the dead horse appeared as the only witness of a hideous deed as yet unrecorded. Each a blurred and uncertain mass—the dead horse and the lonely figure, equally motionless, equally pathetic—were now the sole occupants of the vast and silent immensity.

Not far away, in the little town of Amersfoort, humanity, panic-stricken and terrorized, filled the air with clamour and with wails. Here, beneath the ghostly shroud of humid atmosphere, everything was stilled as if in ghoulish expectancy of something mysterious, indiscernible which was still to come. It was like the arrested breath before the tearing cry, the hush which precedes a storm.

Overhead, a flight of rooks sent their melancholy cawing through the air.

===Chinese===
午后的天光沉沉地、无声地压在平原上。南面尚无敌军来犯的迹象，低地笼在雾幕之下，一时万籁俱寂，仿佛自知胸中藏着那桩罪孽的秘密。雾气确实明显地更浓了。它像一堵墙，围住那个孤零零的身影——他仍旧坐在软泥地上，头埋在双手里。

远处，那匹死马嶙峋的躯壳，成了这桩尚未昭彰于世的可怖罪行唯一的见证。两团模糊难辨的形体——死马与孤影，同样一动不动，同样凄惶——此刻便是这浩瀚沉寂的天地间仅有的居者。

不远处，阿默斯福特小城里，惊惶失措、魂飞魄散的人们，把满天都填满了喧嚣与哀号。而在这里，在湿漉漉的雾殓之下，万物都静止了，仿佛在以一种食尸鬼般的期待，等候着某桩神秘莫辨、尚未来临的事情。恰似撕裂的哭喊前那屏住的一息，暴风雨来临前的那阵死寂。

头顶上，一群白嘴鸦把凄凉的啼声撒进天空。

===Original===
When Pythagoras was within fifty yards of his friend he dismounted, and, leading his horse by the bridle, he walked toward him. When he was quite near Diogenes put out a hand to him.

“I knew you would come back, you fat-witted nonny,” he said simply.

“Socrates had to go on with the Stadtholder,” the other remarked, “or he’d be here, too.” Then he added tentatively: “Will you lean on my arm?”

“Yes, I’ll have to do that now, old crony, shall I not?” Diogenes replied. “That devil,” he murmured under his breath, “has blinded me!”

===Chinese===
毕达哥拉斯离朋友还有五十码时便下了马，牵着笼头，徒步向他走去。走到近前，第欧根尼向他伸出一只手。

「我就知道你会回来，你这蠢头蠢脑的呆子。」他平平淡淡地说。

「苏格拉底得跟着执政继续赶路，」对方说，「不然他也会来的。」接着他试探着补了一句：「要我扶你一把吗？」

「是啊，如今只好这样了，老伙计，不是么？」第欧根尼答道。「那个魔鬼，」他压低声音喃喃道，「把我的眼睛弄瞎了！」

## IX Mala Fides / IX 背信

### I / 一

===Original===
Nicolaes Beresteyn, riding like one possessed had reached Stoutenburg’s encampment one hour before nightfall. He brought the news of the failure of his plan for the capture of the Stadtholder, spoke with many a muttered oath of the Englishman and his two familiars, and of how they had interposed just in the nick of time to stop the runaway horse.

“But for that cursed rogue!” he exclaimed savagely, “Maurice of Nassau would now be a prisoner in our hands. We would be holding him to ransom, earning gratitude, honours, wealth at the hands of the Archduchess. Whereas﻿—now﻿—”

But there was solace to the bitterness of this disappointment. The blinding powder, invented by the infamous Borgia, had done its work. The abominable rogue, the nameless adventurer, who had twice succeeded in thwarting the best-laid schemes of his lordship of Stoutenburg, had paid the full penalty for his audacity and his arrogant interference.

===Chinese===
尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦（Nicolaes Beresteyn）一路纵马疾驰，浑如着魔一般，赶在天黑前一个钟头到了斯托滕贝格（Stoutenburg）的营盘。他带来了自己劫持执政（the Stadtholder）的计划功败垂成的消息，嘴里连声咕哝着咒骂，骂那英国人和他那两个党羽，骂他们偏偏在那千钧一发之际横插一手，拦住了那匹脱缰奔逃的马。

「若不是那个该死的恶棍！」他杀气腾腾地嚷道，「拿骚的莫里斯（Maurice of Nassau）这会儿早已是我们囊中的囚徒了。咱们只管拿他勒索赎金，从大公夫人（the Archduchess）手里挣来恩宠、荣衔和财帛。可是如今——如今——」

不过，这番失望的苦涩里也自有慰藉。那出自声名狼藉的博尔吉亚（Borgia）之手的致盲药粉，毕竟奏了效。那个可憎的恶棍、那个无名冒险家——此人已两度搅坏了斯托滕贝格勋爵安排得滴水不漏的计谋——这回也为他的胆大妄为、为他那嚣张的搅局，十足地付出了代价。

===Original===
Blind, helpless, broken, an object now of contemptuous pity rather than of hate, he was henceforth powerless to wreak further mischief.

“Just before I put my horse to a swift gallop,” Nicolaes Beresteyn had concluded, “I saw him sway in the saddle and roll down into the mud. One of the vagabonds tried to chase me; but my horse bore me well and I was soon out of his reach.”

===Chinese===
瞎了，废了，垮了——如今他只配领受一种带着轻鄙的怜悯，而引不起仇恨——从此再没有本事为非作歹了。

「就在我催马疾驰的前一刻，」尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦末了说道，「我看见他在鞍上晃了晃，一头栽进泥泞里。那两个流氓里有一个想追我；可我的马脚力不弱，不消片刻，他就再也撵不上我了。」

===Original===
That news did, indeed compensate Stoutenburg for all the humiliation which he had endured at the hands of his successful rival in the past. A rival no longer; for the Laughing Cavalier, blind and helpless, was not like ever to return to claim his young wealthy wife and to burden her with his misery. This last tribute to the man’s pluck and virility Stoutenburg paid him unconsciously. He could not visualize that splendid creature, so full of life and gaiety, and conscious of might strength, groping his way back to the side of the woman whom he had dazzled by his power.

“He would sooner die in a ditch,” he muttered to himself, under his breath, “than excite her pity!”

“Then the field is clear for me!” he added exultantly; and fell to discussing with Nicolaes his chances of regaining Gilda’s affections. “Do you think she ever cared for the rogue?” he queried, with a strange quiver of emotion in his harsh voice.

===Chinese===
这消息，确实把斯托滕贝格从前在那位得手的情敌手里受尽的羞辱，一笔一笔都补偿了回来。情敌已不复为情敌；因为那「笑面骑士」，眼瞎身残，断然再不能回来认领他年轻富有的妻子，把自己的苦难压上她的肩头。这是斯托滕贝格不自觉地献与那人的胆气与血性的最后敬意。他无法想象，那样一个堂堂的人物——那样生气勃勃、笑语晏晏、自觉浑身是力的人——会一路摸索着，爬回那个曾被他的威势炫得目眩神迷的女人身边。

「他宁可死在阴沟里，」他压着嗓子喃喃自语，「也断不肯讨她的怜悯！」

「这么说，场子清了，该我登场了！」他扬扬得意地又补一句；随即同尼古拉斯计议起自己重博吉尔达（Gilda）欢心的胜算来。「你说，她可曾有过一丝真爱那恶棍？」他问道，粗厉的嗓音里颤过一阵异样的激动。

===Original===
Nicolaes was doubtful. He himself had never been in love. He liked his young wife well enough; she was comely and rich. But love? No, he could not say.

“She’ll not know what has become of him,” Stoutenburg said, striving to allay his own doubts. “And women very quickly forget.”

He sighed, proud of his own manly passion that had survived so many vicissitudes, and was linked to such a tenacious memory.

===Chinese===
尼古拉斯拿不准。他自己是从没有恋爱过的。年轻妻子他倒也喜欢；她标致，又有钱。可爱情么？不，那他说不上。

「她不会知道他出了什么事，」斯托滕贝格说，一心要按捺自己的疑虑，「再说，女人忘性快。」

他叹了口气，颇为自负——他这一腔男儿的痴情，历尽多少浮沉波折而不死，还系着这样一副挣不脱的记性。

===Original===
“We must not let her know,” Nicolaes insisted.

Stoutenburg gave a short, sardonic laugh. “Are you afraid she might kill you if she did?” he queried.

Then, as the other made no reply, but stood there brooding, his soul a prey to a sudden horror, which was not unlike a vague pang of remorse, Stoutenburg concluded cynically:

“I’ll give the order that every blind beggar found wandering around the city be forthwith hanged on the nearest tree. Will that allay your fears?”

===Chinese===
「咱们决不能让她知道，」尼古拉斯一口咬定。

斯托滕贝格迸出一声短促的讥笑。「怎么，你怕她知道了会要你的命？」他问。

对方不作声，只顾站在那里出神，魂灵儿被一阵骤起的恐怖攫住——那恐怖又隐隐像一记悔恨的刺痛。斯托滕贝格看在眼里，冷冷地下了断语：

「我会下一道令：凡在本城内外游荡的瞎眼乞丐，一经拿获，即刻吊死在最近的树上。这样可压得下你的怕？」

===Original===
Thereafter he paid no further heed to Nicolaes, whom, in his heart, he despised for a waverer and a weakling; but he gave orders to his master of the camp to make an immediate start for Amersfoort.

===Chinese===
此后他便再不理会尼古拉斯——此人在他心里，早被看成一个首鼠两端的软骨头；他只向行营总管传令：即刻拔营，兵发阿默斯福特（Amersfoort）。

### II / 二

===Original===
Amersfoort had, in the meanwhile, so De Voocht avers, become wonderfully calm. Those whose nerves would not stand the strain of seeing the hated tyrants once more within the gates of their peace-loving little city, those who had no responsibilities, and those who had families, fled at the first rumour of the enemy’s approach. Indeed, for many hours the streets and open places, the quays and the sleepy, sluggish river, had on the first day been nothing short of a pandemonium. Then everything gradually became hushed and tranquil. Those who were panic-stricken had all gone by nightfall; those who remained knew the risk they were taking, and sat in their homes, waiting and pondering. Amersfoort that evening might have been a city of the dead.

Darkness set in early, and the sea-fog thickened at sundown. Some wiseacres said that the Spaniards would not come until the next day. They proved to be right. The dawn had hardly spread o’er the whole of the eastern sky on the morning of the twenty-second, when the master of the enemy’s camp was heard outside the ramparts, demanding the surrender of the city.

===Chinese===
其间，据德·福赫特（De Voocht）所言，阿默斯福特竟出奇地平静了下来。有的人，眼见可恨的暴君们再度踏进他们这座爱好和平的小城的城门，神经便吃不住这份煎熬；有的人无官一身轻，有的人拖家带口——一听到敌军将到的风声，这些人便逃了。的确，头一天里，一连好几个钟头，街道和广场、码头和那条睡意沉沉、流得慢吞吞的小河，简直闹作了一座群魔乱舞的地狱。随后一切渐渐归于阒寂。惊慌失措的人到天黑时已逃了个净；留下的人自知是在冒怎样的险，便坐在家中，等着，思量着。那天傍晚的阿默斯福特，俨然一座死城。

夜幕降临得早，日头一沉，海雾便浓了起来。有些自作聪明的角色说，西班牙人要到第二天才会来。他们居然说中了。二十二日清晨，曙光尚未铺满东天，城垣外已听得见敌军行营总管的声音，正在勒令献城投降。

===Original===
The summons was received in absolute silence. The gates were open, and the mercenaries marched in. In battle array, with banners flying, with pikemen, halberdiers and arquebusiers; with fifes and drums and a trainload of wagons and horses, and the usual rabble of beggarly camp followers, they descended on the city like locusts; and soon every tavern was filled to overflowing with loud-voiced, swarthy, ill-mannered soldiery, and all the streets and places encumbered with their carts and their horses and their trappings.

They built a bonfire in the middle of the marketplace, and all around it a crowd of out-at-elbows ruffians, men, women, and children, filled the air with their shrieks and their bibulous songs. Some four thousand troops altogether, so De Voocht states, spread themselves out over the orderly, prosperous town, invaded the houses, broke open the cellars and storehouses, made the day hideous with their noise and their roistering.

===Chinese===
这道通牒换来的是绝对的沉默。城门洞开，雇佣军开进城来。列着战阵，旌旗招展，长枪兵、戟兵、火绳枪兵齐齐整整；横笛与战鼓之外，还有一长列辎重车马，外加照例蜂拥蚁附、衣衫褴褛的随营游民——他们像蝗群一般扑向这座城池；不多时，每家酒馆都塞得水泄不通，满是些大嗓门、黑面孔、没规没矩的兵痞，所有的街巷广场，都被他们的大车、战马和马具辎重堵了个严实。

他们在集市正中燃起一堆篝火，火堆四周围着一群捉襟见肘的无赖汉，男女老少都有，尖叫与醉歌闹得满城喧天。据德·福赫特记载，前后总计约四千兵马，摊满了这座齐整殷实的市镇，闯进一户户人家，撬开地窖货栈，用他们的喧哗和纵酒行乐，把白昼搅得乌烟瘴气。

===Original===
As many as could found shelter in the deserted homes of the burghers; others used the stately kerks as stabling for their horses and camping ground for themselves. The inhabitants offered no resistance. A century of unspeakable tyranny ere they had gained their freedom had taught them the stern lesson of submitting to the inevitable. The Stadtholder had ordered them to submit. Until he could come to their rescue they must swallow the bitter cup of resignation to the dregs. It could not be for long. He who before now had swept the Spanish hordes off the sacred soil of the United Provinces could do so again. It was only a case for a little patience. And patience was a virtue which these grave sons of a fighting race knew how to practise to its utmost limit.

And so the burghers of Amersfoort who had remained in the city in order to watch over its fate and over their property submitted without murmur to the arrogant demands of the invaders. Their wives ministered in proud silence to the wants of the insolent rabble. They saw their dower-chests ransacked, their effects destroyed or stolen, their provisions wasted and consumed. They waited hand and foot, like serving wenches, upon their tyrants; for, indeed, it had been the proletariat who had been the first to flee.

They even succeeded in keeping back their tears when they saw their husbands﻿—the more noted burghers of the town﻿—dragged as hostages before the commander of the invading troops, who had taken up his quarters in the burgomaster’s house.

===Chinese===
能找到空子的，便在市民弃下的空宅里安身；其余的则把庄严的教堂当作马厩和露营地。居民未作丝毫抵抗。赢得自由之前那一百年间不堪言说的暴政，教会了他们一课冷峻的功课：对无可避免之事，唯有俯首。执政有令，叫他们降顺。在他赶来解救之前，他们只得把这杯屈从的苦酒，饮到最后一滴。这不会太久。这位曾经把西班牙的乌合之众从尼德兰联省共和国（the United Provinces）神圣国土上涤荡净尽的人物，尽可以再来一次。所需要的不过是一点耐心。而耐心，恰恰是这班沉毅的战斗种族的子孙，懂得如何修炼到极致的美德。

于是，留守城中照看城池命运与自己产业的阿默斯福特市民，对入侵者倨傲的索求逆来顺受，一声不吭。他们的妻子怀着孤高的沉默，伺候那帮蛮横匪兵的需索。她们眼看着嫁妆箱被翻得底朝天，家什或毁或窃，存粮被糟蹋净尽。她们像使唤丫头一般，寸步不离地侍候这些暴虐之徒；因为，说实在的，头一批逃散的，恰恰是那些穷苦小民。

她们看见自己的丈夫——城中素负重望的市民——被当作人质拖到入侵敌军统帅面前时，甚至把眼泪也忍住了；那位统帅的行辕，就安在市长的宅邸里。

===Original===
That commander was the Lord of Stoutenburg. In high favour with the Archduchess now, he had desired leave to carry through this expedition to Amersfoort. Private grudge against the man who had robbed him of Gilda, or lust for revenge against the Stadtholder for the execution of Olden Barneveldt, who can tell? Who can read the inner workings of a tortuous brain, or appraise the passions of an embittered heart?

Attended by all the sinister paraphernalia which he now affected, the Lord of Stoutenburg entered Amersfoort in the late afternoon as a conqueror, his eyes glowing with the sense of triumph over a successful rival and of power over a disdainful woman. The worthy citizens of the little town gazed with astonishment and dread upon his sable banner, broidered in silver with a skull and crossbones﻿—the emblem of his relentlessness, now that the day of reckoning had come.

===Chinese===
那统帅不是别人，正是斯托滕贝格勋爵。如今他正深得大公夫人的宠信，是他自己讨来了这趟远征阿默斯福特的差事。是对夺走吉尔达的那人的私仇，还是为奥尔登巴内费尔特（Olden Barneveldt）伏法而对执政燃起的报复欲——谁能说得清？谁能参透一颗九曲回肠的脑子里翻腾的机关，又能掂量出一颗怨毒心里种种激情的斤两？

斯托滕贝格勋爵带着他如今偏爱的全套阴森排场，于傍晚时分以征服者的姿态开进阿默斯福特，双眼灼灼放光——既有压倒得胜情敌的扬扬得意，又有辖制一个傲眼女人的权势之感。小城里那些可敬的市民，惊惧交加地望着他那面玄黑的军旗：旗上用银线绣着骷髅与交叉骨——那是他冷酷无情的徽记，如今，清算的日子到了。

===Original===
He rode through the city, hardly noticing its silent deathlike appearance. Not one glance did he bestow on the closed shutters to the right or left of him. His eyes were fixed upon the tall pinnacled roof of the burgomaster’s house, silhouetted against the western sky, the stately abode on the quay where, in the days long since gone by, he had been received as an honoured guest. Since then what a world of sorrow, of passion, of endless misery had been his lot! It seemed as if, on the day when he became false to Gilda Beresteyn in order to wed the rich and influential daughter of Marnix de St. Aldegonde, fickle fortune had finally turned her back on him. His father and brother ended their days of the scaffold; his wife, abandoned by him and brokenhearted; he himself a fugitive with a price upon his head, a potential assassin, and that vilest thing on earth, a man who sells his country to her enemies.

No wonder that, at a comparatively early age, the Lord of Stoutenburg looked a careworn and wearied man. The lines on his face were deep and harsh, his hair was turning grey at the temples. Only the fire in his deepset eyes was fierce and strong, for it was fed with the fire of an ever-enduring passion﻿—hatred. Hatred of the Stadtholder; hatred of the nameless adventurer who had thwarted him at every turn; hatred of the woman who had shut him out wholly from her heart.

===Chinese===
他策马穿城而过，几乎不曾留意这座城死一般的岑寂。左右紧闭的百叶窗，他一眼也不曾赏给。他的目光只顾钉在市长宅邸那高耸的尖顶上——它衬在西天幕前，剪出一抹黑影——那座码头边的华堂，在久已逝去的岁月里，他曾在这里被奉为上宾。从那以后，多少悲愁、多少情孽、多少无穷无尽的苦厄，都落到了他的头上！仿佛就在他背弃吉尔达·贝雷斯坦、转娶马尔尼克斯·德·圣阿尔德贡德（Marnix de St. Aldegonde）家那位有钱有势的女儿的那一天，反复无常的命运女神便终于对他掉转了背。父亲和兄弟在断头台上结束了此生；妻子被他抛弃，心碎肠断；他自己成了悬赏缉拿的逃犯，一个蓄谋的刺客，还是天底下最卑污的东西——一个把祖国出卖给她的敌人的货色。

无怪乎斯托滕贝格勋爵年纪未老，看上去已是个忧劳憔悴、身心俱疲之人。他脸上的沟纹又深又硬，两鬓已经花白。只有深陷眼窝里的那团火，依旧又猛又炽，因为喂养它的，是一腔永不熄灭的激情之火——仇恨。恨执政；恨那个处处掣肘他的无名冒险家；恨那个把他整个人关在心门外的女人。

===Original===
But now the hour of triumph had come. For it had schemed and lied and striven and never once given way to despair. It had come, crowned with immeasurable success. The Stadtholder﻿—thanks to the subtle poison of an infamous Borgia, administered by a black-hearted assassin﻿—was nothing but a physical wreck; whilst those who had brought him﻿—Stoutenburg﻿—to his knees three short months ago were at his mercy at last. A longing as cruel as it was vengeful had possession of his soul whenever he thought of these two facts.

His schemes were not yet mature, and he had not yet arrived at any definite conclusion as to how he would reach the ultimate goal of his desires; but this he did know﻿—that the Stadtholder was too sick to put up a fight for Amersfoort, and that Gilda and her stranger lover were definitely parted, and both of them entirely in his power. Their fate was as absolutely in his hands as his had once been in theirs. And the Lord of Stoutenburg, with his eyes raised to the pinnacled roof of the house that sheltered the woman whom he still loved with such passionate ardour, felt that for the first time for this many a year he might count himself as almost happy.

===Chinese===
可如今，得胜的时辰到了。为了它，他使过计、撒过谎、拼过命，从不曾有一回向绝望低头。它来了，头上戴着无可估量的成功之冠。执政——托那位臭名昭著的博尔吉亚的阴毒药末、经一个黑心刺客下手的福——如今只剩一具垮掉了的躯壳；而短短三个月前把他斯托滕贝格逼到屈膝的那几方人，终于落进了他的掌心。每当念及这两桩事实，一种又残酷、又挟怨的渴望，便攫住他的灵魂。

他的图谋尚未圆熟，也还没拿定什么确切的章程，该怎样走到欲念的终极目标；可有一样他是确知的——执政病得再无余力为阿默斯福特打一仗；吉尔达和她的异乡情郎已被生生拆散，两人都全然落在他掌中。他们的命运彻底攥在他手里，一如他自己的命运，当初攥在他们手里。斯托滕贝格勋爵抬眼仰望那座尖顶高耸的宅子——里面安置着他至今仍以如火痴情恋慕着的女人——只觉得这么多年来，头一遭可以把自己算作几乎是幸福的人了。

### III / 三

===Original===
Nicolaes Beresteyn was among the last to enter his native city. He did so as a shameless traitor, a dishonoured gambler who had staked his all upon a hellish die. Indeed now he seemed like a man possessed, careless of his crime, exulting in it even. The vague fear of meeting his father and Gilda eye to eye seemed somehow to add zest to his adventure. He did not know how much they knew, or what they guessed, but felt a strange thrill within his tortuous soul at the thought of standing up before them as their master, of defying them and deriding their reproaches.

His young wife he knew to be away. Her father had started off for Amsterdam with his family and his servants at the first rumour of the enemy’s approach. In any case she was his. She and her wealth and Mynheer van den Poele’s influence and business connections. He﻿—Nicolaes﻿—who had always been second in his father’s affections, always subservient to Gilda and to Gilda’s interests, and who since that affair in January had been treated like a skulking schoolboy in the paternal home, would now rule there as a conqueror, a protector on whose magnanimity the comfort of the entire household would depend.

===Chinese===
尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦是最后一批进入故乡城的人。他进去时，已然是个不知羞耻的叛徒，一个把全部身家押上一掷地狱骰子的名誉扫地的赌徒。的确，此刻他倒像是着了魔，把自己的罪孽满不在乎，甚至引以为快。与父亲和吉尔达当面相遇的那一层模糊的惧意，不知怎的，反倒给他的冒险平添了几分滋味。他不知道他们知道多少、又猜到多少，可一想到要以主子的身份立在他们面前，公然蔑视他们、讥笑他们的责詈，他那颗诡诈的心里便涌起一阵异样的战栗。

他知道他的年轻妻子不在城里。一听到敌军将到的风声，她的父亲便携着家眷仆役，奔阿姆斯特丹去了。横竖她是他的了。她，连同她的财产、范登普勒老爷（Mynheer van den Poele）的权势和生意门路，都是他的。他尼古拉斯——在父亲的感情里从来屈居第二，从来要仰吉尔达、仰吉尔达的利益之鼻息，自一月里那桩事以后，在父亲家中更被当作个逃学的顽童——如今要以征服者的身份在那里当家做主，做一家的庇护人，全家上下的安乐，全系于他的宽宏。

===Original===
These and other thoughts﻿—memories, self-pity, rage, too, and hatred, and imputations against fate﻿—coursed through his mind as he rode into his native city at the head of the rearguard of Stoutenburg’s troops. He drew rein outside his father’s house. Not the slightest stirring of his dormant conscience troubled him as he ran swiftly up the familiar stone steps.

With the heavy basket-hilt of his rapier he rapped vigorously against the stout oak panels of the door, demanding admittance in the name of the Archduchess Isabella, Sovereign Liege Lady of the Netherlands. At once the doors flew open, as if moved by a spring. Two elderly serving-men stood alone in the hall, silent and respectful.

===Chinese===
这些念头，连同别样的心思——记忆、自怜，还有狂怒、仇恨、对命运的怨天尤人——在他脑中川流不息；这时，他正统领着斯托滕贝格所部的后队，纵马进了故乡城。他在父亲宅邸门前勒住缰绳。快步跑上那级级熟悉的石阶时，他沉睡的良心，没有激起一丝半缕的悸动来搅扰他。

他用细剑那沉重的篮状护手，朝厚实的橡木门板咚咚急叩，要求入内，所奉的名号，是尼德兰宗主女主、大公夫人伊莎贝拉（Archduchess Isabella）。两扇门应声大开，仿佛装着机关。厅堂里只孤零零立着两个上了年纪的男仆，无声无息，毕恭毕敬。

===Original===
At the sight of their young master they both made a movement as if to run to him, deluded for the moment into hopes of salvation, relief from this awful horror of imminent invasion. But he paid no heed to them. His very look chilled them and froze the words of welcome upon their lips, as he strode quickly past them into the hall.

The shades of evening were now rapidly drawing in. Except for the two serving-men, the house appeared deserted. In perfect order, but strangely still and absolutely dark. As he looked about him, Nicolaes felt as if he were in a vault. A cold shiver ran down his spine. Curtly he bade the men bring lighted candles into the banqueting-hall.

===Chinese===
一见他们的少主人，两人都不由得挪了挪身子，仿佛要迎上前去——一时间错生了指望，以为来了救星，能把他们从兵临城下的可怕灾殃里解脱出来。可他理也不理他们。他疾步从他们身边擦过、走进厅来，光那一副眼色，就把他们冻彻了骨，把迎候的话生生冻结在唇边。

暮色此刻正急速四合。除了那两个男仆，整座宅子恍若人去楼空。样样齐整，却静得出奇，黑得彻底。尼古拉斯环顾四周，只觉得自己像置身墓穴。一股寒战顺着脊梁骨直淌下去。他没好气地吩咐仆人点起蜡烛，送到宴厅去。

===Original===
Here, too, silence and darkness reigned. In the huge monumental hearth a few dying embers were still smouldering, casting a warm glow upon the red tiles, and flicking the knobs and excrescences of the brass tools with minute crimson sparks.

Nicolaes felt his nerves tingling. He groped his way to one of the windows, and with an impatient hand tore at the casement. Stoutenburg’s troops were now swarming everywhere. The quay was alive with movement. Some of the soldiers were bivouacking against the house, had build up a fire, the ruddy glow of which, together with the flicker of resin torches, thew a weird and uncertain light into the room. Nicolaes felt his teeth chattering with cold. His hands were like fire. Could it be that he was afraid﻿—afraid that in a moment or two he would hear familiar footsteps coming down the stairs, that in a moment or two he would have to face the outraged father, come to curse his traitor son?

===Chinese===
这里也是死寂与黑暗当道。那座巨大的纪念壁炉里，几星将熄的余烬还在闷燃，往红砖地上投下一圈暖光，又用细碎的绯红火星，撩拨着黄铜炉具上鼓凸的疙瘩棱角。

尼古拉斯只觉神经阵阵发麻。他摸索着走到一扇窗前，不耐烦地伸手去扯窗扉。斯托滕贝格的队伍此刻已四处蚁聚。码头上人影攒动。有些士兵就贴着宅子扎营，生起了火，那红彤彤的光焰，加上树脂火把的摇曳，往屋里投进一片怪诞而晃忽不定的光。尼古拉斯觉得牙齿冷得格格打战，双手却烫得像火。莫非他是怕了——怕再过片刻，就要听见熟悉的脚步声从楼梯下来，怕再过片刻，就得面对那位怒不可遏的父亲，来咒骂他这个叛徒儿子？

===Original===
Bah! This was sheer cowardice! But a brief while ago he had exulted in his treachery, gloried in his callous disregard of his monstrous crime. How it seemed to him that a pair of sightless yet still mocking eyes glared at him from out the gloom. With a shudder and a quickly smothered cry of horror, Nicolaes buried his face in his hands.

The next moment the two serving-men came in, carrying lighted candles in heavy silver candelabra. These they set upon the table; and one of them, kneeling beside the hearth, plied the huge bellows, coaxing the dying embers into flame. After which they stood respectfully by, awaiting further commands. Obviously they had had their orders﻿—absolute obedience and all those outward forms of respect which they were able to accord. Nicolaes looked at them with a fierce, defying glance. He knew them both well. Greybeards in the service of his father, they had seen the young master grow up from cradle to this hour when he stood, a rebel and a skunk, on the paternal hearth.

===Chinese===
呸！这纯属懦怯！方才他还为自己的背叛扬扬得意，还为自己对滔天罪行的麻木不仁引以为荣呢。此刻，他恍惚觉得，有一双失明的眼睛——却依旧含着讥诮——正从幽暗深处瞪视着他。尼古拉斯打了个寒噤，急急咽下一声惊怖的喊叫，把脸埋进了双手。

紧接着，那两个男仆捧着沉重的银烛台进来，烛焰通明。他们把烛台搁在桌上；其中一个跪在壁炉旁，拉动那副笨大的风箱，把将熄的余烬哄成火苗。此后便恭恭敬敬侍立一旁，静候差遣。显然，他们早得了嘱咐——绝对服从，外加凡他们所能给予的种种表面敬意。尼古拉斯恶狠狠、带着挑衅地瞪视他们。这两个他都熟识。都是伺候他父亲多年的白头老家仆，眼看着少主人从襁褓一路长到这个时辰——长成个站在自家炉边的叛徒、一个可鄙的败类。

===Original===
But they did not flinch under his glance. They knew that they had been specially chosen for the unpleasant task of waiting upon the enemy commanders because their tempers had no longer the ebullience of youth, and they might be trusted to remain calm in the face of arrogance or even of savagery﻿—even in the face of Mynheer Nicolaes, the child they had loved, the youth they had admired, now a branded traitor, who had come like a thief in the night to barter his honour for a crown of shame.

===Chinese===
可在他的瞪视之下，他们眼皮也不曾眨一下。他们知道，之所以单单挑中他们来当这份伺候敌军统领的苦差，正因为他们脾性里已没了年轻人的火爆，任凭对方倨傲、甚至凶横，也保管沉得住气——哪怕面对的是尼古拉斯老爷（Mynheer Nicolaes）：那个他们疼爱过的孩子、敬重过的少年，如今一个烙了印的叛徒，趁着夜色像贼一样摸回家来，拿自己的名誉，换了一顶耻辱之冠。

### IV / 四

===Original===
A certain commotion outside on the quay proclaimed the fact that the commander of the troops, the Lord of Stoutenburg, had entered the town at the head of his bodyguard, and followed by his master of the camp and his equerries.

He, too, made straight for the burgomaster’s house, brought his horse to a halt at the foot of the stone steps. With a curt nod, Nicolaes bade the old crones to run to the front door and receive his Magnificence. In this, as in everything else, the men obeyed at once and in silence.

But already Stoutenburg, preceded by his equerries and his torchbearers, had stepped across the threshold. He knew his way well about the house. As boys, he and his brother Groeneveld had played their games in and around the intricate passages and stairs. As a young man he had sat in the deep window embrasures, holding Gilda’s hand, taking delight in terrifying her with his impetuous love, and forcing her consent to his suit by his masterful wooing. A world of memories, grave and gay, swept over him as he entered the banqueting-hall, where, but for his many misfortunes﻿—as he callously called his crimes﻿—he would one day have sat at the bridegroom’s table beside Gilda, his plighted wife.

===Chinese===
码头上一阵骚动，宣示着军队统帅斯托滕贝格勋爵已然入城——他自领卫队当先，行营总管与侍从武官们随后。

他也径直奔市长宅邸而来，在石阶脚下勒住了马。尼古拉斯草草一颔首，吩咐那两个老仆赶到正门，迎候大人阁下。这件事，同其他一切事情一样，两个老仆立刻照办，一声不吭。

可斯托滕贝格早已跨过门槛——侍从武官和执炬手为他前导。这宅子的门径，他烂熟于心。少年时，他和兄弟格勒内费尔德（Groeneveld）就在这些曲折的廊道楼梯间追逐嬉戏。成年后，他曾坐在深深的窗龛里，握着吉尔达的手，以他那狂风骤雨式的爱情吓她为乐，用强横的追求逼得她点头许婚。走进宴厅时，千般回忆——庄重的与欢快的——一齐向他心头涌来：若不是遭了那许多「不幸」——他给自己的罪行冷冷地起了这么个名字——总有一天，他本会以新郎的身份，坐在吉尔达、他的未婚妻身旁的喜筵上。

===Original===
Both he and Nicolaes felt unaccountably relieved at meeting one another here. For both of them, no doubt, the silence and gloom of this memory-haunted house would in the long run have proved unendurable.

“I did not know that I should meet you here,” Stoutenburg exclaimed, as he grasped his friend by the hand.

“I thought it would be best,” Nicolaes replied curtly.

===Chinese===
他和尼古拉斯两人在这里撞见彼此，都不由得莫名地松了口气。对他们双方来说，这座被回忆萦绕不去的宅子，那份死寂与幽暗，日子一久，保准谁也受不住。

「想不到会在这里遇上你，」斯托滕贝格握住朋友的手，朗声说道。

「我想这样最妥当，」尼古拉斯简短地答。

===Original===
But this warm greeting from the infamous arch-traitor, in the presence of the two loyal old servants, brought a hot flush to the young man’s brow. The last faint warning from his drugged conscience, mayhap. But the feeling of shame faded away as swiftly as it had come, and the next moment he was standing by, impassive and seemingly unconcerned, while the Lord of Stoutenburg gave his orders to the men.

These orders were to prepare the necessary beds for my lord and for Mynheer Nicolaes Beresteyn, also for the equerries, and proper accommodation for my lord’s bodyguard, which consisted of twenty musketeers with their captain. Moreover, to provide supper for his Magnificence and mynheer in the banqueting-hall, and for the rest of the company in some other suitable room, without delay.

===Chinese===
可是，当着两个忠心老家仆的面，受到这个恶名昭彰的头号国贼这般热络的迎接，年轻人的额角不禁腾起一阵燥热。这或许是他那被蒙了药的良心发出的最后一声微弱警告。可羞愧之感来时如潮，去时也如潮，转眼间他已袖手立在一边，不动声色、仿佛事不关己，任凭斯托滕贝格勋爵向仆人们发号施令。

这些命令是：为大人、为尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦老爷，并为众侍从武官铺设必需的床铺；为大人卫队——二十名火枪手及其队长——安排妥当的宿处；此外，即刻在宴厅为大人阁下与老爷备晚膳，其余人等，则在别的合适房间里用饭，不得延误。

===Original===
The two old crones took the orders in silence, bowed, and prepared to leave the room.

“Stay,” my lord commanded. “Where is the burgomaster?”

“In his private apartments, so please you,” one of the men replied.

“And his daughter?”

“The jongejuffrouw is with Mynheer the Burgomaster.”

“Tell them both I want them to sup here with me and Mynheer Nicolaes.”

===Chinese===
两个老仆默默领命，躬了躬身，正要退出房去。

「站住，」大人喝道。「市长在哪里？」

「回大人的话，在他自家的内室里，」其中一个仆人答道。

「他女儿呢？」

「小姐（jongejuffrouw）在市长老爷身边。」

「去告诉他们两个：我要他们在此地陪我同尼古拉斯老爷共进晚餐。」

===Original===
Again the men bowed with the same silent dignity. It was impossible to gather from their stolid, mask-like faces what their thoughts might be at this hour. When they had gone, Stoutenburg peremptorily dismissed his equerries.

“If you have anything to complain of in this house,” he said curtly, “come and report to me at once. Tomorrow we leave at dawn.”

Both the equerries gave a gasp of astonishment.

“Tomorrow?” one of them murmured, apparently quite taken aback by this order.

===Chinese===
两个仆人又是一躬，仍是那副无声的庄严。从他们那一张张呆板如面具的脸上，休想猜出这个时辰他们心里转着什么念头。他们退下之后，斯托滕贝格便断然遣散了侍从武官。

「这宅子里若有什么不称心处，」他简截地说，「立刻来向我回报。明日我们天一亮就动身。」

两名侍从武官都倒抽了一口气。

「明日？」其中一个嗫嚅道，显然被这道命令打了个措手不及。

===Original===
“At dawn,” Stoutenburg reiterated briefly.

This was enough. Neither did the equerries venture on further remarks. They had served for some time now under his Magnificence, knew his obstinacy and the irrevocableness of his decisions when once he had spoken.

“No further commands until then, my lord?” was all that the spokesman said.

===Chinese===
「天一亮，」斯托滕贝格言简意赅地重申一遍。

这就够了。两名侍从武官再不敢多言半句。他们在大人阁下麾下当差也有些时日了，深知他的固执，深知他金口一开，令出必行、无可挽回。

「在那之前就没有别的吩咐了吗，大人？」那位代言的只问了这么一句。

===Original===
“None for you,” Stoutenburg replied curtly. “But tell Jan that the moment﻿—the moment, you understand﻿—that the burgomaster enters this room, he is to be prevented from doing any mischief. If he carries a weapon, he must at once be disarmed; if he resists, there should be a length of rope handy wherewith to tie his hands behind his back. But otherwise I’ll not have him hurt. Understand?”

“Perfectly, my lord,” the equerry gave answer. “ ’Tis simple enough.”

===Chinese===
「你们没有了，」斯托滕贝格简截地答。「不过告诉扬（Jan）：市长一踏进这间屋子——一踏进，你听明白——就得拦住他，不许他闯祸。他若带着兵器，立刻缴械；他若反抗，手边要备好一截绳子，把他双手反绑起来。但除此之外，我不许任何人伤他。明白吗？」

「完全明白，大人，」那侍从武官答道。「这事再简单不过。」

### V / 五

===Original===
Now the two friends﻿—brothers in crime﻿—were alone in the vast, panelled hall.

Nicolaes had said nothing, made no movement of indignation or protest, when the other delivered his monstrous and treacherous commands against the personal liberty of the burgomaster. He had sat sullen and glowering, his head resting against his hand.

Stoutenburg looked down on him for a moment or two, his deep-set eyes full of that contempt which he felt for this weak-kneed and conscience-plagued waverer. Then he curtly advised him to leave the room.

===Chinese===
此刻，这两个朋友——这一对犯罪路上的弟兄——独自待在这间四壁镶板的宏伟大厅里。

对方发出那道践踏市长人身自由的阴毒乱命时，尼古拉斯一言未发，也没有半点义愤或抗议的表示。他阴沉着脸闷坐，怒目而视，一手支着头。

斯托滕贝格朝他俯视了片刻，深陷的双眼里盛满了轻蔑——对这个膝盖发软、良心缠身的墙头草的轻蔑。随即，他简截地劝他离开这间屋子。

===Original===
“You might not think it seemly,” he remarked with a sneer, “to be present when I take certain preventive measures against your father. These measures are necessary, else I would not take them. You would not have him spitting some of our men, or mayhap do himself or Gilda some injury, would you?”

“I was not complaining,” Nicolaes retorted dryly.

Indeed, he obeyed readily enough. Now that the time had come to meet his father, he shrank from the ordeal with horror. It would have come, of course; but, like all weak natures, Nicolaes was always on the side of procrastination. He rose without another word, and, avoiding the main door of the banqueting-hall, he went out by the back one, which gave on a narrow antechamber and thence on the service staircase.

===Chinese===
「我对你父亲采取几项防范措施的时候，你若在场，只怕有失体统，」他冷笑道。「这些措施势在必行，否则我也不会用。你总不至于愿意看见他把我们几个人穿在剑尖上，或者一个失手，伤了他自己或是吉尔达吧？」

「我并没有抱怨，」尼古拉斯干巴巴地回敬。

的确，他巴不得从命。真到了要同父亲照面的时候，他却怕得直往后缩。这一关横竖是躲不过的；可尼古拉斯同一切软弱的品性一样，向来站在能拖则拖的一边。他一言不发地起身，避开宴厅正门，从后门出去——后门通向一间狭窄的前厅，再由前厅通到仆役用的楼梯。

===Original===
“I’ll remain in the antechamber,” he said. “Call me when you wish.”

Stoutenburg shrugged his shoulders. He was glad to remain alone for awhile﻿—alone with that wealth of memories which would not be chased away. Memories of childhood, of adolescence, of youth untainted with crime; of love, before greed and ambition had caused him to betray so basely the girl who had believed in him.

“If Gilda had remained true to me,” he sighed, with almost cynical inconsequence, exacting fidelity where he had given none. “If she had stuck to me that night in Haarlem everything would have been different.”

===Chinese===
「我就在前厅里待着，」他说。「要用我时，唤一声便是。」

斯托滕贝格耸了耸肩。他乐得独自清静片刻——同那一大堆赶也赶不散的回忆独处。童年的回忆，少年的回忆，还未染上罪孽的青春的回忆；还有爱情的回忆——在他的贪欲和野心，逼得他那般卑劣地辜负那个信赖他的姑娘之前。

「倘若吉尔达当初守着我没有变心，」他叹道，要求旁人忠贞，自己却从未给过半分，前后几乎矛盾得无耻——「倘若那天夜里在哈勒姆，她肯守在我身边，一切都会是另一副光景。」

===Original===
He went up to the open window, and, leaning his arm against the mullion, he gazed upon the busy scene below. The current of cold, humid air appeared to do him good. His arquebusiers and pikemen, bivouacking round the spluttering fires, striving to keep the damp air out of their stiffening limbs; the shouts, the songs, the peremptory calls; the shrieks of frightened women and children; the loud Spanish oaths; the medley of curses in every tongue﻿—all this confused din pertaining to strife seemed to work like a tonic upon his brooding spirit. A blind beggar soliciting alms among the soldiery chased all softer thoughts away.

===Chinese===
他走到敞开的窗前，手臂倚着窗棂，俯瞰底下那一片忙碌景象。湿冷气流的涌动，似乎对他大有裨益。他的火绳枪兵和长枪兵们围着噼啪作响的营火露宿，竭力不让潮气侵入渐渐僵硬的肢体；呼喝声、歌声、急促的传令声；妇孺惊恐的尖叫；响亮的西班牙咒骂；各种语言混杂一团的诅咒——这一片属于厮杀的嘈杂，竟像一剂强心之药，提起了他那郁结的精神。倒是一个在兵丛里求告施舍的瞎眼乞丐，把一切较为柔软的念头统统赶跑了。

===Original===
“Hey, there!” he shouted fiercely, to one of the soldiers who happened just then to have caught his eye, “Have I not given orders that every blind beggar lurking around the city be hung to the nearest tree?”

The men laughed. A monstrously tyrannical order such as that suited their present mood.

“But this one was inside the city, so please your Magnificence,” one of them protested with a cynical laugh, “when we arrived.”

“All the more reason why he should be hung forthwith!” Stoutenburg riposted savagely in reply.

A loud guffaw greeted this inhuman order. His Magnificence was loudly cheered, his health drunk in deep goblets of stolen wine. Then a search was made for the blind beggar. But he, luckily for himself, had in the meanwhile taken to his heels.

===Chinese===
「喂，那边的！」他冲一个恰好在此时落入他眼中的士兵厉声喝道，「我不是下过令吗：凡在本城内外潜行的瞎眼乞丐，一律吊死在最近的树上！」

士兵们哄笑起来。这样一道暴虐绝伦的命令，正合他们此刻的心境。

「可这一个是在城里的呀，回大人阁下的话，」其中一个带着无赖气的笑申辩道，「我们开到的时候，他就在城里了。」

「那就更有理由即刻吊死他！」斯托滕贝格凶狠地反唇相讥。

这道惨无人道的命令，博得了一阵狂笑。众人高声为大人阁下喝彩，举起一盏盏抢来的美酒，深杯痛饮，祝他康健。随后便有人去搜那瞎眼乞丐。可他——算他自己走运——早已趁乱溜之大吉。

### VI / 六

===Original===
The next moment a slight noise behind him caused the Lord of Stoutenburg to turn on his heel. The door had been thrown open, and the burgomaster, having his daughter on his arm, stood upon the threshold. He was dressed in his robes of office, with black cloak and velvet bonnet; but he wore a steel gorget round his neck and rapier by his side.

At the sight of his arch enemy, he had paused under the lintel, and the ashen pallor of his cheeks became more marked. But he had no time to move, for in an instant Jan and three or four men were all around him.

===Chinese===
说时迟那时快，背后一声轻响，引得斯托滕贝格勋爵霍然旋身。门已敞开，市长手挽女儿，伫立在门槛上。他身着公务礼服，罩黑斗篷，戴丝绒小帽；颈间却围着一副钢制护喉，身侧悬着一柄细剑。

一看见那个宿敌，他在门楣下顿住了步，颊上死灰般的苍白愈发分明。可他连动都没来得及动，转眼间，扬和三四条汉子已经把他团团围住。

===Original===
At this treacherous onslaught a fierce oath escaped Beresteyn’s lips. In an instant his sword was out of its scabbard, he himself at bay, covering Gilda with his body, and facing the men who had thus scurrilously rushed on him out of the gloom.

But obviously resistance was futile. Already he was surrounded and disarmed, Gilda torn forcibly away from him, thrust into a corner, whilst he himself was rendered helpless, even though he fought and struggled magnificently. The whole unequal combat had only lasted a few seconds; and now the grand old man stood like a fettered lion, glowering and defiant, his hands tied behind his back with a length of rope, against which he was straining with all his might.

===Chinese===
遭这暗算似的突袭，贝雷斯坦唇边迸出一声狠咒。顷刻间他的剑已离鞘，本人退守死角，以身躯掩住吉尔达，面冲那几个从幽暗里下作扑来的人。

可抵抗显然徒劳。顷刻之间，他已被围住、被缴械；吉尔达被从他身边硬生生扯开，推到一个角落里；他本人也被制得动弹不得——尽管他搏斗挣扎得何等壮烈。这场众寡悬殊的格斗，统共只持续了几秒钟；如今这位气度轩昂的老人，像一头被缚的狮子，怒目而视，宁折不弯，双手被一截绳子反绑在背后，正拼尽全身力气绷着那绳子。

===Original===
One of the most disloyal pitfalls ever devised against an unsuspecting civilian﻿—and he the chief dignitary of a peace-loving city. Stoutenburg watched the scene with an evil glitter in his restless eyes. Shame and compunction did, in truth, bear no part in his emotions at this moment. He was exulting in the thought of his vile stratagem, pleased that he had thought of enticing Gilda hither by summoning her father at the same time. It was amusing to watch them both﻿—the burgomaster still dignified, despite his helplessness, and Gilda beautiful in her indignation. By St. Bavon, the girl was lovely, and still desirable. And thank Beelzebub and all the powers of darkness who lent their aid in placing so exquisite a prize in the hands of the conqueror.

---

===Chinese===
对付一个不设防的平民，这大约是天下设计得最背信弃义的陷阱了——何况这平民，还是一座和平之城的首席缙绅。斯托滕贝格望着这一幕，那双不安分的眼睛里闪着邪祟的光。羞惭与内疚，此刻在他的情感里，实在连一丝一毫也占不上。他正为自己那条毒计洋洋自得，庆幸自己想出了同时传召她父亲、把吉尔达诱到此地的高招。看着他们两个——市长虽陷于无助，仍不改其尊严；吉尔达盛怒之下，愈见其美——实在有趣。圣巴沃（St. Bavon）在上，这姑娘真可爱，至今仍旧教人动心。还得谢谢别西卜（Beelzebub）和一切鼎力相助的黑暗权势，把这样一件精美绝伦的奖品，送到了征服者的手里。

===Original===
