Stoutenburg could have laughed aloud with glee. As it was, he made an effort to appear both masterful and indifferent. He knew that he could take his time, that any scheme which he might formulate for his own advancement and the satisfaction of his every ambition was now certain of success. The future was entirely his, to plan and mould at will.

So now he deliberately turned back to the window, closed it with a hand that had not the slightest tremor in it. Then he returned to the centre of the room, sat down beside the table, and took on a cool and judicial air. All his movements were consciously slow. He looked at the burgomaster and at Gilda with ostentatious irony, remained silent for awhile as if in pleasant contemplation of their helplessness. “You are in suspense,” his silence seemed to express. “You know that your fate is in my hands. But I can afford to wait, to take mine ease. I am lord of the future, and you are little better than my slaves.”

===Chinese===
斯托滕贝格直想放声大笑，一抒狂喜。但他没有，只努力装出一副既强势又淡漠的神气。他知道尽可以从容行事，知道如今无论他筹划什么图谋——为自己的飞黄腾达、为满足自己的每一分野心——都必成无疑。未来整个儿是他的，任凭规划，任凭揉捏。

于是他故意踱回窗前，用一只分毫不颤的手关上了窗。随后他回到房间中央，在桌旁落座，摆出一副冷静威严的裁断气派。一举一动，无不着意从容。他带着张扬的讥诮，望望市长，又望望吉尔达，沉默了片刻，仿佛正惬意地鉴赏着他们的无助。他的沉默仿佛在说：「你们悬着心呢。你们知道，你们的命运攥在我手里。可我等得起，乐得先歇上一歇。我是未来的主宰，而你们，同我的奴隶相差无几。」

===Original===
“Was it not foolishness to resist, mynheer?” he said at last, in a tone of gentle mockery. “Bloodshed, eh? In truth, the role of fire-eater ill becomes your dignity and your years.”

“Spare me your insults, my lord,” Beresteyn retorted, with calm dignity. “What is your pleasure with my daughter and with me?”

“I will tell you anon,” Stoutenburg replied coolly, “when you are more composed.”

“I am ready now to hear your commands.”

“Quite submissive, eh?” the other retorted with a sneer.

===Chinese===
「何苦来要反抗呢，老爷？」他终于开口，声调里带着温存的一嘲。「见血，嗯？说真的，充好汉这个角色，实在有失您的身份与年纪。」

「少来这一套羞辱，大人，」贝雷斯坦回敬，平静而不失尊严。「你要把我的女儿和我怎样？」

「回头我自会奉告，」斯托滕贝格冷冷地答，「等您定下神来。」

「现在我就恭听你的号令。」

「够恭顺的，嗯？」对方冷笑一声回敬。

===Original===
“No; only helpless, and justly indignant at this abominable outrage.”

“Also surprised﻿—what?﻿—at seeing me here tonight?”

“In truth, my lord, I had not expected to see the son of Olden Barneveldt at the head of enemy troops.”

“Or *your* son in his train, eh?”

The burgomaster winced at the taunt. But he rejoined quite simply:

“If what rumour says is true, my lord, then I have no son.”

===Chinese===
「不；只是无能为力，并且对这桩可憎的暴行，怀着理直气壮的愤慨。」

「还捎带着吃惊——怎么样？——今夜竟在此地见到我？」

「说实话，大人，我实在不曾料到，会看见奥尔登巴内费尔特的儿子统领着敌军开进来。」

「更不曾料到*您的*儿子也厕身其间，嗯？」

市长被这一讪，浑身一缩。可他答得极其坦然：

「倘若谣言所说不虚，大人，那我就没有儿子了。」

===Original===
“If,” Stoutenburg retorted dryly, “rumour told you that Nicolaes Beresteyn hath returned to his allegiance, then the jade did not lie. Your son, mynheer, hath shown you which way loyalty lies. Not in the service of a rebel prince, but in that of Archduchess Isabella, our Sovereign Liege.”

He paused, as if expecting some word of reply from the burgomaster; but as the latter remained silent, he went on more lightly:

“But enough of this. Whether you, Mynheer Beresteyn, and your son do make up your differences presently is no concern of mine. You will see him anon, no doubt, and can then discuss your family affairs at your leisure. For the nonce, I do desire to know whether your city intends to be submissive. I have exercised great leniency up to this hour; but you must remember that I am equally ready to punish at the slightest sign of contumely or of resistance to my commands.”

===Chinese===
「倘若，」斯托滕贝格冷冷回敬，「谣言告诉您，尼古拉斯·贝雷斯坦已经重归故主，那么这长舌妇倒没有撒谎。您的儿子，老爷，已经指给您看了：忠心当归于何处——不在叛军亲王的麾下，而在大公夫人伊莎贝拉、我们的宗主女主的麾下。」

他顿了顿，仿佛等着市长回敬一句；见对方始终缄默，便又轻快地说下去：

「不过，这个话头够了。您，贝雷斯坦老爷，同令郎眼下能不能捐弃前嫌，与我无干。您转眼就会见到他，届时尽可从容商谈家务。眼下当务之急，我想知道的是：贵城是否打算恭顺。至今为止，我已施了极天的宽仁；但您必须记住，只消有丝毫轻慢、丝毫违抗我命令的征兆，我同样随时准备惩办。」

===Original===
“For the leniency to which the Lord of Stoutenburg lays claim,” Beresteyn rejoined with perfect dignity, “in that, up to this hour he has not murdered our peaceful citizens, burned down our houses, or violated our homes, we tender him our thanks. As for the future, the treacherous pitfall into which I have fallen, and the unwarrantable treatment that is meted out to me, will mayhap prove to my unfortunate fellow-citizens that resistance to overwhelming force is worse than useless.”

“Excellent sentiments, mynheer!” Stoutenburg retorted. “Dictated, I make no doubt, by one who knows the usages of war.”

“We do all of us,” the burgomaster gave quiet answer, “obey the behests of our Stadtholder, our Sovereign Liege.”

===Chinese===
「至于斯托滕贝格勋爵所自矜的那份宽仁，」贝雷斯坦答得尊严十足，「——迄今不曾屠杀我们安分的市民、不曾焚毁我们的房屋、不曾污闯我们的家门——我们谨向他致谢。至于将来，我落入的这个背信陷阱，加之加诸我身的这份无端凌辱，或许恰能向我那些不幸的同胞证明：面对压倒的强权，抵抗比束手无策更糟。」

「高论，老爷！」斯托滕贝格回敬。「毫无疑问，是深谙战阵规矩的人教的。」

「我们所有人，」市长静静地答道，「都服从我们的执政、我们的宗主君主的号令。」

===Original===
“The rebel prince, mynheer, who, by commanding you to submit, hath for once gauged rightly the temper of the Sovereign whom he hath outraged. Will you tell me, I pray you,” Stoutenburg added, with a sardonic grin, “whether the jongejuffrouw your daughter is equally prepared to obey Maurice of Nassau’s behests and submit to my commands?”

At this cruel thrust an almost imperceptible change came over the burgomaster’s calm, dignified countenance; and even this change was scarce noticeable in the uncertain, flickering light of the wax candles. Perhaps he had realized, for the first time, the full horror of his position, the full treachery of the snare which had been laid for him, and which left him, pinioned and helpless, at the mercy of an unscrupulous and cowardly enemy. Not only him, but also his daughter.

A groan like that of a wounded beast escaped his lips, and his powerful arms and shoulders strained at the cords that fettered him. Nevertheless, after a very brief moment of silence he rejoined with perfect outward calm:

===Chinese===
「那位叛军亲王，老爷，这一回勒令你们降顺，倒总算摸准了一回他被自己冒犯的那位宗主的脾气。那么可否请您相告，」斯托滕贝格讪笑着加了一句，「您家小姐、您的女儿，是否也同样准备服从拿骚的莫里斯的号令，乖乖听命于我？」

这残忍的一击，在市长那张平静威严的脸上，掠过一丝几乎无从察觉的变化；而在烛焰明灭不定之下，连这变化也难于看清。或许直到此刻，他才头一回认清自己处境的全部恐怖，认清那张为他设下的罗网何等阴毒——网住了他，捆住他的手脚，把他丢在一个肆无忌惮而又怯懦卑劣的敌人掌心里，任其摆布。而且不光是他，还有他的女儿。

一声如同受伤野兽的呻吟，从他唇间逸出；他强有力的臂膀和肩头，死命绷着缚他的绳索。然而，只沉默了短短一瞬，他便以外表十足的平静答道：

===Original===
“My daughter, my lord, was under my protection until vile treachery rendered me helpless. Now that her father can no longer watch over her, she is under the protection of every man of honour.”

“That is excellently said, mynheer,” Stoutenburg replied. “And in a few words you have put the whole situation tersely and clearly. You have orders from the Stadtholder to obey my commands; therefore I do but make matters easier for you by having you removed to your apartments, instead of merely commanding you to return thither﻿—an order which, if you were free, you might have been inclined to disobey.”

“A truce on your taunts, my lord!” broke in the burgomaster firmly. “What is your pleasure with us?”

===Chinese===
「我的女儿，大人，本在我的护佑之下，直到卑鄙的背信使我失去了护她的能力。如今她的父亲既不能再照看她，她便处在天下每一个正人君子的护佑之下。」

「这话说得再漂亮不过，老爷，」斯托滕贝格答道。「寥寥数语，您已把整个局面说得又干净又明白。执政有令，命您服从我的命令；因此我把您送回您的内室去，不过是替您省事——免得我只下一道命令，让您自己走回去；那样一道命令，您若是自由之身，说不定还存心违抗呢。」

「你的讥诮也该有个够，大人！」市长斩钉截铁地打断。「你到底要怎样发落我们？」

===Original===
“Just what I have had the honour to tell you,” Stoutenburg replied coolly. “That you return forthwith to your apartments.”

“But my daughter, my lord?”

“She sups here, with her brother Nicolaes and with me.”

“ ’Tis only my dead body you’ll drag away from here,” the burgomaster rejoined quietly.

Once more Stoutenburg broke into that harsh, mirthless laugh which had become habitual to him and which seemed to find its wellspring in the bitterness of his soul.

===Chinese===
「正是我有幸告诉过您的那句话，」斯托滕贝格冷冷地答。「您即刻回您的内室去。」

「可我女儿呢，大人？」

「她在此地用晚餐，同她哥哥尼古拉斯，还有我。」

「要弄我离开这里，除非抬走我的尸首，」市长不动声色地回敬。

斯托滕贝格又一次迸出那阵刺耳的、毫无欢趣的笑——这笑声如今已成他的积习，而它的源头，仿佛就藏在他灵魂的苦毒里。

===Original===
“Fine heroics, mynheer!” he said derisively. “But useless, I fear me, and quite unnecessary. Were I to assure you that your daughter hath ceased to rouse the slightest passion in my heart or to stir my senses in any way, you would mayhap not credit me. Yet such is the case. The jongejuffrouw, I’ll have you believe, will be as safe with me as would the ugliest old hag out of the street.”

“Nevertheless, my lord,” Beresteyn rejoined with calm dignity, “whilst I live I remain by my daughter’s side.”

Stoutenburg shrugged his shoulders.

“Jan,” he called, “take mynheer the burgomaster back to his apartments. I have no further use for him.”

===Chinese===
「好一番慷慨的豪言，老爷！」他讥诮道。「可惜无用，我怕是，而且全无必要。我若向您保证，令嫒已不能再在我心中激起最细微的情热、再不能以任何方式撩动我的官感，您多半不肯信我。然而事实如此。请您相信，小姐在我这里，会同从街上拉来的最丑的老婆子一样安全。」

「话虽如此，大人，」贝雷斯坦答得平静而尊严，「只要我一息尚存，我就守在我女儿身边。」

斯托滕贝格耸了耸肩。

「扬，」他唤道，「把市长老爷押回他的内室去。我再用不着他了。」

### VII / 七

===Original===
Mynheer Beresteyn was still a comparatively young and vigorous man. In his day, he had been counted one of the finest soldiers in the armies of the Prince of Orange, and had accomplished prodigies of skill and valour at Turnhout and Ostend. The feeling that at this moment, when he would have given his life to protect his daughter, he was absolutely helpless, was undoubtedly the most cruel blow he had ever had to endure at the hands of Fate. His eyes, pathetic in their mute appeal for forgiveness, sought those of Gilda. She had remained perfectly still all this while, silent in the dark corner whither Jan and the soldiers had thrust her at their first onslaught on the burgomaster. But she had watched the whole scene with ever-increasing horror, not at thought of herself, of her own danger, only of her father and all that he must be suffering. Now her one idea was to reassure him, to ease the burden of sorrow and of wrath which his own impotence must have laid upon his brave soul.

Before any of the men could stop her, she had evaded them. Swift and furtive as a tiny lizard, she had wormed her way between them to her father’s side. Now she had her arms round his neck, her head against his breast.

===Chinese===
贝雷斯坦老爷还算得上年富力强。当年，他曾名列奥兰治亲王麾下数一数二的战将，在蒂伦豪特和奥斯坦德建立过技艺与胆略的奇功。此时此刻，他甘愿舍出性命护住女儿，却全然无能为力——这份感受，无疑是他有生以来在命运之手底下挨过的最残酷的一击。他的眼睛，带着无言的祈恕、凄楚动人，寻向吉尔达的眼睛。而她，自始至终一动不动，默默待在那个昏暗的角落里——扬和士兵们初初扑向市长时，把她推到了那里。可她眼看着这一幕从头演到尾，恐惧愈积愈深：不是想到自己、想到自身的危险，而是想到父亲，想到他此刻必在经受的一切。如今她只有一个念头：让他安心，卸下那份悲哀与怒火的重负——自身的无能为力，必已把这重负压上了他勇毅的灵魂。

不等哪个来得及拦阻，她已从人缝里脱身。像一只小小蜥蜴般敏捷而悄没声儿，她从他们中间蜿蜒穿行，钻到父亲身边。此刻她双臂搂住他的脖颈，头贴在他胸口。

===Original===
“Do not be anxious because of me, father dear,” she whispered under her breath. “God hath us all in His keeping. Have no fear for me.”

A deep groan escaped the old man’s breast. His eyes, fierce and indignant, rested with an expression of withering contempt upon his enemy.

“Jan,” Stoutenburg broke in harshly, “didst not hear my commands?”

===Chinese===
「别为我忧心，亲爱的父亲，」她压着嗓子喃喃低语。「上帝把我们众人都握在祂的掌中。不要为我害怕。」

老人胸中逸出一声深长的呻吟。他那双喷着怒火的眼睛，带着一种令人无地自容的鄙夷，牢牢钉在仇人身上。

「扬，」斯托滕贝格厉声插进来，「没听见我的命令吗？」

===Original===
Four pairs of hands immediately closed upon the burgomaster. He, like a creature at bay, started to struggle.

“Someone knock that old fool on the head!” his lordship shouted with a fierce oath.

And Jan raised his fist, overwilling to obey. But, with a loud cry of indignation, Gilda had already interposed. She seized the man’s wrist with her own small hands and turned flaming eyes upon Stoutenburg.

===Chinese===
四双手立刻按上了市长。他像一头陷于绝地的困兽，挣扎起来。

「谁来把那老蠢货敲昏过去！」勋爵大人厉声咒骂着喝道。

扬举起了拳头，正巴不得从命。可吉尔达一声愤慨的高呼，早抢在头里拦住了。她用自己一双小手攥住那人的手腕，把一双喷火的眼睛转向斯托滕贝格。

===Original===
“Violence is unnecessary, my lord,” she said, vainly striving to speak coolly and firmly. “My father will go quietly, and I will remain here to listen to what you have to say.”

“Bravely spoken!” Stoutenburg rejoined with a sneer. “And you, Mynheer Beresteyn, would do well to learn wisdom at so fair a source. You and your precious daughter will come to no harm if you behave like reasonable beings. There is such a thing,” he added cynically, “as submitting to the inevitable.”

“Do not trust him, Gilda,” the old man cried. “False to his country, false to his wife and kindred, every word which he utters is a lie or a blasphemy.”

===Chinese===
「用不着动粗，大人，」她说，徒然竭力要说得又冷静又坚定。「我父亲会安安静静地走，我留在此地，听您有什么话要说。」

「说得好有胆气！」斯托滕贝格冷笑着回敬。「您呢，贝雷斯坦老爷，也不妨从这样一位漂亮的源头讨些教益。您同您那位千金，只要行事像个明白人，便不会吃到什么亏。天下毕竟有这么一桩事，」他冷冷地补上一句，「叫做顺从无可避免者。」

「别信他，吉尔达，」老人喊道。「对国家不忠，对妻子亲族不义，他嘴里吐出的每一个字，不是谎言，便是渎神的话。」

===Original===
“Enough of this wrangle,” Stoutenburg exclaimed, wrathful and hoarse. “Jan, take that ranting dotard away!”

Then it was that, just before the men had time to close in all round the burgomaster, Gilda, placing one small, white hand upon her father’s arm, pointed with the other to the door at the far end of the room. Instinctively the old man’s glance turned in that direction. The door was open, and Nicolaes stood upon the threshold. He had heard his father’s voice, Stoutenburg’s brutal commands, his sister’s cry of indignation.

===Chinese===
「这场聒噪够了，」斯托滕贝格怒哑着嗓子喝道。「扬，把那咆哮的老糊涂弄走！」

就在众人将要合成合围之势的当口，吉尔达把一只雪白的小手搭上父亲的手臂，另一只手，指向房间尽头的那扇门。老人的目光本能地转向那边。门敞着，尼古拉斯正立在门槛上。他听见了父亲的声音，听见了斯托滕贝格那些粗暴的命令，听见了妹妹愤慨的呼喊。

===Original===
“Nicolaes is here, father dear,” Gilda said simply. “God knows that he is naught but an abominable traitor, yet methinks that even he hath not fallen so low as to see his own sister harmed before his eyes.”

At sight of his son an indefinable look had spread over the burgomaster’s face. It seemed as if an invisible and ghostly hand had drawn a filmy grey veil all over it. And a strange hissing sound﻿—the intaking of a laboured sigh﻿—burst through his tightly set lips.

“Go!” he cried to his son, in a dull, toneless voice, which nevertheless could be heard, clear and distinct as a bell, from end to end of the vast hall. “A father’s curse is potent yet, remember!”

Nicolaes broke into a forced and defiant laugh, tried to assume a jaunty, careless air, which ill agreed with his pallid face and wild, scared eyes. But, before he could speak, Jan and the soldiers had finally seized the burgomaster and forcefully dragged him out of the room.

===Chinese===
「尼古拉斯来了，亲爱的父亲，」吉尔达简单地说。「上帝知道，他不过是个可憎的叛徒；可我想，纵然是他，也还没有堕落到眼睁睁看着自己的妹妹在自己眼前受辱的地步。」

一看见儿子，市长脸上漫过一种难以名状的神情。仿佛有一只看不见的、鬼魅的手，往他整张脸上罩了一层薄薄的灰纱。一声奇异的嘶响——一口艰难抽气的叹息——从他紧闭的齿唇间迸了出来。

「滚！」他朝儿子喊道，嗓音喑哑平板，却依然清越分明如钟声，从这间宏伟大厅的这头，一直响到那头。「父亲的诅咒，至今仍是灵验的，记住！」

尼古拉斯迸出一阵勉强而挑衅的笑，极力装出一副轻快随便的神气——这神气同他惨白的脸色、狂乱惊惶的眼睛，全不相称。可他还没来得及开口，扬和士兵们已经终于捉牢了市长，硬生生把他拖出了房间。

## X A Prince of Darkness / X 黑暗亲王

### I / 一

===Original===
Gilda had seen her father dragged away from her side without a tear. Whatever tremor of apprehension made her heart quiver after she had seen the last of him, she would not allow these two men to see.

She was not afraid. When a woman has suffered as Gilda had suffered during these past two days, there is no longer in her any room for fear. Not for physical fear, at any rate. All her thoughts, her hopes, her anxieties were concentrated on the probable fate of her beloved. That unerring instinct which comes to human beings when they are within measurable distance of some acute, unknown danger amounts at times to second sight. This was the case with Gilda. With the eyes of her soul she could see and read something of what went on in her enemy’s tortuous brain. She could see that he knew something about her beloved, and that he meant to use that knowledge for his own abominable ends. What these were she could not divine. Prescience did not go quite so far. But it had served her in this, that when her father was taken away she had just sufficient time and strength of will to brace herself up for the ordeal which was to come.

It is always remarkable when a woman, young and brought up in comparative seclusion and ignorance, is able to face moral danger with perfect calm and cool understanding. It was doubly remarkable in the case of a young girl like Gilda. She was only just twenty, had been the idol of her father; motherless, she had no counsels from those of her own sex, and there are always certain receptacles in a woman’s soul which she will never reveal to the most loving, most indulgent father.

===Chinese===
吉尔达（Gilda）眼看着父亲被人从自己身边拖走，没有落一滴眼泪。目送他走远之后，无论心头怎样因忧惧而颤栗，她也不肯让这两个男人看出来。

她并不害怕。一个女人在过去的两天里受尽了吉尔达所受的苦楚之后，她心里便再也容不下恐惧了——至少容不下对皮肉之苦的恐惧。她全部的思绪、希望与忧虑，都集中在她心上人可能的命运上。人距某种剧烈而未知的危险近在咫尺时，会生出一种不出错的直觉，这直觉有时几等于未卜先知。吉尔达正是如此。她用灵魂的眼睛，能看见、能读出几分她仇敌那副九曲回肠的脑子里正转着什么。她看得出，关于她的心上人，他知道些内情，而且他打算拿这内情去为他那可鄙的目的效力。那些目的究竟是什么，她参不透。先见之明还没有远到那一步。但这份直觉已帮了她一个忙：父亲被带走时，她恰好还有足够的时间和意志力，把自己武装起来，迎接即将到来的磨难。

一个年轻女子，自幼在相当的幽闭与蒙昧中长大，面对心灵上的险境竟能全然镇定、头脑清明——这向来已是难得；而像吉尔达这样的少女，则更是加倍难得。她年方二十，一直是父亲的掌上明珠；自幼丧母，得不到同性亲人的教导，何况女人的灵魂里总有几处秘藏的角落，是连最慈爱、最纵容的父亲也永远无缘窥见的。

===Original===
Three months ago, this same absolutely innocent, unsophisticated girl had suddenly been confronted with the vehement, turbulent passions of men. She had seen them in turmoil all round her—love, hatred, vengeance, treachery—she herself practically the pivot around which they raged. Out of the deadly strife she had emerged pure, happy in the arms of the man whom her wondrous adventures as much as his brilliant personality had taught her to love.

Since then her life had been peaceful and happy. She had allowed herself to be worshipped by that strangely captivating lover of hers, whose passionately wilful temperament, tempered by that persistent, sunny gaiety she had up to now only half understood. He made her laugh always, made her taste a strange and exquisite bliss when he held her in his arms. But withal she had up till now kept an indulgent smile in reserve for his outbursts of vehemence, for his wayward, ofttimes irascible moods, his tearing impatience when she was away from him. Her love for him in the past had been almost motherly in its tenderness.

Somehow, with his absence, with the danger which threatened him, all that had become changed, intensified. The tenderness was still in her heart for him, an exquisite tenderness which caused her sheer physical ache now, when her mind conjured up that brief vision which she had had of him yesterday morning, wearied, with shoulders bent, his face haggard above a three-day’s growth of beard, his eyes red-rimmed and sunken. But with that tenderness there was mingled at this hour a feeling which was akin to fierceness—the primeval desire of the woman to defend and protect her beloved—that same tearing impatience with Fate, of which he had been wont to suffer, for keeping him away from her sheltering arms.

Oh, she understood his vehemence now! No longer could she smile at his fretfulness. She, too, was a prey at this hour to a wildly emotional mood, tempest-tossed and spirit-stirring; her very soul crying out for him. And she hated—ay, hated with an intensity which she herself scarcely could apprise—this man whom she knew to be his deadly enemy.

===Chinese===
三个月前，正是这个绝对天真、不谙世事的姑娘，猝然直面了男人们狂暴激烈的情欲。她眼看着它们在自己四周翻腾——爱、恨、复仇、背信——而她自己，差不多正是那风暴肆虐所绕的轴心。从那场殊死的纷争中，她一身清白地走了出来，幸福地依在那个男人怀里；是他的煊赫人格、同样是她那一场场奇遇，教会了她去爱他。

自那以后，她的生活平静而幸福。她任由她那位有着奇异魔力的恋人把自己当作神明来崇拜——他那热烈而任性的性情，被一种始终不改的、阳光似的欢快中和着，这份欢快，她至今也只懂得一半。他总能逗她发笑，总能让她在他怀抱里尝到一种奇妙而精致的至福。可尽管如此，直到今日，对他的激烈爆发，对他任性多变、动辄愠怒的情绪，对她不在身边时他那难熬的焦灼，她始终还留着一点宽容的微笑。往日里，她对他的爱，那份柔情几乎带着母性。

不知怎的，随着他的离开，随着威胁着他的危险，这一切都变了，变浓变烈了。她心中对他的柔情还在——一种精致的柔情，此刻每当她脑海里浮起昨天早晨匆匆一瞥见他的模样，竟叫她肉体般地真疼起来：疲惫不堪，肩背佝偻，三天没刮的胡须衬着一张枯槁的脸，眼眶红肿，双目深陷。可这时辰，与那柔情交融在一起的，还有一种近乎凶悍的情绪——那是女人捍卫和保护心上人的原始欲望——也正是他一向饱尝的那份对命运的难熬的焦灼，怨命运把他拦在她这双庇护的臂弯之外。

啊，如今她懂得他的激烈了！再不能对他的烦躁付之一笑了。此时此刻，她自己也被一种狂放的情绪攫住了，如遭风暴颠簸，心魂激荡；她的灵魂整个在为他呼号。而她恨——是的，恨得那样烈，连她自己都难以估量——恨这个她明知是他死敌的男人。


### II / 二

===Original===
“Sit down, sister; you are overwrought.”

Nicolaes’ cool, casual words brought her straightway back to reality. Quietly, mechanically she took the seat which he was offering—a high-backed, velvet-covered chair—the one in which the Stadtholder had sat at her wedding feast. She closed her eyes, and sat for a moment or two quite still. Visions of joy and of happiness must not obtrude their softly insidious presence beside the stern demands of the moment. Stoutenburg brought a footstool, and placed it to her feet. She felt him near her, but would not look on him, and he remained for awhile on his knees close beside her, she unable to move away from him.

“How beautiful you are!” he murmured, under his breath.

Her hand was resting on the arm of her chair. She felt his lips upon it, and quickly drew it back, wiping it against her gown as if a slimy worm had left its trail upon her fingers Seeing which, he broke into a savage curse and jumped to his feet.

“I thank you for the reminder, mejuffrouw,” he said coldly.

After which he sat down once more beside the long centre table, at some little distance from her, but so that the light from the candles fell upon her dainty figure, graceful and dignified against the background of the velvet-covered chair, the while his own face remained in shadow. Nicolaes, nervous and restless, was pacing up and down the room.

===Chinese===
「坐下吧，妹妹；你太激动了。」

尼古拉斯（Nicolaes）这一句冷冷的、随口的话，立时把她拽回了现实。她静静地、机械地在他递来的座位上坐下——一把高背的丝绒圈椅，正是她婚宴上执政（Stadtholder）坐过的那一把。她闭上眼睛，一动不动地坐了片刻。欢乐与幸福的种种景象，万不可在这个严峻时刻软软地、阴险地挤进人心头来。斯托滕贝格（Stoutenburg）搬来一只脚凳，安放在她脚边。她觉出他就在近旁，却不愿看他一眼；他就这样跪在她身侧挨了半晌，而她无从躲开。

「你多美啊！」他压着嗓子喃喃道。

她的手正搭在椅子扶手上。她觉出他的嘴唇落了上来，便赶紧缩回手，在裙子上擦拭，仿佛一条黏滑的虫子在她指头上留下了爬痕。他看在眼里，顿时爆出一句狠毒的咒骂，跳起身来。

「多谢您这一提醒，小姐（mejuffrouw），」他冷冷地说。

说罢，他又在长条案桌旁坐下，离她稍有一段距离，却安排得烛光恰恰照在她娟秀的身姿上——丝绒椅的映衬下，那身影优雅而端庄，他自己的脸则隐在暗处。尼古拉斯心神不宁、坐立不安，在屋里来回踱着步子。


### III / 三

===Original===
“Allow me, mejuffrouw,” Stoutenburg began coolly after awhile, “to tender you my sincere regrets for the violence to which necessity alone compelled me to subject the burgomaster; a worthy man, for whom, believe me, I entertain naught but sincere regard.”

“I pray you, my lord,” she retorted with complete self-possession, “to spare me this mockery. Had you not determined to put an insult on me, an insult which, apparently, you dared not formulate in the presence of my father, you had not, of a certainty, subjected him to such an outrage.”

“You misunderstand my motives, mejuffrouw. There was, and is, no intention on my part to insult you. Surely, as you yourself very rightly said just now, your brother’s presence is sufficient guarantee for that.”

“I said that, in order to quieten my father’s fears. The treacherous snare which you laid for him, my lord, is proof enough of your cowardly intentions.”

“You do yourself no good, mejuffrouw,” rejoined the lord of Stoutenburg harshly, “by acrimony or defiance. I had to lure your father hither, else he would not have allowed you to come. Violence to you—though you may not believe it—would be repellent to me. But, having got you both here, I had to rid myself of him, using what violence was necessary.”

===Chinese===
「请容我，小姐，」过了半晌，斯托滕贝格冷冷地开了腔，「向您郑重致歉——我实在是迫于必要，才对市长（burgomaster）动了粗；他是位可敬的人，请相信，我对他怀有的，唯有诚挚的敬意。」

「我求您了，勋爵大人，」她全然自持地回敬道，「免了这套嘲弄吧。您若不是打定了主意要羞辱我——这羞辱，显然是您当着我父亲的面不敢明言的——您就断不会让他受这般暴行。」

「您误会了我的用意，小姐。我从前没有、如今也没有半点羞辱您的意思。方才您自己说得非常在理：令兄在场，便是十足的担保。」

「我说那话，是为了安我父亲的心。您为他设下的那个奸诈圈套，勋爵大人，已足以证明您怯懦的居心。」

「尖刻也好，违逆也好，小姐，」斯托滕贝格勋爵厉声答道，「对您都没有半点好处。我不得不把你父亲诓到此处，否则他绝不会放您前来。对您动粗——尽管您未必肯信——是令我自己都厌恶的事。可既然把你们二位都弄到了这里，我就不得不甩脱他，需要用多大的暴力就用多大。」

===Original===
“And why, I pray you, had you, as you say, to rid yourself of my father? Were you afraid of him?”

“No,” he replied; “but I am compelled to put certain matters before you for your consideration, and did not desire that you should be influenced by him.”

A quick sigh of satisfaction—or was it excitement?—escaped her breast. Fretful of all these preliminaries, which she felt were but the opening gambits of his dangerous game, she was thankful that, at last, he was coming to the point.

“Let us begin, mejuffrouw,” Stoutenburg resumed, after a moment’s deliberation, “by assuring you that the whereabouts of that gallant stranger who goes by the name of Diogenes are known to me and to your brother Nicolaes. To no one else.”

He watched her keenly while he spoke. Shading his eyes with his hand, he took in every transient line of her face, noted the pallor of her cheeks, the pathetic droop of the mouth. But he was forced to own that at that curt announcement, wherewith he had intended to startle and to hurt, not the slightest change came over her. She still sat there, cool and impassive, her head resting against the velvet cushion of the chair, the flickering light of the candle playing with the loose tendrils of her golden hair. Her eyes he could not see, for they were downcast, veiled by the delicate, blue-veined lids; but of a surety, not the slightest quiver marred the perfect stillness of her lips.

In truth, she had expected some such statement from that execrable traitor. Her intuition had not erred when it told her that, in some subtle, devilish way, he would use the absence of her beloved as a tool wherewith to gain what he had in view. Now what she realized most vividly was that she must not let him see that she was afraid. Not even let him guess if she were hurt. She must keep up a semblance of callousness before her enemy for as long as she could. With her self-control, she would lose her most efficacious weapon. Therefore, for the next minute or two, she dared not trust herself to speak, lest her voice, that one uncontrollable thing, betrayed her.

“I await your answer, mejuffrouw,” Stoutenburg resumed impatiently, after awhile.

===Chinese===
「那么请问，照您自己的说法，您为什么非得甩脱我父亲不可？您怕他么？」

「不怕，」他答道；「只是我不得不把几件事摆到您面前请您斟酌，却不愿您受他的左右。」

一声急促的、满意的叹息——抑或是兴奋？——从她胸中逸出。这一切开场白令她心焦，她感觉得到，这些不过是他那危险牌局的头几手布局；她庆幸的是，他终于要说到正题了。

「咱们这就开场吧，小姐，」斯托滕贝格沉吟片刻，接着说道，「先向您保证一件事：那位以第欧根尼（Diogenes）之名行世的豪侠异客的下落，我同令兄尼古拉斯都已知晓。旁人一概不知。」

说话时，他目光锐利地盯着她。他用手遮着眼，把她脸上每一丝一闪而过的神色都收进眼底，记下她双颊的苍白、嘴角凄然的垂落。然而他不得不承认，这句存心要惊她、伤她的简慢宣告，竟没有在她身上引起丝毫变化。她仍旧坐在那里，冷静而无动于衷，头靠在椅子的丝绒软垫上，摇曳的烛光在她金发散落的鬈丝上跳跃。她的眼睛他看不见，因为低垂着，被那薄薄的、青筋隐现的眼睑掩住；但可以断言，连最细微的一颤也没有惊扰她双唇那份完美的静止。

其实，从那个可憎的叛徒口中，她早已料到会有这样一番话。她的直觉不曾出错：它告诉她，他迟早会拿她心上人的失踪当作一件工具，用某种阴巧而狠毒的手段，去谋取他觊觎的东西。此刻她体会得最真切的，就是绝不能让他看出她害怕，甚至连她是否受伤也不能让他猜到。她必须在仇人面前把一副无动于衷的假面撑得越久越好。丢了自持，她便丢了最锋利的武器。因此，接下来的一两分钟里，她不敢冒险开口，唯恐她那唯一管束不住的东西——嗓音——出卖了她。

「我在等您回话，小姐，」过了片刻，斯托滕贝格不耐烦地又开口道。

===Original===
“You have asked me no question, my lord,” she rejoined simply. “Only stated a fact. I but wait to hear your further pleasure.”

“My pleasure, fair one,” he went on lightly, “is only to prove to you that I, as ever before, am not only your humble slave but also your sincere friend.”

“A difficult task, my lord. But let me see, without further preamble, I pray you, how you intend to set about it.”

“By trying to temper your sorrow with my heartfelt sympathy,” he murmured softly.

“My sorrow?”

“I am forced to impart sad news to you, alas!”

“My husband is dead?” The cry broke from her heart, and this time she was unable to check it. Will and pride had been easy enough at first. Oh, how easy! But not now. Not in the face of this! She would have given worlds to appear calm, incredulous. But how could she? How could she, when such a torturing vision had been conjured up before her eyes?

===Chinese===
「您并没有问我什么，勋爵大人，」她淡淡地应道，「不过是陈述了一桩事实。我只等听您下一步的高见。」

「我的心愿，美人儿，」他轻飘飘地接下去说，「只是要向您证明：我一如既往，不仅是您卑顺的奴仆，更是您真诚的朋友。」

「这可是桩难事，勋爵大人。不过请让我瞧瞧，不必再来什么开场白，您打算从何着手。」

「用我发自肺腑的同情，来抚慰您的悲伤，」他柔声低语道。

「我的悲伤？」

「唉！我不得不向您通报一个悲惨的消息。」

「我的丈夫死了？」这一声呼喊是从她心底迸出来的，这一回她再也按捺不住。起初，意志与骄傲还很容易支撑。啊，何等容易！可现在不行了。面对这个，不行了！她宁愿舍出整个世界，也要显得镇静、显得难以置信。可她怎么做得到？眼前已被召唤出这样一幅凌迟般的幻象，她怎么做得到？

===Original===
For a moment it seemed as if reason itself began to totter. She looked on the man before her, and he appeared like a ghoulish fiend, with grinning jaws and sinister eyes, the play of light behind him making his face appear black and hideous. She put her hands up to her face, closed her eyes, and, oh, Heaven, how she prayed for strength!

None indeed but an implacable enemy, a jealous suitor, could have seen such soul-agony without relenting. But Stoutenburg was one of those hard natures which found grim pleasure in wounding and torturing. His love for Gilda, intensely passionate but never tender, was nothing now but fierce desire for mastership of her and vengeance upon his successful rival. The girl’s involuntary cry of misery had been as balm to his evil soul. Now her hands dropped once more on her lap. She looked at him straight between the eyes, her own still a little wild, lit by a feverish brightness.

“You have killed him,” she said huskily. “Is that it? Answer me! You have killed him?”

He put up his hand, smiling, as if to soothe a crying child.

“Nay! On my honour!” he replied quietly. “I have not seen that gallant adventurer these three months past.”

“Well, then?”

“Ask your brother Nicolaes, fair one. He saw him but a few hours ago.”

===Chinese===
有一瞬间，连理性本身仿佛都开始摇晃了。她望着面前这个男人，他活像一个食尸鬼般的恶魔，咧着狞笑的嘴，一双阴鸷的眼，身后光影的晃动把他的脸映得又黑又狰狞。她双手掩面，闭紧双眼——哦，上天啊，她是怎样祈求力量啊！

若不是不共戴天的仇敌、妒火中烧的求婚者，谁见了这样的灵魂酷刑也会心软。可斯托滕贝格偏偏是那种硬心肠的人，以伤人、折磨人为阴森的快意。他对吉尔达的爱，激烈如火而从不温柔，如今只剩下两样东西：对她强烈的占有欲，和对情场得胜的情敌的报复欲。姑娘这一声不由自主的哀呼，在他邪恶的灵魂里不啻一剂香膏。这时她的双手重新落回膝上。她直直地正视着他的双眼，自己的目光仍有些狂乱，燃着热病似的亮光。

「你们害死了他，」她嗓音喑哑地说。「是么？回答我！你们害死了他？」

他抬起手，微笑着，仿佛在哄一个啼哭的孩子。

「不！以我的名誉起誓！」他平静地答道。「那位豪侠的冒险家，我已有整整三个月没见过他了。」

「那么呢？」

「问问你哥哥尼古拉斯吧，美人儿。几个钟头前他还见过他呢。」

===Original===
“Ay, yesterday,” she retorted. “When he tried to assassinate him. I saw the murderous hand uplifted; I saw it all I tell you! And in my heart I cursed my only brother for the vile traitor that he is. But, thank Heaven, my lord was only hurt. I believe—”

She paused, put her hand up to her throat. The glance in Stoutenburg’s eyes gave her a feeling as if she were about to choke.

“You are quite right, mejuffrouw,” he broke in drily, “in believing that the intrepid Englishman who, for reasons best known to himself, hath chosen to meddle in the affairs of this country—that he, I say, was only hurt when your brother interposed yesterday betwixt him and the Stadtholder. The two ragamuffins who usually hang around him did probably save him from further punishment at the moment. But not altogether. Nicolaes will tell you that, half an hour later, that same intrepid and meddlesome English gentleman did once more try to interfere in the affairs of our Sovereign Liege the Archduchess Isabella. This time with serious consequences to himself.”

“My brother Nicolaes,” she murmured, more quietly this time, “hath killed my husband?”

===Chinese===
「不错，是昨天，」她反驳道。「他企图行刺的时候。我亲眼看见那只杀人的手扬了起来；我告诉你，我全都看见了！我在心里咒骂我唯一的哥哥，骂他这天生的卑劣叛徒。可是，感谢上天，大人只是受了伤。我相信——」

她顿住，抬手按住喉咙。斯托滕贝格眼中的那种神色，给了她一种行将窒息的感觉。

「您想得很对，小姐，」他干巴巴地插进来道，「那位无所畏惧的英格兰人——他出于只有自己最清楚的理由，偏要插手这个国家的事务——我是说，昨天您哥哥横身挡在他与执政之间时，他确实只是受了伤。平日围着他转的那两个破衣烂衫的帮闲，当时多半救了他，使他没吃更大的亏。但没能全救下来。尼古拉斯会告诉您，半个钟头之后，还是这位胆大包天、爱管闲事的英格兰绅士，又一次插手起我们至尊的君主大公夫人（the Archduchess）伊莎贝拉的事务来。这一回，后果对他自己就严重了。」

「我哥哥尼古拉斯，」这一回她语声更为低平地喃喃道，「害死了我的丈夫？」

===Original===
“No, no!” here broke in Nicolaes at last. “The whole thing, I vow, was the result of an accident.”

“What whole thing?” she reiterated slowly. “I pray you to be more explicit. What hath happened to my husband?”

“The explosion of a pistol,” Nicolaes stammered, shamed out of his defiance at seeing his sister’s misery, yet angered with himself for this weakness. “He is not dead, I swear!”

“Maimed?” she asked.

“Blind,” Nicolaes replied, “but otherwise well. I swear it!” he protested, shutting his ears to Stoutenburg’s scornful laugh, his eyes to the other’s sardonic grin, his miserably weak nature swaying like a pendulum ’twixt his ambition, his hatred of the once brilliant soldier of fortune, and his dormant tenderness for the sweet and innocent sister to whom his treacherous hand had dealt such a devilish blow.

===Chinese===
「不，不！」尼古拉斯终于忍不住插嘴道。「我发誓，整个这件事，纯粹是场意外。」

「什么整个这件事？」她缓缓地追问道。「我求你们说得更明白些。我的丈夫到底出了什么事？」

「是手枪走了火，」尼古拉斯讷讷地说，眼见妹妹的苦楚，那股对抗的气焰被羞得无影无踪，却又为自己的软弱恼恨不已。「他没有死，我发誓！」

「落了残疾？」她问。

「瞎了，」尼古拉斯答道，「旁的都安好。我发誓！」他申辩着，把耳朵向斯托滕贝格轻蔑的笑声关上，把眼睛向对方讥诮的咧嘴闭拢；他那可怜的软弱天性，像钟摆一样在两极之间摇晃：一极是他的野心、他对那个昔日煊赫的雇佣冒险家的恨，一极是他心底对甜美无辜的妹妹那份蛰伏的柔情——正是他那只背信弃义的手，给了她这样一记恶魔般的打击。

===Original===
There was silence in the room now. Gilda had uttered no cry when that same blow fell on her like a crash. It had seemed to snap the very threads that held her to life. One sigh, and one only, came through her lips, like the dying call of a wounded bird. All feeling, all emotion, seemed suddenly to have died out of her, leaving her absolutely numb, scarcely conscious, with wide, unseeing eyes staring straight out before her, striving to visualize that splendid creature, that embodiment of gaiety, of laughter, of careless insouciance, stricken with impotence; those merry, twinkling eyes sightless. The horror of it was so appalling that it placed her for the moment beyond the power of suffering. She was not a human being now at all; she had no soul, no body, no life. Her senses had ceased to be. She neither saw nor heard nor felt. She was just a thing, a block of insentient stone into which life would presently begin to trickle slowly, bringing with it a misery such as could not be endured even by lost souls in hell.

How the time went by she did not know.

Just before this awful thing had happened she had chanced to look at the clock. It was then five minutes to eight. But all this was in the past. She no longer heard the ticking of the clock, nor her enemy’s laboured breathing, nor Nicolaes’ shuffling footsteps at the far end of the room. Fortunately, she could not see the triumph, the ominous sparkle, which glittered in Stoutenburg’s eyes. He knew well enough what she suffered, or would be suffering anon when consciousness would return. Knew and revelled in it. He was like those inquisitors, the unclean spirits that waited on Spanish tyranny, who found their delight in watching the agony of their victims on the rack; who treasured every groan, exulted over every cry, wrung by unendurable bodily pain. Only with him it was the moral agony of those whom he desired to master that caused him infinite bliss. His stygian nature attained a demoniacal satisfaction out of the mental torture which he was able to inflict.

It is an undoubted fact that even the closest scrutiny of contemporary chronicles has failed to bring to light a single redeeming feature in this man’s character, and all that the most staunch supporters of the Barneveldt family can bring forward in mitigation of Stoutenburg’s crimes is the fact that his whole soul had been warped by the judicial murder of his father and of his elder brother, by his own consequent sufferings and those of his unfortunate mother.

===Chinese===
屋里此刻一片寂静。这一记打击轰然落在吉尔达身上时，她没有喊出一声。它仿佛喀的一声，把系着她生命的那几根细线齐齐崩断了。只一声叹息，仅只一声，从她唇间逸出，像一只伤鸟垂死的鸣叫。一切感觉、一切情绪，仿佛都已骤然从她身上死去，只剩她全然麻木，神志恍惚，一双睁大的、无光的眼睛直直望向前方，竭力去具象那堂堂的人儿——那个欢快、笑声不断、无忧无虑的化身——竟遭了这样的残废；那双欢喜闪烁的眼睛，从此不见天光。这份恐怖骇人到这个地步，一时竟把她压到了受苦能力之外。她此刻全然不是一个人了；她没有灵魂，没有身体，没有生命。她的感官已经停止存在。她不看、不听、不觉。她只是一件东西，一块无知无觉的石头，须臾之后，生命才会一点一点缓缓渗回石中，随之带来一种连地狱里沉沦的灵魂也承受不住的惨痛。

时间是怎么过去的，她不知道。

这骇人的事情发生之前，她恰巧看过一眼时钟。那时是八点差五分。可这一切都已属于过去。她再也听不见时钟的滴答，听不见仇敌粗重的呼吸，听不见尼古拉斯在屋子那头蹭来蹭去的脚步声。所幸她看不见斯托滕贝格眼中闪烁的那份得意、那不祥的亮光。她受的苦，或者等知觉恢复之后将要受的苦，他清楚得很。他清楚，而且以此为乐。他像那些审讯官——那些侍奉西班牙暴政的不洁妖氛——以观看受刑者在拉肢刑架上翻滚哀号为乐事；把每一声呻吟都当作珍宝，为每一声惨叫而欢腾，那都是无法忍受的皮肉之痛绞出来的。只不过在他，令他无限极乐的，是那些他想征服的人灵魂上的痛楚。他那冥河似的本性，从自己能够施加的精神酷刑里，榨取一种恶魔般的满足。

即便以最严密的目光翻检同时代的编年史，也未能在这人的品性里发掘出一星半点可赎之处，这是确凿无疑的事实；奥尔登巴内费尔特（Olden Barneveldt）家族最坚定的拥趸，能为斯托滕贝格的罪孽开脱的，也只有这一件事：他的整个灵魂，早被他父亲与长兄那场司法谋杀扭坏了，也被他自身随之而来的苦难、和他那不幸的母亲的苦难扭坏了。

### IV / 四

===Original===
“You will, I hope, mejuffrouw, give me the credit of having tried to break this sad news to you as gently as I could.”

The words, spoken in smooth, silky tones were the first sounds that reached Gilda’s returning perceptions. What had occurred in between she had not the vaguest idea. She certainly was still sitting in the same chair, with that sinister creature facing her, and her brother Nicolaes skulking somewhere in the gloom. The fire was still cracking in the hearth, the clock still ticking with insentient monotony. A tiny fillet of air caused the candlelight to flicker, and sent a thin streak of smoke upwards in an ever-widening spiral.

That streak of smoke was the first thing that Gilda saw. It arrested her eyes, brought her back slowly to consciousness. Then came Stoutenburg’s hypocritical tirade. Her senses were returning one by one. She even glanced up at the clock. It marked three minutes before eight. Only two minutes had gone by. One hundred and twenty seconds. And they appeared longer than the most phantasmagoric conception of eternity. Two minutes! And she realized that she was alive, that she could feel, and that her beloved was sightless. Was it at all strange that, with return to pulsating life, there should arise within her that indestructible attribute of every human heart—a faint germ of hope?

===Chinese===
「我想，小姐，总该信得过我：为了把这件悲惨的消息尽量委婉地透露给您，我是用过了力气的。」

这几句用圆滑的、丝绸似的声调说出的话，是头一缕传回吉尔达知觉里来的声音。其间发生过什么，她浑然没有一点印象。她确实还坐在原先那把椅子里，那个阴森的人物仍面对着她，她哥哥尼古拉斯还在暗处什么地方缩着。炉火仍在壁炉里噼啪作响，时钟仍在无知无觉地单调滴答。一缕细细的空气吹得烛光摇曳，又送起一线轻烟，盘旋着、越旋越宽地向上飘去。

那一线轻烟是吉尔达看到的第一样东西。它牵住了她的目光，慢慢把她带回了知觉。接着便是斯托滕贝格那套伪善的长篇大论。她的感官正一个一个地回来。她甚至抬眼看了看时钟。八点差三分。只过去了两分钟。一百二十秒。而它们竟比最光怪陆离的永恒幻象还要漫长。两分钟！而她意识到自己还活着，还能感觉，而她的心上人已双目失明。随着搏动的生命一起回到她身上，她心里便升起那颗人间每颗心都磨灭不掉的东西——一线微弱的希望的胚芽——这难道有什么可怪么？

===Original===
When first the awful truth was put before her by her bitterest foe, she had not been conscious of the slightest feeling of doubt. Nicolaes’ stammering protests, his obvious desire to minimise his own share of responsibility, had all helped to confirm the revelation of a hideous crime.

“He is not dead, I swear!” and “He is not otherwise hurt!” which broke from the dastard’s quaking lips at the moment, had left no room for doubt or hope. At least, so she thought. And even now that faint ray of light in the utter blackness of her misery was too elusive to be of any comfort. But it helped her to collect herself, to look those two craven miscreants in the face. Nicolaes obviously dared not meet her glance, but Stoutenburg kept his eyes fixed upon her, and the look of triumph in them whipped up her dormant pride.

And now, when his double-tongued Pharisaism reached her ear, she swallowed her dread, bade horror be stilled. She knew that he was about to place an “either—or” before her which would demand her full understanding, and all the strength of mind and body that she could command. The fate of her beloved was about to be dangled before her, and she would be made to choose—what?

===Chinese===
当那可怕的真相由她最狠毒的仇敌初次摆到她面前时，她心里不曾有过丝毫怀疑。尼古拉斯结结巴巴的辩白，他那种显而易见想推轻自己罪责的做派，都只是在坐实那桩骇人罪行的昭示。

「他没有死，我发誓！」「他旁的没有受伤！」——这两句当时从那懦夫发颤的唇间迸出的话，曾叫怀疑与希望都无处容身。至少，她是这么想的。即便此刻，那一线微光在她苦难的彻天黑暗里也太缥缈了，成不了任何安慰。但它帮她收拢了自己，去直面那两个怯懦的恶徒。尼古拉斯分明不敢迎她的目光，斯托滕贝格却把眼睛牢牢盯着她，那眼中的得意神色，鞭起了她蛰伏的傲气。

而此刻，当他那两面三刀的法利赛式伪善传进她耳中，她咽下了恐惧，喝令恐怖噤声。她知道，他就要把一道「二者择一」摆到她面前，那将要求她全部的领悟力，和她能调动的一切心力与体力。她心上人的命运就要被拎到她眼前晃荡，而她将被迫选择——选择什么？

===Original===
“You began, my lord,” she said, with something of her former assurance—and God alone knew what it cost her to speak—“by saying that you desired to place certain matters before me for my consideration. I have not yet heard, remember, what those matters are.”

“True—true!” he rejoined, with hypocritical unction. “But I felt it my duty—my sad duty, I may say—”

“A truce on this hollow mockery!” she riposted. “I pray you, come to the point.”

“The point is, fair one, that both Nicolaes and I desire to compass your welfare,” he retorted blandly.

“This you can do best at this hour, my lord, by allowing me to return to the privacy of mine apartments.”

“So you shall, *myn engel*—so you shall,” he rejoined suavely. “You will need time to prepare for departure.”

===Chinese===
「您起头说过，勋爵大人，」她说，恢复了些许从前的从容——只有上帝知道这句话花了她多大的代价——「说您想摆几件事到我面前，供我斟酌。请您记住，那些事是什么，我至今还没有听到。」

「是——是！」他应道，带着伪善的悲悯。「可我觉得这是我的责任——不如说，我悲哀的责任——」

「别再来这套空洞的装模作样了！」她反击道。「我求您，直入正题。」

「正题就是，美人儿，尼古拉斯和我，都盼着成全你的幸福，」他温和地反驳道。

「在这个时辰，您成全我的幸福最好的法子，勋爵大人，就是容我回我自己房里的清静中去。」

「你会如愿的，我的天使（*myn engel*）——你会如愿的，」他温文尔雅地答道。「你需要时间为启程做些准备。」

===Original===
She frowned, puzzled this time.

“For departure?” she asked, a little bewildered.

“I leave this town tomorrow at the head of my troops.”

“Thank God for that!” she rejoined earnestly.

“And you, mejuffrouw,” he added curtly, “will accompany us.”

“I?” she asked, not altogether understanding, the frown more deeply marked between her brows.

“Methought I spoke clearly,” he went on, in his habitual harsh, peremptory tone. “I only came to this town in order to fetch you, *myn engel*. Tomorrow we go away together.”

===Chinese===
她皱起眉，这一回是真困惑了。

「启程？」她问，有几分茫然。

「明天，我将率领我的队伍离开这座城。」

「这可得感谢上帝！」她诚心诚意地应道。

「而你，小姐，」他直截了当地补上一句，「将与我们同行。」

「我？」她问，不很明白，眉间的蹙痕刻得更深了。

「我原以为我说得够清楚了，」他接着说，用的是他惯常那种严厉专断的口吻。「我到这座城里来，就为了接你，我的天使。明天我们一同离去。」

===Original===
“The folly of human grandeur hath clouded your brain, my lord!” she said coldly.

“In what way?” he queried, still perfectly bland and mild.

“You know well that I would sooner die than follow you.”

“I know well that most women are over-ready with heroics. But,” he added, with a shrug of the shoulders, “these tantrums usually leave me cold. You are an intelligent woman, mejuffrouw, and you have seen your valiant father resign himself to the inevitable.”

“I pray you waste no words, my lord,” she rejoined coolly. “Three months ago, when at Ryswick, your crimes found you out, and you strove to involve me in your own disgrace and ruin, I gave you mine answer—the same that I do now. My dead body you can take with you, but I, alive, will never follow you!”

“ ’Twas different then,” he retorted, with a cynical smile. “You had a fortune-hunting adventurer to hand who was determined to see that your father’s shekels did not lightly escape his grasp. Today—”

===Chinese===
「人世煊赫的迷梦迷了您的心窍，勋爵大人！」她冷冷地说。

「怎么讲？」他问，依旧十足的温和大度。

「您知道得很清楚，我宁可死，也绝不跟你走。」

「我知道得很清楚，大多数女人张口就是慷慨激昂的戏文。不过，」他耸了耸肩，补上一句，「这一类撒泼寻常动不了我的心。您是位明白人，小姐，您也亲眼见到您那位英勇的父亲如何向不可回避的命运低头了。」

「请您不必白费唇舌了，勋爵大人，」她冷静地答道。「三个月前，在赖斯韦克（Ryswick），您的罪行败露，您想把我拖进您自己的耻辱与毁灭里，我当时就给过您答复——如今还是那一句。您可以带走我的尸首，可我这个人，只要活着，就绝不跟您走！」

「那时不一样，」他反唇相讥，脸上挂着一副讥诮的笑。「那时你手边有个猎财的冒险家，打定了主意，非把你父亲的钱财攥在自己手心里不可。今天——」

===Original===
“Today,” she retorted, and rose to her feet, fronted him now, superb with indignation, “he is sightless, absent, impotent, you would say, to protect me against your villainy! You miserable, slinking cur!”

Stoutenburg’s harsh, forced laugh broke in upon her wrath.

“Ah!” he exclaimed lightly. “You little spitfire! In very truth, I like you better in that mood. Heroics do not become you, *myn schat*, and they are so unnecessary. Did you perchance imagine that it was love for you that hath influenced my decision to take you away from here?”

“I pray God, my lord, that I be not polluted by as much as a thought from you!”

===Chinese===
「今天，」她反唇相讥，霍然起身，傲然面对着他，义愤得光彩照人，「他瞎了眼，不在跟前，无能为力了，您是想这么说吧，说他护不住我不受您的毒手！你这卑劣鬼祟的癞皮狗！」

斯托滕贝格那阵刺耳的、挤出来的大笑，打断了她的怒火。

「啊！」他轻快地喊了一声。「你这小火爆性子！说实在的，你这样儿我倒更喜欢。慷慨激昂的戏文不衬你，我的宝贝（*myn schat*），况且又何必呢。你莫不是臆想，把我从这里带走的决心，是出于对你的爱？」

「我向上帝祈求，勋爵大人，别让我连你的一丝念头都沾身受污！」

===Original===
“Your prayers have been granted, fair one,” he retorted with a sneer. “ ’Tis but seldom I think of you now, save as an exquisite little termagant whom it will amuse me to tame. But this is by the way. That pleasure will lose nothing by procrastination. You know me well enough by now to realise that I am not likely to be lenient with you after your vixenish treatment of me. For the nonce, I pray you to keep a civil tongue in your head,” he added roughly. “On your conduct at this hour will depend your future comfort. Nicolaes will not always be skulking in dark corners, ready to interfere if my manner become too rough.”

“He is here now,” she said boldly, “and if there is a spark of honour left in him he will conduct me to my rooms!”

With this she turned and walked steadily across the room. Even so his harsh laugh accompanied her as far as the door. When her hand was upon the knob, he called lightly after her:

“The moment you step cross the threshold, *myn schat*, Jan will bring you back here—in his arms!”

===Chinese===
「你的祈祷已经应验了，美人儿，」他冷笑一声反驳道。「如今我也难得想起你，只当你是只娇滴滴的小悍妇，驯服你不过是我的一桩消遣。不过这是闲话。这桩乐事，拖上一拖也不会减了滋味。到现在你该很了解我了，明白你这样泼妇一般地作践我之后，我不大可能再对你手下留情。眼下，我劝你嘴里放规矩些，」他粗声补了一句。「你往后的处境好坏，全看你这个时辰的行止。尼古拉斯不会永远缩在黑角落里，等我的手段一粗就跳出来打岔。」

「他现在就在这里，」她大着胆子说，「只要他胸中还有一星荣誉的火星，他就会送我回我的房间去！」

说着她转过身，步履沉稳地穿过房间。饶是如此，他那刺耳的笑声仍一路送她到门口。她的手刚搭上门把，他在背后轻悠悠地喊道：

「你一跨出门槛，我的宝贝，扬（Jan）就会把你弄回这里来——用他那两条胳膊抱着！」

### V / 五

===Original===
Instinctively she paused, realizing that the warning had come just in time—that the next moment, in very truth, she would be in the hands of those vile traitors who were there ready to obey their master’s every command. She paused, too, in order to murmur a quick prayer for Divine guidance, seeing that human protection was denied her at this hour. What could she do? She was like a bird caught in a snare from which there seemed to be no issue. Stoutenburg’s sneering laugh rang in her ear. He was beside her now, took her hand from the knob and held it for a moment forcibly in his. His glance, charged with cruel mockery, took in every line of her pallid face.

“Heroics again, fair one!” he said, with an impish grin. “Must I assure you once more that you are perfectly safe with me? See, if you were in danger from me, would not your brother interfere? Bah! Nicolaes knows well enough that passion doth not enter into my schemes at this hour. My plans are too vast to be swayed by your frowns or your smiles. I have entered this city as a conqueror. As a conqueror I shall go out of it tomorrow, and you will come with me. I shall go hence because I choose, and for reasons which I will presently make clear to you.

“But you shall come with me. When you are with me in my camp, I may honour you as my future wife, or cast you from me as I would a beggar. That will depend on my mood, and upon your temper. Nicolaes will not be there to run counter to my will. Therefore, understand me, my pretty fire-eater, that from this hour forth you are as absolutely my property as my dogs are, my horse, or the boots which I wear. I am the master here,” he concluded with strangely sinister calm, “And my will alone is law.”

===Chinese===
她本能地顿住了，明白这声警告来得恰是时候——当真再往前一步，她就要落进那些卑劣叛徒的手心，他们正在那里候着，随时听候主子的每一道号令。她顿住，也是为了匆匆默祷一句，求上天指引，因为这个时辰，人间的护佑已与她无缘。她还能怎么样呢？她像一只落进罗网的鸟，四下里望不见一个出口。斯托滕贝格冷嘲的笑声在她耳边回响。此刻他已到她身旁，把她的手从门把上拿开，攥在自己手里握了片刻。他那含着残忍嘲弄的目光，把她苍白脸上的每一道纹路都扫了一遍。

「又是慷慨激昂的戏文，美人儿！」他说，咧开一个顽童似的鬼脸。「难道还要我再向你担保一次，说你在危险之外？你想，你若真有险，你哥哥会不出来阻拦么？呸！尼古拉斯心里清楚得很，此时此刻，情欲进不了我的盘算。我的计划太大了，大得不会为你蹙眉或者一笑所动摇。我是以征服者的身份进的这座城。明天我也将以征服者的身份离城，而你将随我同行。我离去，是因为我愿意，其中的缘由，回头我自会向你说明白。

「可你必须跟我走。等到了我的大营，我可以抬举你做我未来的妻子，也可以把你像叫花子一样一脚踢开。那要看我的兴致，也要看你的脾气。尼古拉斯不会在跟前逆我的意。所以你听明白了，我漂亮的小烈性子，从这一刻起，你就彻头彻尾是我的私产，同我的狗、我的马、我脚上这双靴子毫无两样。这里我是主人，」他以一种出奇阴森的平静收束道，「而我的意志，就是唯一的法律。」

===Original===
“A law unto yourself,” she retorted, faced him with absolute composure, neither defiant nor afraid, her nerves quiescent, her voice perfectly steady, “and mayhap unto your cringing sycophants. But above your will, my lord, is that of God; and neither death nor life are your slaves.”

“Ay! But methinks they are, *myn engel*,” he answered drily. “Yours in any case.”

“No human being, my lord, can lose the freedom to die.”

“You think not?” he sneered. “Well, we shall see.”

He let go her hand, then quietly turned and walked to the window, threw open the casement once more, then beckoned to her. Strangely stirred, she followed, moved almost mechanically by something she could not resist.

At a sign from him she looked out upon the busy scene on the quay below—the enemy soldiers in possession, their bivouac fires, their comings and goings, the unfortunate citizens running hither and thither at their bidding, fetching and carrying, hustled, pushed, beaten, ordered about with rough words or the persuasive prod of pike or musket. A scene, alas, which already as a child had been familiar to her. A peaceable town in the hands of ruthless soldiery; the women fleeing from threatened insults, children clinging to their mother’s skirts, men standing by, grim and silent, not daring to protest lest mere resentment brought horrible reprisals upon the city.

===Chinese===
「你自己的法律，」她反唇相讥，以全然的镇定面对着他，既不挑衅也不畏怯，神经安宁，嗓音平稳至极，「兴许还有你那些胁肩谄笑的奴才的法律。可是在你的意志之上，勋爵大人，还有上帝的意志；生与死，都不做你的奴仆。」

「是啊！不过依我看，它们偏偏就是，我的天使，」他干巴巴地答道。「至少你的生死是。」

「没有哪个人，勋爵大人，能被夺走向死的自由。」

「你以为不能么？」他嗤笑道。「好，走着瞧。」

他放开她的手，随即不动声色地转身走向窗边，再次推开窗扉，向她招了招手。她心头奇异地一动，跟了过去，几乎是被一种无从抗拒的力量机械地推着走。

他做了个手势，她便望向下面码头上忙碌的景象——占领着的敌军士兵，他们的露营篝火，他们的来来往往；倒霉的市民被他们呼来喝去，搬东搬西，被推搡、被驱赶、被殴打，被粗话或者长矛、火枪那「劝人服帖」的一戳支使得团团转。唉，这景象她自孩提时就已看熟了。一座安分的城池落进无情的兵痞手里：女人们躲避着步步进逼的凌辱，孩子们死死攥住母亲的裙角，男人们站在一旁，铁青着脸，一声不吭，不敢抗议，唯恐单单一腔怨恨就给全城招来可怕的报复。

===Original===
Gilda looked out for awhile in silence, her heart aching with the misery which she beheld, yet could not palliate. Then she turned coldly inquiring eyes on the prime mover of it all.

“I have seen a reign of terror such as this before, my lord,” she said. “I was at Leyden, as you well know, and I have not forgotten.”

“A reign of terror, you call it, mejuffrouw?” he retorted coolly. “Nay, you exaggerate. What is this brief occupation? Tomorrow we go, remember. Is there a single house demolished at this hour, a single citizen murdered? You are too young to recollect Malines of Ghent, the reign of Alva over these recalcitrant countries. I have been lenient so far. I have spared fire and sword. Amersfoort still stands. It will stand tomorrow, even after my soldiers have gone,” he went on speaking very slowly, “if—”

“If what, my Lord?” she asked, for he had paused.

The moment had come, then, the supreme hour when that dreaded “either—or” would be put before her. Even now he went on with that same sinister quietude which seemed like the voice of some relentless judge, sent by the King of Darkness to sway her destiny.

===Chinese===
吉尔达默默地望了半晌，眼见的惨状叫她心口作痛，她却无力减轻分毫。随后她掉转过冷冷的、质问的目光，落在这一切的罪魁祸首身上。

「这样的恐怖统治，我从前也见过，勋爵大人，」她说。「我在莱顿（Leyden）待过，您清楚得很，我没有忘。」

「恐怖统治，您管这叫恐怖统治，小姐？」他冷冷地回嘴道。「不，您言重了。不过短短一占而已。明天我们就走，您记着。此时此刻，可有一间房屋被拆？可有一个市民被杀？你太年轻，记不得马利讷（Malines）和根特（Ghent）的旧事，记不得阿尔瓦（Alva）在这些桀骜不驯的邦国头顶上的统治。我至今下手留了情。我刀下留了人、火下留了城。阿默斯福特（Amersfoort）还立着。明天它还会立着，哪怕我的士兵已经开拔，」他说得极慢，「只要——」

「只要什么，勋爵大人？」她问，因为他停住了。

那一刻终于来了，那至高的时辰：那叫人心胆俱裂的「二者择一」就要摆到她面前。直到此刻，他仍旧带着那同一种阴森的平静往下说，那平静像极了某位冷酷判官的声音——黑暗之王派来拨弄她命运的判官。

===Original===
“If,” Stoutenburg concluded drily, “you mejuffrouw, will accompany me. Oh,” he added quickly, seeing that at once she had resumed that air of defiance which irritated even whilst it amused him. “I do not mean as an unwilling slave, pinioned to my chariot-wheel or strapped into a saddle, nor yet as a picturesque corpse, with flowing hair and lilies ’twixt your lifeless hands. No, no, fair one! I offer you the safety of your native city, the immunity of your fellow-citizens, in exchange for a perfectly willing surrender of your live person into my charge.”

She looked on him for awhile, mute with horror, then murmured slowly:

“Are you a devil, that you should propose such an execrable bargain?”

He laughed and shrugged his shoulders.

“I am what you and my native land have made me,” he replied. “As to that, the Stadtholder never offered to bargain with me for my father’s life.”

===Chinese===
「只要，」斯托滕贝格干巴巴地下了结论，「你，小姐，肯随我同行。哦，」他旋即补上一句，因为他看见她脸上霎时又挂起了那副倔强之色——那神气逗他又恼他。「我不是说要你做不情愿的奴隶，捆在我的车轮上、绑在马鞍上，也不是要一具好看的尸首，长发披散、百合花夹在死人手中。不，不，美人儿！我向你出价的，是你故城的安全，是你同胞市民的保全，交换的条件是：你本人，心甘情愿、完完全全地自投到我的掌中。」

她默然带惧地望了他半晌，然后缓缓地低声道：

「你是魔鬼么，竟提出这样一笔天谴的交易？」

他笑了，耸了耸肩。

「我是被你、被我的祖国造成这副模样的，」他答道。「说到交易，当年执政可从没提出过用我父亲的性命来同我讨价还价。」

===Original===
“Who but a prince of darkness would dream of doing so?” she retorted.

“Call me that, an you wish, fair one,” he put in lightly; “and come back to the point.”

“And the point is, my Lord?”

“That I will respect this city if you come tomorrow, willing and submissive, with me.”

“That, never!” she affirmed hotly.

“In that case,” he riposted coldly, “my soldiers will have a free hand ere they quit the town, to sack it at their pleasure. Pillage, arson, will be rewarded; looting will be deemed a virtue, as will murder and outrage. Even your father—”

“Enough, my lord!” she exclaimed, with passionate indignation. “Tell me, I pray, which of the unclean spirits of Avernus did suggest this infamy to you?” Then, as he met her burning glance with another careless shrug and a mocking laugh, she turned to Nicolaes, and cried out to him, almost with entreaty: “Klaas! You at least are not a party to such hideous villainy!”

===Chinese===
「除了黑暗亲王，谁会做梦想到这么办？」她反唇相讥。

「随你怎么称呼我，美人儿，」他轻巧地插话道；「咱们回到正题上来。」

「那么正题是，勋爵大人？」

「正题就是：明天你若肯乖乖地、顺从地随我同行，我便放过这座城。」

「这个，休想！」她激烈地断言。

「既然如此，」他冷冷地反击，「我的士兵临撤出此城之前便可放开手脚，随心所欲地洗劫。抢掠、纵火，皆有赏格；劫夺将被视为美德，屠杀与暴行也一样。就连你父亲——」

「够了，勋爵大人！」她悲愤交集地喊道。「我请问，究竟是阿维尔努斯（Avernus）的哪一个妖魔，向你进的这般毒计？」而他只报以又一声满不在乎的耸肩和一阵嘲弄的笑，迎着她灼灼的目光。她便转向尼古拉斯，几乎是哀求般地朝他喊道：「克拉斯（Klaas）！总不至于你也是这等骇人罪行的同谋吧！」

===Original===
But he, sullen and shamefaced, only threw her an angry look.

“You make it very difficult for us, Gilda,” he said moodily, “by your stupid obstinacy.”

“Obstinacy?” she retorted, puzzled at the word. Then reiterated it once or twice. “Obstinacy—obstinacy? My God, hath the boy gone mad?”

“What else is it but obstinacy?” he rejoined vehemently. “You know that, despite all he says, Stoutenburg hath never ceased to love you. And now that he is master here you are lucky indeed to have him as a suitor. He means well by you, by us all, else I were not here. Think what it would mean to me, to father, to every one of us, if you were Stoutenburg’s wife. But you jeopardize my future and the welfare of us all by those foolish tantrums.”

===Chinese===
可他只是阴沉着脸、带着愧色，恼怒地瞪了她一眼。

「吉尔达，你这就叫我们很难办了，」他郁郁地说，「都是因为你这愚蠢的死心眼。」

「死心眼？」她反问，被这个词弄糊涂了。又把它念叨了一两遍。「死心眼——死心眼？我的天，这孩子疯了么？」

「不是死心眼又是什么？」他激昂地反驳道。「你知道的，不管他嘴上说什么，斯托滕贝格对你的爱从来没有断过。如今他做了这里的主人，你有他这么个求婚者，实在是天大的运气。他待你是好意，待我们全家都是好意，不然我也不会在这里。想想看，你若做了斯托滕贝格的妻子，对我、对父亲、对我们每一个人意味着什么。可你偏拿这些愚蠢的撒泼，拿我前程和我们全家的安稳去冒险。」

===Original===
She gazed on him in utter horror—on this brother whom she loved; could scarcely believe her ears that it was he—really he—who was uttering such odious words. She felt her gorge rising at this callous avowal of a wanton and insulting treachery. And he, feeling the contempt which flashed on him from her glowing eyes, avoided her glance, tried to shift his ground, to argue his point with the sophistry peculiar to a traitor, and sank more deeply every moment into the mire of dishonour.

“It is time you realized, Gilda,” he said, “that our unfortunate country must sooner or later return to her true allegiance. The Stadtholder is sick. His arbitrary temper hath alienated some of his staunchest friends. The Netherlands are the unalienable property of Spain; though two rebel princes have striven to wrest them from their rightful master, the might of Spain was sure to be felt in the end. ’Twas folly ever to imagine that this so-called Dutch Republic would ever abide; and the hour, though tardy, has struck at last when such senseless dreams must come to an end.”

===Chinese===
她全然惊骇地凝视着他——凝视着这个她深爱的哥哥；简直不敢相信自己的耳朵：吐出这般可憎字句的，竟是他——千真万确是他。这番对无耻而侮辱性的背叛之冷酷自白，叫她一阵阵反胃。而他呢，觉出她灼灼目光里直射过来的轻蔑，便避开她的视线，试图挪个立足点，用叛徒特有的诡辩来申说自己的道理，于是一分一秒地，在耻辱的泥沼里越陷越深。

「吉尔达，你是时候明白了，」他说，「我们这不幸的国家，迟早总得归回她真正的君上。执政病了。他刚愎的性子，早把他一些最坚定的朋友都推远了。尼德兰是西班牙不可分割的产业；任凭两个叛逆的亲王如何拼力想把它从合法的主人手里夺走，西班牙的伟力终究要叫人领教。竟以为这个所谓的荷兰共和国会万世长存，从来就是愚蠢；如今，虽然迟了，这班痴人梦话终于到了该收场的时候。」

===Original===
“Well spoken, friend Nicolaes!” Stoutenburg put in lustily. “In verity, our Liege Lady the Archduchess Isabella, whom may God protect, could with difficulty find a more eloquent champion.”

“Or our noble land so vile a traitor!” Gilda murmured, burning now with shame. “Thank Heaven, Nicolaes, that our poor father is not here, for the disgrace of it all would have struck him dead at your feet. Would to God,” she murmured under her breath, “that it killed me now!”

“An undutiful prayer, *myn engel*,” Stoutenburg rejoined, “seeing that its fulfilment would mean that Amersfoort and her citizens would be wiped off the face of the earth.”

This time he spoke quite quietly, without any apparent threat, only with determination, like one who knows that he is master and hath full powers to see his will obeyed. She looked at him keenly for a moment or two, wondering if she could make him flinch, if she could by word or prayer shake him in that devilish purpose which in truth must have found birth through the whisperings of uncanny fiends.

===Chinese===
「说得好，尼古拉斯朋友！」斯托滕贝格起劲地插嘴道。「说实在的，我们的君主大公夫人伊莎贝拉——愿上帝庇佑她——要想找到一位比这更雄辩的辩护士，怕也难呢。」

「也再找不到我们高贵的祖国更卑劣的叛徒了！」吉尔达喃喃道，此刻羞得满面发烧。「谢天谢地，尼古拉斯，咱们可怜的父亲不在这里，不然这场奇耻大辱当场就能把他气死在你脚边。只求上帝，」她压着嗓子喃喃道，「这耻辱现在就杀了我吧！」

「这可是个忤逆的祈祷，我的天使，」斯托滕贝格应道，「因为一旦应验，阿默斯福特和满城百姓就要从地面上被抹掉了。」

这一回他说得十分平静，不带一点显豁的威吓，只有决断，像一个深知自己是主人、且有大权确保意志得行的人。她锐利地盯了他一两眼，拿不准自己能不能叫他退缩，能不能凭言语或祈求，动摇他那个恶毒的图谋——那图谋，想必本是妖魔的耳语催生出来的。

===Original===
Gilda gazed critically at his lean, hard face with the sunken, restless eyes that spoke so eloquently of disappointed hopes and frustrated ambitions; the mouth, thin-lipped and set; the unshaven chin; the hollow temples and grizzled hair. She took in every line of his tall, gaunt figure; the shoulders already bent, the hands fidgety and claw-like; the torn doublet and shabby boots, all proclaiming the down-at-heel adventurer who has staked his all—honour, happiness, eternity—for ambition; has staked all he possessed and played a losing game.

But for pity or compunction Gilda sought in vain. The glance which after awhile was raised to hers revealed nothing but unholy triumph and a cruel, callous mockery. In truth, that glance had told her that she could expect neither justice nor mercy from him, and had spared her the humiliation of a desperate and futile appeal.

A low moan escaped her lips. She tottered slightly, and felt her knees giving way under her.

Vaguely she put out her hand, fearing that she might fall. Even so, she swayed backwards, feeling giddy and sick. But the dread of losing consciousness before this man whom she loathed and despised kept up both her courage and her endurance. She felt the panelling of the window-embrasure behind her, and leaned against it for support.

===Chinese===
吉尔达细细端详着他那张瘦硬的脸：深陷而不安生的眼睛，滔滔诉说着失望的希望与受挫的野心；薄薄的、抿紧的嘴唇；未剃的下巴；凹陷的太阳穴和斑白的头发。她又把他那高瘦憔悴的身形从头到脚看了一遍：已经佝偻的肩，烦躁不安、鹰爪似的手；撕裂的紧身衣和寒碜的靴子——处处都在宣告一个穷途落魄的冒险家：为了野心，他把一切都押了上去——荣誉、幸福、永生——押上了他的全部所有，赌的却是一场输局。

可是怜悯或良心上的悔痛，吉尔达寻找再三也是徒然。片刻之后他抬眼看她的那一瞥，露出的只有渎神的得意和残忍冷漠的嘲弄。说实在的，那一瞥已告诉她：从他那里休想得到公道，也休想得到宽宥；倒也省了她一场绝望而无谓的哀求，免去了那番屈辱。

一声低低的呜咽从她唇间逸出。她微微一个趔趄，只觉双膝在身子底下直往下软。

她恍恍惚惚地伸出手去，生怕自己摔倒。饶是如此，她还是朝后晃了晃，只觉头昏欲呕。可是，一想到要在这个她既恨又鄙的男人面前失去知觉，恐惧便撑住了她的勇气和耐力。她摸到身后窗龛的木护板，便倚上去撑住身子。


### VI / 六

===Original===
Stoutenburg had made no effort to come to her assistance, neither had Nicolaes. Probably both of them knew that she would never allow either of them to touch her. But Stoutenburg’s mocking glance had pursued her all through her valiant fight against threatening unconsciousness. Now that she leaned against the framework of the window, pale and wraithlike, only her delicate profile vaguely distinguishable in the semi-gloom, her lips parted as if to drink in the cold evening air, she looked so exquisite, so desirable, that he allowed his admiration of her to override every other thought.

===Chinese===
斯托滕贝格没有伸手来扶她一把的意思，尼古拉斯也没有。多半他们两个都知道，她决计不会让任何一个人碰她。但在她与迫近的昏厥奋勇搏斗的全程，斯托滕贝格那嘲弄的目光始终追着她。此刻她倚着窗框，苍白得像个幽灵，半明半暗里只辨认得出她那秀美的侧影，双唇微启，仿佛要啜饮傍晚的冷气——她看起来那样精致、那样惹人怜爱，他竟由着自己对她的倾慕盖过了一切旁的念头。

===Original===
“You are lovely, *myn schat*,” he said quietly. “Exquisite and worthy to be a queen. And, by Heaven,” he exclaimed with sudden passion, “you’ll yet live to bless this hour when I broke your obstinacy. Hand in hand, *myn engel*, you and I, we’ll be masters of this beautiful land. I feel that I could do great things if I had you by my side. Listen, Gilda,” he went on eagerly, thinking that because she remained silent and motionless she had given up the fight, and was at last resigned to the inevitable—“listen, my beautiful little vixen! The Archduchess will wish to reward me for this; the capture of Amersfoort is no small matter, and I have further projects in mind. In the meanwhile, De Berg hath already hinted that she might reestablish the republic under the suzerainty of Spain, and appoint me as her Stadtholder. Think, *myn Geliefde*: think what a vista of glorious, satisfied ambition lies before us both! Nay, before us all. Your father, chief pensionary; Nicolaes, general of our armies; your family raised above everyone in the land. You’ll thank me, I say; thank me on your knees for my constancy and for my unwavering loyalty to you. And even tonight, presently, when you are quite calm and at rest, you’ll pray to your God, I vow, for His blessing upon your humble and devoted slave.”

He bent the knee when he said this, still scornful even in this affectation of humility, and raised the hem of her gown to his lips. She did not look down on him, nor did she snatch her skirts out of his hand. She just stared straight out before her, and said slowly, with great deliberation:

“Tonight—presently—when I am at rest—I will pray God to kill you ere you put your monstrous threat into execution.”

===Chinese===
「你真美，我的宝贝，」他静静地说。「精美得配做一位王后。而且，上天作证，」他蓦地激情迸发地喊道，「等我磨掉了你这股死心眼，你会活着为这个时辰祝福的。手挽着手，我的天使，你和我，我们将做这片美丽国土的主人。我觉得，只要有你在我身边，我干得出轰轰烈烈的大事。听着，吉尔达，」他热切地说下去，以为她沉默不动便是认输了，终于向命运低头了——「听着，我美丽的小泼妇！大公夫人要为此犒赏我；攻取阿默斯福特不是小事一桩，我心中还有更远的筹划。德·贝格（De Berg）已经暗示过，她或可在西班牙宗主权之下重建立共和，并任命我为她的执政。想想吧，我的爱人（*myn Geliefde*）：想想展现在你我二人面前的，是怎样一幅荣耀圆满、壮志得酬的远景！不，是在我们大家面前。令尊，做首席大议长（chief pensionary）；尼古拉斯，做我们大军的统帅；你们全家，高踞全国人人之上。我告诉你，你会感谢我的；会跪下来感谢我，谢我的坚忍，谢我对你始终不渝的忠诚。就在今晚，等会儿，等你完全平静安歇下来，我敢说，你还会向你的上帝祈祷，求他赐福给你这谦卑而忠心的奴仆呢。」

他说这些话时屈下了膝，即便在这番假装的谦卑里也还是一脸轻蔑，随后把她裙裾的边缘举到唇边。她没有低头看他，也没有把裙子从他手里夺回来。她只是直直望着前方，缓缓地、一字一顿地说：

「今晚——待会儿——等我安歇下来——我要祈求上帝杀了你，趁你还没把你那骇人的威胁付诸实行。」

===Original===
With a light laugh he jumped to his feet.

“Still the shrewish little vixen, what?” he said carelessly. “Yet, see what a good dog I am. I’ll not bear resentment, and you shall have the comfort of your father’s company at the little supper party which I have prepared. Only the four of us, you and the burgomaster, and Nicolaes and I; and we can discuss the arrangements for our forthcoming wedding, which shall be magnificent, I promise you. But be sure of this, fair one,” he went on harshly, drew up his gaunt figure to its full height, “that what I’ve said I’ve said. Tomorrow at sunrise I go hence, and you come with me, able-bodied and willing, to a place which I have in mind. But this city will be the hostage for your good behaviour. My soldiers remain here under the command of one Jan, who obeys all my behests implicitly and without question, because he hates the Stadtholder as much as I do, and hath a father’s murder to avenge against that tyrant, just as I have. Jan will stay in Amersfoort until I bid him go. But at one word from me, this city will be reduced to ashes, and not one man, woman or child shall live to tell the tale of how the jongejuffrouw Gilda Beresteyn set her senseless obstinacy above the lives of thousands.

===Chinese===
他轻笑一声，跳了起来。

「还是那只凶悍的小泼妇，嗯？」他漫不经心地说。「不过，瞧我这狗脾气有多好。我不记仇，你还会有你父亲作伴的安慰——我备下了一桌小小的晚宴。就我们四个：你，市长，尼古拉斯和我；咱们正好商议商议我们即将举办的婚事，我向你担保，婚宴必定盛大。可有一样你得拿准了，美人儿，」他接着说，声音转厉，把那瘦长的身板挺到笔直，「我说出口的话，就是说定了。明天日出时分我离城，你随我同行，四肢健全、心甘情愿，去往我心里选定的地方。而这座城，就是你安分守己的人质。我的士兵留驻在此，归一个扬统辖——他对我唯命是从、从不多问，因为他恨执政不比我差，也和我一样，有一笔杀父之仇要向那暴君讨。扬会留在阿默斯福特，直到我吩咐他走。可只要我一言既出，这座城就会化为灰烬，无论男女童叟，休想留一个活口，去讲述贝雷斯坦家的吉尔达小姐（jongejuffrouw）如何把她那愚蠢的死心眼，摆在千万条人命之上。

===Original===
“Think not that I’ll relent,” he concluded, and once more turned to the open window, gazed down upon the unfortunate city which he had marked as the means to his fiendish ends. His restless eyes roamed over the busy scene; his soldiers, his—the executioners who would carry out his will! Never had he been so powerful; never had his ambition been so near its goal! It had all come together—the humiliation of the Stadtholder, his own success in this daring enterprise, Gilda entirely at his mercy! Success had crowned all his nefarious schemes at last. “Nothing will change me from my purpose,” he said, with all the harsh determination which characterized his every action—“nothing! Neither your tears nor your frowns nor your prayers. There is no one, understand me, no one who can stand between me and my resolve.”

“No one but God,” she murmured under her breath. “Oh, God, protect me now! My God, save me from this!”

Dizzy, moving like a sleepwalker, she tried to hold herself erect, tried to move from the window, and from the propinquity of that execrable miscreant.

===Chinese===
「休想我会心软，」他收束道，随即又转向敞开的窗，俯瞰着那座不幸的城——他已把它标作自己恶魔图谋的筹码。他那双安生不下来的眼睛在忙碌的景象上来回逡巡；他的士兵，他的士兵——替他行刑的刽子手！他从来不曾这样权势煊赫；他的野心从来不曾这样逼近鹄的！一切都凑齐了——执政的受辱、他这桩大胆事业的得手、吉尔达整个落在他掌心！成功终于为他全部的奸谋加冕了。「什么也改变不了我的决心，」他说，带着他一举一动里那股冷硬的决绝——「什么也不能！你的眼泪不能，你的蹙眉不能，你的祈祷也不能。没有人，你听明白，没有一个人，能横在我和我的决意之间。」

「除了上帝，」她气音般喃喃道。「哦，上帝，此刻护佑我！我的上帝，救我脱离这个！」

她头昏目眩，像梦游者似的挪动着，竭力挺直身子，竭力从窗边、从那个可憎的恶徒身边挪开。

===Original===
“Have I your permission to go now?” she murmured faintly.

“Yes,” he replied; “to your father. I’ll order Jan to release our worthy burgomaster, and you and he can pray for my demise at your leisure. Whether you confide in him or not is no concern of mine. I would have you remember that my promise to respect this city and her inhabitants only holds good if you, of your own free will, come with me tomorrow. Amersfoort shall live if you come willingly. You are the best judge whether your father would be the happier for this knowledge. Methinks it would be kinder to let him think that you come tomorrow as my willing bride. But that is for you to decide. I want him here anon to give his blessing upon our future union in the presence of your brother Nicolaes. I wish the bond to be made irrevocable as soon as may be. If you or your father break it afterwards, it will be the worse for Amersfoort. Try and believe that the alternative is one of complete indifference to me. I have everything in the world now that I could possibly wish for. My ambition is completely satisfied. To have you as my wife would only be the pandering to a caprice. And now you may go, *myn schat*,” he concluded. “The destinies of your native city are in your dainty hands.”

He watched her progress across the room with a sarcastic grin. But in his heart he was conscious of a bitter disappointment. Unheard by her, he muttered under his breath:

“If only she would care, how different everything might be!”

===Chinese===
「现在您可以容我走了么？」她微弱地喃喃道。

「可以，」他答道；「去你父亲那儿吧。我会吩咐扬放我们可敬的市长出来，你和他尽可以慢慢为我早死祈祷。你对他交不交底，与我无干。我只要你记住一件事：我放过这座城和城中居民的承诺，只有等你明天自愿随我同行，方能作数。你肯自愿来，阿默斯福特就活得成。你父亲知道这内情是不是会更安心些，你自己最会掂量。依我看，让他以为你明天是做我心甘情愿的新娘来的，反倒更仁慈些。不过这由你定夺。我马上要他到这儿来，当着你哥哥尼古拉斯的面，为我们未来的结合赐福。我要这门婚约尽早定得无可反悔。今后你或你父亲若毁了约，阿默斯福特只会更遭殃。你尽管相信，另一条路对我来说全然无关痛痒。如今这世上我想要的一切，我已应有尽有。我的野心已经完全餍足。娶你为妻，不过是迁就一时兴致罢了。现在你可以走了，我的宝贝，」他收束道。「你故城的命运，就攥在你那双纤手里。」

他咧着讥诮的嘴，看她一路穿过房间。可心底里，他却觉出一股苦涩的失望。她没有听见，他压着嗓子自语道：

「她若肯有心，一切将会何等不同啊！」

===Original===
Aloud he called to Nicolaes: “Escort your sister, man, into the presence of the burgomaster! And see that Jan and a chosen few form a guard of honour on the passage of the future Lady of Stoutenburg.”

Nicolaes hastened to obey. Gilda tried to check him with a brief. “I thank you; I would prefer to go alone!”

But already he had thrown open the door, and anon his husky voice could be heard giving orders to Jan.

Gilda, at the last, turned once more to look on her enemy. He caught her eye, bowed very low, his hand almost touching the ground ere he brought it with a sweeping flourish back to his breast, in the most approved fashion lately brought in from France.

“In half an hour supper will be served,” he said. “I await the honour of the burgomaster’s company and of your own!”

And he remained in an attitude of perfect deference whilst she passed silently out of the room.

===Chinese===
随即他扬声唤尼古拉斯：「伙计，护送你妹妹到市长跟前去！再叫扬带上几个挑出来的人，为未来的斯托滕贝格夫人一路排开仪仗。」

尼古拉斯赶紧从命。吉尔达想用一句话把他拦下。「多谢你了；我情愿一个人走！」

可他已一把推开了门，不多时，只听他沙哑的嗓音在向扬发号施令。

临出去时，吉尔达又回头看了她的仇敌一眼。他接住她的目光，深深一躬，垂下去的那只手几乎触到地面，随即带着一道潇洒的大弧收回胸前——用的正是新近从法兰西时兴过来的最得体的礼数。

「半小时后开晚饭，」他说。「我恭候市长先生大驾光临，也恭候您的！」

他保持着十足的恭敬姿态，直到她默然走出房去。

## XI The Danger-Spoke / XI 祸辐

### I / 一

===Original===
Gilda had refused her brother’s escort, preferring to follow Jan; and Nicolaes, half indifferent, half ashamed, watched her progress up the stairs, and when she had disappeared in the gloom of the corridor above, he went back to his friend.

The two old serving-men were now busy in the banqueting-hall, bringing in the supper. They set the table with silver and crystal goblets, with jugs of Spanish and Rhenish wines, and dishes of cooked meats. They came and went about their business expeditiously and silently, brought in two more heavy candelabra with a dozen or more lighted candles in their sconces, so that the vast room was brilliantly lit. They threw fresh logs upon the fire, so that the whole place looked cosy and inviting.

Stoutenburg had once more taken up his stand beside the open window. Leaning his arm against the mullion, he rested his head upon it. Bitterness and rage had brought hot tears to his eyes. Somehow it seemed to him as if in the overflowing cup of his triumph something had turned to gall. Gilda eluded him. He could not understand her. The experience which he had of women had taught him that these beautiful and shallow creatures, soulless for the most part and heartless, were easily to be cajoled with soft words and bribed with wealth and promises. Yet he had dangled before Gilda’s eyes such a vision of glory and exalted position as should have captured, quite unconditionally, the citadel of her affections, and she had remained indifferent to it all.

===Chinese===
吉尔达（Gilda）谢绝了兄长相送，情愿跟着扬（Jan）去；尼古拉斯（Nicolaes）半是漠然、半是惭愧，目送她上楼；待她的身影消失在楼上走廊的幽暗里，他便回到他那位朋友身边去了。

两个年老的仆人此刻正在宴会厅里张罗，把晚饭一样样端进来。他们在桌上摆好银器和水晶高脚杯，摆好一坛坛西班牙酒和莱茵酒，还有一盘盘熟肉。他们来来去去，办事利落而无息无声，又搬进来两座沉重的多头烛台，插座里点着十几支蜡烛，把偌大的厅堂照得通明。他们往火里添了新柴，叫整个地方看上去暖意融融，引人入座。

斯托滕贝格（Stoutenburg）重新在敞开的窗边站定。他把一条手臂倚在窗棂上，头也枕在上面。辛酸与狂怒逼出了他眼里的热泪。不知怎的，他只觉得，在他那盈盈欲溢的凯旋杯里，有什么东西化成了胆汁。吉尔达避着他。他弄不懂她。他对女人的经验教导过他：这些美丽而浅薄的造物，多半无魂无情，用甜言蜜语哄一哄、用财货许诺买一买，便可轻易到手。可他偏偏在吉尔达眼前晃出了那样一幅荣耀与显赫的幻景，本该不折不扣地攻下她芳心的堡垒，她却对这一切无动于衷。

===Original===
He had owned himself still in love with her, and she had remained quite callous to his ardour. He had tried indifference, and had only been paid back in his own coin. To a man of Stoutenburg’s intensely egotistical temperament, there could only be one explanation to this seeming coldness. The wench’s senses—it could be nothing more—were still under the thrall of that miserable adventurer who, thank Beelzebub and his horde, had at last been rendered powerless to wreak further mischief. There could be, he argued to himself, no aversion in her heart for one who was so ready to share prosperity, power, and honour with her, to forgive and forget all that was past, to raise her from comparative obscurity to the most exalted state that had ever dazzled a woman’s fancy and stormed the inmost recesses of her soul.

She was still infatuated with the varlet, and that was all. A wholly ununderstandable fact. Stoutenburg never could imagine how she had ever looked with favour on such an adventurer, whose English parentage and reputed wealth were, to say the least, problematical. Beresteyn had been a fool to allow his only daughter to bestow her beauty and her riches on a stranger, about whom in truth he knew less than nothing. The girl, bewitched by the rascallion, had cajoled her father and obtained his consent. Now she was still under the spell of a handsome presence, a resonant voice, a provoking eye. It was, it could be, nothing more than that. When once she understood what she had gained, how utterly inglorious that once brilliant soldier of fortune had become, she would descend from her high attitude of disdain and kiss the hand which she now spurned.

But, in anticipation of that happy hour, the Lord of Stoutenburg felt moody and discontented.

===Chinese===
他承认自己依然爱着她，她却对他的炽热全然冷漠。他试过冷淡，结果只落得让人以其人之道还治其身。在斯托滕贝格这等极端自我中心的性情看来，这表面的冷若冰霜只有一个解释：那丫头——这无非是情热作祟了——仍旧被那个可鄙的冒险家迷住了心窍；而感谢别西卜（Beelzebub）和他那一窝妖魔，此人总算已被弄得再无力兴风作浪。他心里盘算，她的心断无嫌恶他的道理——这样一个人，甘愿与她共享富贵、权势和荣光，甘愿捐弃前嫌、尽忘旧恶，甘愿把她从湮没无闻里提拔起来，抬上那从未有过的、足以炫惑女人遐想、直捣她灵魂最深处的至高之位。

她仍旧迷恋着那个下贱坯，仅此而已。一桩全然不可理解的事。斯托滕贝格怎么也想不通，她当初怎会青眼相加于这样一名冒险家——此人的英格兰出身和号称的家财，往轻里说，都成问题。贝雷斯坦真是个傻瓜，竟容许独生女儿把美貌和钱财都交付给一个外人，一个他实际上连一知半解都谈不上的人。姑娘叫那无赖坯子迷了心窍，哄得父亲点了头。如今她仍旧着了魔——为着一副堂堂的仪表、一副洪亮的嗓音、一双撩人的眼睛。事情是、也只能是如此而已。一旦她明白了自己赢得了什么，明白了那个一度光彩照人的雇佣冒险家堕成了何等不体面的光景，她自会从高高的睥睨之上走下来，亲吻这只如今为她所唾弃的手。

可是，一想到那快乐时辰还要等待，斯托滕贝格勋爵便觉着心绪恶劣、百般不遂。

### II / 二

===Original===
Nicolaes’ voice, close to his elbow, roused him from his gloomy meditations.

“You must be indulgent, my friend,” he was saying in a smooth conciliatory voice. “Gilda had always a wilful temper.”

“And a tenacious one,” Stoutenburg retorted. “She is still in love with that rogue.”

“Bah!” the other rejoined, with a note of spite in his tone. “It is mere infatuation! A woman’s whimsey for a good-looking face and a pair of broad shoulders! She should have seen the scrubby rascal as I last caught sight of him—grimy, unshaven, broken. No woman’s fancy would survive such a spectacle!”

===Chinese===
尼古拉斯的嗓音就在他肘边响起，把他从阴沉的冥想里惊醒。

「你得宽宏些，我的朋友，」他正用一种圆滑劝解的口吻说着。「吉尔达向来一副任性脾气。」

「还固执得很呢，」斯托滕贝格反唇相讥。「她仍旧爱着那个恶棍。」

「呸！」对方应道，话音里带着一股毒气。「那不过是鬼迷心窍！女人家见了张漂亮脸蛋、两扇宽肩膀犯的一阵痴劲儿！她真该瞧瞧我末一眼见他那副寒碜样——浑身泥污，胡子拉碴，整个人全垮了。那样的惨相，哪个女人的痴情熬得过！」

===Original===
Then, as Stoutenburg, still unconsoled, continued to stare through the open window, muttering disjointed phrases through obstinately set lips, he went on quite gaily:

“You are not the first by any means, my friend, whose tempestuous wooing hath brought a woman, loving and repentant, to heel. When I was over in England with my father, half a dozen years ago, we saw there a play upon the stage. It had been writ by some lowborn mountebank, one William Shakespeare. The name of the play was *The Taming of the Shrew*. Therein, too, a woman of choleric temper did during several scenes defy the man who wooed her. In the end he conquered; she became his wife, and as tender and submissive an one as e’er you’d wish to see. But, by St. Bavon, how she stormed at first! How she professed to hate him! I was forcibly reminded of that play when I saw Gilda defying you awhile ago; and I could have wished that you had displayed the same good-humour over the wrangle as did the gallant Petruchio—the hero of the piece.”

Stoutenburg was interested.

===Chinese===
见斯托滕贝格仍不见开怀，兀自透过敞开的窗子向外凝望，从固执抿紧的唇间迸出几句没头没尾的话，他便兴致颇高地接着说：

「无论如何，我的朋友，你绝不是头一个用暴风骤雨式的求爱把一个又爱又悔的女人驯服到脚跟前的。六年前我随父亲在英格兰时，我们在那儿看过一出舞台戏。戏是一个出身低贱的江湖骗子写的，叫威廉·莎士比亚（William Shakespeare）。戏名是《驯悍记》（*The Taming of the Shrew*）。戏里也是一个烈性子的女人，好几场戏里都跟向她求婚的男人对着干。到末了他胜了；她做了他的妻子，温存顺从得你要多称心有多称心。可是，凭圣巴沃（St. Bavon）起誓，她起先闹得多凶啊！她嘴里说得多么恨他！方才我见吉尔达跟你犟，便不由得想起那出戏；只可惜你在这一场口角里，没能拿出戏里那位主角——英勇的彼特鲁乔（Petruchio）——那份好脾气来。」

斯托滕贝格来了兴致。

===Original===
“How did he succeed in the end?” he queried. “Your Petruchio, I mean.”

“He starved the ranting virago into submission,” Nicolaes replied, with an easy laugh. “Gave her nothing to eat for a day and a night; swore at her lackeys; beat her waiting-maids. She was disdainful at first, then terrified. Finally, she admired him, because he had mastered her.”

“A good moral, friend Nicolaes!”

“Ay! One you would do well to follow. Women reserve their disdain for weaklings, and their love for their masters.”

“And think you that Gilda—”

“Gilda, my friend, is but a woman after all. Have no fear, she’ll be your willing slave in a week.”

===Chinese===
「他末了是怎么得手的？」他问。「我是说你那位彼特鲁乔。」

「他把那撒泼的母老虎饿服了，」尼古拉斯轻快地笑着答道。「一天一夜不给她东西吃；骂她的跟班；打她的贴身侍女。她起先睨着他，后来怕了。到最后，她反倒佩服起他来，因为他降伏了她。」

「好一则训条，尼古拉斯朋友！」

「是啊！你照着办准没错。女人的睥睨是留给软骨头男人的，爱情是留给主子的。」

「那你觉得吉尔达——」

「吉尔达嘛，我的朋友，到底不过是个女人。放一百个心，不出一个礼拜，她就甘心做你的奴隶了。」

===Original===
Stoutenburg’s eyes glittered at the thought.

“A week is a long time to wait,” he murmured. “I wish that now—”

He paused. Something that was happening down below on the quay had attracted his attention—unusual merriment, loud laughter, the strains of a bibulous song. For a minute or two his keen eyes searched the gloom for the cause of all this hilarity. He leaned far out the window, called peremptorily to a group of soldiers who were squatting around their bivouac fire.

“Hey!” he shouted. “Peter! Willem!—whatever your confounded names may be! What is that rascallion doing over there?”

===Chinese===
想到这里，斯托滕贝格双眼放光。

「一个礼拜，等起来可够长的，」他喃喃道。「但愿现在就——」

他顿住了。码头那边正在发生的事引去了他的注意——异乎寻常的喧闹、放肆的大笑、一支醉醺醺的歌子的调门。有一两分钟，他锐利的眼睛在幽暗里搜寻这阵欢闹的因由。他把身子远远探出窗外，专断地朝一群围坐在篝火边的士兵吆喝。

「喂！」他喊道。「彼得（Peter）！威廉（Willem）！——管你们这些该死的家伙叫什么名字呢！那边那个无赖坯子在干什么？」

===Original===
“Making us all laugh, so please your lordship,” one of the soldiers gave reply; “by the drollest stories and quips any of us have ever heard.”

“Where does he come from?”

“From nowhere, apparently,” the man averred. “He just fell among us. The man is blind, so please you,” he added after a moment’s hesitation.

Stoutenburg swore.

“How many times must I give orders,” he demanded roughly, “that every blind beggar who comes prowling round the camps be hanged to the nearest post?”

“We did intend to hang him,” the soldier replied coolly; “but when first he came along he was so nimble that, ere we could capture him, he gave us the slip.”

===Chinese===
「逗我们大伙儿乐呢，但凭大人吩咐，」一个士兵答话，「讲的那些笑话俏皮话，是我们谁都没听过的。」

「他是打哪儿来的？」

「看样子哪儿也不是，」那人一口咬定。「他就是冷不丁掉进我们堆里的。那人是瞎子，请您明鉴。」迟疑了片刻，他又补上一句。

斯托滕贝格骂了一句。

「我下过多少回命令，」他粗声质问，「凡是瞎子乞丐到营盘周围乱转的，一律吊死在最近的柱子上？」

「我们原是要吊死他的，」士兵不紧不慢地答道，「可他刚来那阵儿脚底下太滑溜，我们还没逮住，就叫他溜了。」

===Original===
“Well,” Stoutenburg rejoined harshly, “it is not too late. You have him now.”

“So we have, Magnificence,” the man replied, hesitated for a second or two, then added: “But he is so amusing, and he seems a gentleman of quality, too proud for the hangman’s rope.”

“Too proud is he?” his lordship retorted with a sneer. “A gentleman of quality, and amusing to boot? Well, let us see how his humour will accommodate itself to the gallows. Here, let me have a look at the loon.”

===Chinese===
「哼，」斯托滕贝格冷硬地应道，「现在也不迟。你们这不是抓住他了么。」

「抓住了，大人阁下（Magnificence），」那人应道，迟疑了一两秒，又添上：「可他实在太逗乐了，瞧着还是位有身份的绅士，那股傲气，套上绞索都嫌糟蹋。」

「太傲气，是么？」大人冷笑着反诘。「有身份的绅士，还外加逗乐？好啊，且看他的风趣到了绞架跟前还使得上使不上。来，把这活宝带上来让我瞧瞧。」

===Original===
There was much hustling down below after this; shouting and prolonged laughter; a confused din, through which it was impossible to distinguish individual sounds. Stoutenburg’s nerves were tingling. He was quite sure by now that he had recognised that irrepressible merry voice. A gentleman of quality! Blind! Amusing! But, if Nicolaes’ report of yesterday’s events were true, the man was hopelessly stricken. And what could induce him to put his head in the jackal’s mouth, to affront his triumphing enemy, when he himself was so utterly helpless and abject?

Not long was the Lord of Stoutenburg left in suspense. Even whilst he gazed down upon the merry, excited throng, he was able to distinguish in the midst of them all a pair of broad shoulders that could only belong to one man. The soldiers, laughing, thoroughly enjoying the frolic, were jostling him not a little for the sheer pleasure of measuring their valour against so hefty a fellow. And he, despite his blindness, gave as good as he got; fought valiantly with fist and boot and gave his tormentors many a hard knock, until, with a loud shout of glee, some of the men succeeded in seizing hold of him, and hoisted him up on their shoulders and brought him into the circle of light formed by the resin torches.

A double cry came in response—one of amazement from Stoutenburg and one of horror from Nicolaes. But neither of them spoke. Stoutenburg’s lips were tightly set; a puzzled frown appeared between his brows. In truth, for once in the course of his devilish career, he was completely taken aback and uncertain what to do. The man whom he saw there before him, in ragged clothes, unshaved and grimy, blinking with sightless eyes, was the man whom he detested above every other thing or creature on earth—the reckless soldier of fortune of the past, for awhile the proud and successful rival; now just a wreck of humanity, broken, ay, and degraded, and henceforth an object of pity rather than a menace to his rival’s plans. His doublet was in rags, his plumed hat battered, his toes shone through the holes in his boots. The upper part of his face was swathed in a soiled linen bandage. This had, no doubt, been originally intended to shield the stricken eyes; but it had slipped, and those same eyes, with their horrible fixed look, glittered with unearthly weirdness in the flickering light.

===Chinese===
